The order in which stiff joints give is the solver's, so the worker does not build it; the question is with the solver owner.
7. **The 8110 is checked once, on `preview`, before `main` is offered.** A worker whose change touches the 8110's reading does not run the slow check itself; the orchestrator's run before a merge to `main` covers it.
8. **A steering wheel turns on its stud or half pin.** The steering wheel 30663 (and 16091, built on it) has a stud tube at its centre that was read as holding a stud fast, so the 42101's wheel was one body with its column and could not be turned. One correction clears that hold and leaves the round hole. 10 sets read differently, each with a steering wheel now a part of its own; no function steers with one. The 42101's function 5 and the 8281's function 4 now work, none is worse.
9. **Three of the worker's pieces are in: 612 functions work where 607 did.** The part data of decision 2's steps (3 better: the 952's function 5, the 42108's functions 6 and 7), only friction pins let turn (0 better, 0 worse) and the steering wheel (2 better), each 0 worse. The 42094's function 3 works with its ring in the middle, so it is no question for Erik. Of the 8 pushed parts locked from the start: 4 are held at two places in the model file and are questions for Erik (the 42053's function 8, the 8081's function 5, the 8285's function 15, the 8063's function 11), 2 wait for springs (the 8865's functions 5 and 6), 1 was the steering wheel, and 1 (the 8865's function 10) waits for pins on demand to run for a pushed part with the frame held, which the worker builds. The 42066's nose wheel cannot steer because its twin tyres on one axle may not slip along their travel; that rule is the solver owner's and the question is with it.
10. **A set file opens by address on the local server.** `?file=` with a path the browser does not keep is fetched beside the page, so a report can link any OMR set (`?file=omr/8081-1+Extreme+Cruiser.mpd`) and not only a packed one. It works only where the files are served, which is `npm run dev`.
11. **The check lets friction pins turn for a pushed part that no grab can move: 614 functions work.** A hinged part held by friction (a seat back on hinge plates) has a joint that turns only by swinging the rest of the model round it, so no point of it moves, the push could not start, and pins on demand, which ran only for a control with no motion at all, was never tried. It now runs there too. It can act only where no grab point is found, so the gate was the 46 functions whose push stops before it starts, the two sets that changed and the quick check: the 8865's function 10 (the seat backs) and the 42077's function 7 (the rear body tilts up) now work, none is worse.
12. **The 42099's front axle hangs on ball joints alone; the planetary hub's part data is next.** With the rack's slide kept, the 42099's functions 2 and 3 end 311 LDU out. The steering linkage is rightly rigid; each front upright with its hub 46490c01 is joined only by two towballs, and four parts beside the uprights read as loose, so the axle wanders as the car rolls. The worker looks at the hub's connection data first, on a branch of its own. The differential's gear data (batch 3) stays held: two of its three worse functions wait for the solver owner's answer on whether a motor gives last, and the 42109's function 3 has a friction pin in the same hole as an axle's end, which may be a fault of the file.
13. **The planetary hub is split and its reduction read, together with the constant-velocity joint's data.** The joint (52731 in 52730) and the hub 46490c01 had no connection data where they meet, and neither has a shadow file. The hub is one LDraw part, read as one rigid body, so no drive passes it: 9 functions in the 42099, 42129 and 42131 wait on that. With the joint's data alone the 42099's front axle is held (its functions 2 and 3 end 97 LDU out, 311 before), but the 42131's four drive functions jam, since each motor's shaft then ends in a ball on its own axis and the solver holds such a body from spinning. Of three ways on, the solver's rule is not bent for a state the split removes, and the hold is not given up: the hub is read as its three pieces (bearing, driving shaft, wheel hub) with a new coupling of turning between two coaxial bodies at a ratio. The ratio comes from the teeth counted in the LDraw shapes, not from a review; a ratio of 1 is every coupling there was. The 42109's function 3 has a second question for Erik: a friction pin and an axle fill the same hole in its file.
14. **The 243 functions that are not driven or held fixed, sorted, and the order they are worked in.** On a7f681d: 149 not-driven, 60 part-fixed, 34 control-fixed (the worker's sorting-2026-11-14.md has every function). One worker takes them in this order, each group a branch of its own with its own gate: after the hub, the constant-velocity and universal-joint shortcuts that do not bend (10, part data); two mends of the check (10: pins on demand for a listed part, and several controls each moving its own part); gear meshes not found (16); gearbox steps (25: those the notes give are written, the rest go to Erik as one list); connections read rigid that turn, or turning that hold (20); controls fixed by an axle through them or with no joint to the frame (26); and the function-list groups (13). Later: linkages (13), strings (7), chains (3), sliding shifts (4), the pneumatic valve's studs (7), and the 42 that each need a trace, by family. Shock absorbers (14) and rolling (3) are the solver owner's; 5 file faults go to Erik.
15. **The hub and the joint are in: 619 functions work where 614 did.** The hub's reduction is 5.4: its LDraw shapes carry no teeth, so the figure is from a count on X-ray pictures of the part (sun 10, ring 44, ring fixed, carrier out: 1 + 44/10; Eurobricks, "Planetary hub internals") and from Rebrickable's page for 46490, not from Erik, who may count on the real part. The 42099's front and four-wheel drive and the 42131's three track drive functions now work; the 42099's two differentials and the 42129's function 3 go from not working to partly. Five functions end further out of place after there and back, the 42099's function 6 by 500 LDU, since the drive now reaches the wheels and the model rolls: the solver owner's, with the scrub mend. The split added two parts for each hub, so the 42099, 42129 and 42131 were renumbered under decision 7 of 2026-11-13; six references to a whole hub now name its bearing body, the part that steers.
16. **A part read as several pieces keeps its number, and the further pieces are numbered after the model's last part.** The complete universal joint 3712c01 is read as one rigid part in 13 sets, its two shafts fixed together, and is to be read as its cross and two halves. A split that puts its pieces in place moves every later number in the model, and with it the references, the tests and the viewer's addresses of 13 sets and 9 packed models, and so again for each later part. So a split no longer moves a number. The worker measures whether the three parts already split (3403c01, 3680c01, 46490c01) are brought to the same rule or left, and takes the cheaper.
17. **Gear meshes not found: the kinds one rule covers are built, the single cases wait.** Of 16: a pinion a little outside a crown gear's tooth band or a little too deep (3), turntable teeth with a 12 tooth double bevel (4), knob wheels side by side on parallel axles (2) and a spur pair exactly at the tolerance (1) are built on one branch; every new mesh the looser tests make in any set is listed and judged. A worm on a crown, two worms and three tilted gears (6) are each a look of their own, later.
18. **The check passes only what the viewer shows: 623 functions work.** The check now lets the friction pins turn that hold a listed part fast, but only where the control does not go (it jams, or a push has no grab point), which is where a user's drag of the control asks for pins; the viewer's own question for a drag gives the same pins in the three functions that pass this way (the 8418's function 2, the 42032's functions 2 and 3). Where the control turns its reach and its part stays fast, the function fails as before, with the note that the part is held by friction pins the control does not reach: the 42082's function 9, the 42050's function 4, the 42077's function 5 and the 42109's function 1. A control that turns freely while its part is held means the drive between them gives somewhere; the four are one family for a trace. Two function lists follow their own words: the 42043's function 6 and the 42111's function 4 now work.
19. **The universal joint's split waits for two mends and a trace.** Numbering as decision 16, for parts split from now on only, since bringing the 16 parts already split to the rule would renumber 14 sets and a train. A joint is not rigid because it lies straight in the file; it is rigid where both its ends turn on one line in the same rigid group, and there it is read as a rigid connection (the 956's rear axle, which has no differential). A shaft that hung on two joints alone drifted 5 LDU, because a coupling's position rows were never closed after a step; they now get their gap as a loop's joint does, which changes how every model with a joint is solved, so its gate is every set with a coupling, with the solver owner's three points (the sign, ends built apart, new stops). The 956's front drive, two joints back to back, turns its engine a tenth of what it should: traced before anything is built round it.
20. **The solver owner's mends of the check pass their full check, and its next work is ordered.** On a7f681d: 626 functions work against 614, 12 better and 0 worse by result, three further out of place (the 42037's function 2, the 8002's function 6, the 8259's function 1, noted for its own list); a piston whose rod passes square to its cylinder's line is now expected at the right place (the 856). Its branch is merged with preview and waits for one more full check, after the slow 8110 check. Then, in order: a motor's output is kept still before a friction pin that was let turn, so the pin gives first (the 42109's function 2); the 42124's steering, which stops at a dead point, is traced; twin tyres on one body and one axle line, closer than about a tyre's width, give one contact at their middle (the 42066's function 5). A general rule that lets a tyre slip along its travel is not built unless the trace calls for it, since it could let a driven wheel spin in place.
21. **The gear meshes are in: 627 functions work, and a held part is to be held.** Decision 17's mends found 14 new meshes in 12 sets and lost none: 9 pairs of knob wheels side by side at 40 LDU, 4 pinions on crown gears and the 8850's spur pair at exactly the tolerance; the four turntable functions were not turntable faults and go back to the traces. Six functions go from partly to working (the 42110's function 5, the 8825's 1, the 8844's 2, the 8850's 4, the 8854's 2, the 9394's 2). Two go from working to partly, the 8464's functions 4 and 5, each a differential "with the drive held": the crown mesh that is now found is real, and with the drive meshed a turned wheel turns the drive. Held as the text says, the wheel cannot turn at all, so the way through the differential is not in the reading, and the two passed only because nothing was meshed. 19 functions' texts hold a part (a drive, the motors, a wheel) and the check holds none; 6 of them count as working. The check gets a field for it, the viewer's own freeze, written for all 19 from their own words, on a branch of its own; those that then fail are accepted as the honest result and sorted by cause. Further out of place and accepted: the 42076's function 2 (8.4 LDU), and four of the 8850's functions by 1.6 degrees, its 8 tooth on a mesh 2 LDU short.
22. **The 8110's slow check at 982f697 passes, and `main` is offered at that commit with the 8110's result.** 7 of its functions work, as before; function 5 now comes back and function 14 comes back in 3 more ways; function 7 (the winch through the yellow and red levers) goes from partly to not working. Before, its motor turned free of the winch; with the driving ring extension's clutch data the drive jams once both levers are set. The function's note says the levers' directions are inferred, so this is decision 2's fault again, a gearbox function that had not been tested, and it is accepted; the worker finds from the model which way each lever is up and what jams. The check took 18,074 s of work, about 2 hours 40 minutes on this Mac beside other checks. `main` is offered at ca95bf0, which is 982f697 with the 8110's result; the check mends of decision 18 and the gear meshes of decision 21 came after it and wait for the next slow check. Erik moved `main` to ca95bf0 on 2026-11-14.
23. **Held parts are in, and the count falls to 626: a held part is frozen, a lifted model is to be lifted, and a frozen part is held against what its shaft turns in.** With the hold of decision 21 written for 18 functions, two newly work and four that counted as working stop (the 42043's function 11, the 42125's 3, the 8081's 3, the 8063's 3), with six more from partly to not working. Nine of the ten stop because the model stands on its tyres and would have to pivot on the spot. A grip rule that allowed such a pivot would give every driving model a new motion; instead a model can be worked lifted off the ground (no tyre contacts, the frame still), by a toggle in the viewer and the address and a `lifted` field in a function, both stated and not implied by `held`, so the check's state is one a user can set. On the way the solver owner found that freezing held a body on its own joint in the solver's tree, whose parent is not always what a user means (a differential's housing on a half shaft): a frozen body that turns on a shaft is now held against what that shaft turns in, which is not always the frame either, since a frozen wheel on a steered hub must still steer. Five of the held functions had such a parent, so their results wait for that mend. Both are on the solver owner's branch claude/solver-lifted and merge after its main branch. The 8110's winch (function 7) works with both catches pushed the same way, the one of four settings that drives it and locks nothing: 8 of the 8110's functions work.
24. **The function lists are audited, not rebuilt.** Erik asked whether lists "inferred from pictures" should be rebuilt from better sources. They were not made from pictures: agent sessions wrote them on 2026-10-24 from LEGO's booklets, and each function states its source. 800 cite the booklet alone, 276 the booklet plus one inferred detail, 148 are wholly inferred, and 121 say unsure or should be checked. Erik's four corrections of this day (the 8081's doors and the 8063's trailer beams, fixed; the 8258's outriggers, pulled out by hand; the 8285's lockers, still open) were all in the marked part. So the doubt is concentrated and labelled, and what failed was that the labels were never worked through. The audit: for each set an independent second reading of the booklet's functions pages before the list is opened, a comparison, and every function marked inferred or unsure settled from the functions page, then the build steps, then a video summary as a lead only. A session is proposed to Erik for 28 sets first. Erik's sources are the functions pages at the end of a set's last booklet and Google's summary of review videos; the second gave him and the orchestrator opposite readings for the 8285, so it is a lead and not an authority.
25. **What makes a model move is per part or general; only the answer key is per set.** Erik asked whether we are at the limit of what the data and algorithms can work out, and how a user's own model can work without a per-set database. The viewer reads no function list: a model file, the part data and the rules and solver are all it uses, and a user works his own model by hand. The lists are the check's answer key for the official sets. Of the 17 questions put to Erik, 9 were faults of the answer key and 8 were faults of model files that Kinebrick had found itself (two parts in one hole, a worm that turns nothing), which for a user's model is a warning to give, not data to keep. Offered and not yet decided: Kinebrick writing a model's function list itself (every part a user can work and what it moves), as a panel for any model and as a comparison with the booklet for the official sets.
26. **Wheels that hang on nothing get their mounts, and a loose part cannot make a function pass.** 61 tyres read as loose; in 58 the rim is loose too. Four rim types lack their mount in the part data (the 32020's axle bush, the 44772's three pegholes, the 30285, and the 6580, an LDraw "moved to" stub read as nothing: the reading is to follow any such stub to its target); seven sit on an axle that is itself attached to nothing, which gets a look for each set and is then grouped by cause. Until now a loose wheel rode along with its nearest part, so "rolls freely" passed by accident in about a dozen functions: a function whose control or listed part is in a loose group is to count as partly at most, with the cause loose-part.
27. **A link can point at a part, and symbols are off by default (Erik's request).** `show=` outlines parts in magenta whether they move or not and starts the camera close; `autospin=N,paused` chooses a control without starting it; the symbols on frozen and loose parts show only with `option=symbols`. Every question about a set that goes to Erik now carries such a link.
28. **A CV ball slides in its hub's bore.** Held at a point, the 42000's half shaft locked its rear suspension; joined for drive only, the shaft would no longer follow its hub. The coupling at such a socket holds its two ends together across the bore's axis and not along it, switched by a mark in the part data, with the solver owner's three checks (the axis from the socket's pose at each step, no drift along the shaft's own line, how far the ball travels against the bore's length).
29. **Kinebrick writes a model's function list itself, as a report and never as the answer key (agreed with Erik).** For any model: every part a user can work (a knob, a lever, a wheel, a part that slides or is pushed), what moves with it, at what ratio and how far, and the parts that cannot move at all. Erik's concern was that LDraw models have errors, so a generated list stands on an unknown foundation, and he asked whether to build it only for vetted models. It is built for all models, since on an unvetted model it is how the errors show (a knob that turns a worm and nothing else, a door with no motion), but what it is trusted for is limited: the check's answer key is never taken from it, only from the booklet; an official set's panel in the viewer shows the audited list, and the generated one only where no audited list exists, marked as such; a user's own model shows it as what the file can do as built, with its warnings beside it. A set is vetted when its function list is audited against the booklet and every difference between the generated list and the booklet is mended or in the errata; the status page can then show that for each set. Scheduled after the solver owner's branch is merged, for the phase 3 worker.
30. **A part that a model file leaves out may be added as a correction (Erik's yes to question 17).** A rubber band or a motor missing from an OMR file is an erratum like a part out of place: listed in docs/ldraw-errata.md with where the booklet shows it and the correction Kinebrick uses, and signed off by Erik. The OMR's files are not ours to edit, so a corrections file for a set, applied when its model is loaded in the viewer and the check alike, is to be added; the errata "corrected in Kinebrick" so far are all in models kept in `models/`. The audit confirms each case from the booklet first (the 8094's and the 8844's rubber bands, the 41999's servo, the 8838's axle).
31. **The solver's tree starts where a group is held, not at its lowest part number.** The 8258's outriggers, pushed by hand (Erik's answer), spun about a hinge: the tree is built from turning joints only, a group joined to the chassis by a slide alone becomes a tree of its own, and its root was the lowest-numbered body, so the pushed beam's own joint was the hinge to its other beam. The viewer's drag had the same fault, and the 8258 alone has 14 such roots among 229 bodies. A new tree is to start at the body that slides on (or hangs by a ball from) a body already placed, and the check is to work such a control by a push along its slide, as the viewer does. On the solver owner's branches after the lifted model and before the motor tiers, with a full check that lists every function whose control now works a different joint.
32. **Erik took back two answers, and a set's function is settled from the booklet and a video together.** His first answers for the 8258 (outriggers pulled out by hand) and the 8081 (doors fixed) were wrong: the 8258's Power Functions leaflet shows the outriggers motor-driven through a lever and the red handle, and a review video shows the 8081's doors swinging up about their lower front corner with no pin pulled. Review videos, found through Google's summary, are a named source beside the booklet: the summary points and the video proves. An answer about a set, from anyone, is checked against the functions page. The audit of the first 28 sets is docs/audits/function-lists-batch-1.md; its table waits for Erik's sign-off, and the other 158 sets for his word. In the 8258, one setting of the four drives the outriggers (the second lever's catch p171 by -0.4), which also shows the list had its two levers the wrong way round; the 8081's rear door pin, which holds the door shut in the file, is most likely an erratum.
33. **Shock absorbers slide, and a file may hold more than one model.** About 95 two-part shock absorbers in 27 sets were not joined at all, their rod or bore missing or cut short in the part data. They are mended once: the rod slides in its cylinder with end stops from the parts' shapes and no spring until the physics, where the rule so far made a joined pair a rigid strut; the solver owner confirmed that free sliding suspension is sound in the solver. A second vehicle in a set's file (five or six sets, 17 functions) is read as a further frame of one mechanism, not as a loose group welded to the nearest part and not as a mechanism of its own, so what joins or touches the two is kept; the solver owner builds its side after the tree's roots, about a day and a full check, and every set with one model must come out the same. The sliding CV ball of decision 28 is parked: on 12 sets it made nothing better and three functions worse, a half shaft that can slide at both ends drifting along its own line.
34. **Findings go upstream once, near the end.** The errata in LDraw models and the connection data for parts the LDCad shadow library lacks are worth reporting to the OMR and to the library, but Erik wants one report when close to all sets work, not repeated updates. Until then they are recorded here as they are found and signed off.
35. **The solver owner's mends of the check are in: 638 functions work where 626 did.** Its branch, merged with preview 4350f07 and checked in full (102 minutes): 13 better and 0 worse by result. Twelve go from partly to working (the 42055's function 7, the 42060's 6, the 42066's 2, the 42100's 5 and 6, the 42108's 5, the 8002's 3, the 8273's 9, the 8448's 2, the 856's 1, the 8862's 8 and 9), by the longer turn for a function geared far down, the push aimed afresh at each step, the rate rule and the piston rule; the 42078's function 4 now comes back. Four end further out of place and stay on the solver owner's list: the 42037's function 2 (136 LDU) and the 8002's function 6 (150 degrees) from the push's new aim, the 8259's function 1 (2.4 LDU), and the 42066's function 2 (391 LDU for 245), which now works only because it is turned much further. Preview was held from 4350f07 until this merge so that the branch was checked against the preview it joined; the worker's branches built meanwhile go in after it, the loose-part rule first.
36. **The lifted model and the freeze mend are in: 645 functions work.** Of the nine functions with a held part that stopped dead on their tyres, seven now work lifted with the part frozen (the 42082's function 3, the 42125's 3, the 8063's 3, the 8081's 3, the 8448's 4, the 8464's 4 and 5) and two are partly (the 42043's function 11, a gear in the differential fixed; the 8466's 7, its centre differential not driven and a tyre loose); none is worse and the guard on held parts never fired. The viewer has "Lift off the ground" in its options and `lifted=1` in the address, off by default.
37. **The sliding shock absorbers wait for the tree's roots, and an end stop ends a function's turn rightly.** Their first gate on 34 sets had 7 better and 24 worse, of three kinds. Eight stop where the shock absorber ends its travel, 0.17 to 0.4 radian, which is the real part's motion: the check's measure was wrong, so a turn or push that ends at an end stop from part data has gone as far as it can, and the function is judged by whether its listed parts moved. Six have no motion because a group reached only through slides gets its root by part number: decision 31's mend, which therefore goes before the shock absorbers and before the frames. Two creep on a long drag and fail a test of the 8880; that is with the solver owner. The test for a second model was too eager, taking a suitcase, a tool tray and three containers: a group is a further frame only if it does not touch the main model and stands on the ground, a group that touches rides along as a load, and the 42078's trailer, which touches because it is hitched, is to be read as one model with a joint at its hitch.
38. **A loose part can no longer make a function pass: 617 functions work where 645 did, and that is the truer figure.** Five of the worker's branches went in together. The loose-part rule of decision 26 takes 31 functions from working to partly, each naming a part attached to nothing: 13 on a second vehicle, the rest on wheels and assemblies that hang loose. The rim mounts bring wheels back onto their axles (the 8257's functions 4 and 5, the 8285's 2 and the 8418's 3 now work, and the 41999's, 8063's and 8418's rolling functions keep working for the right reason), and with wheels that grip, the 8285's two rolling functions end out of place, the solver owner's rolling. Erik's answers are in: the 8081's doors work, its controls the doors and not the sills; the 8063's trailer beams are skipped; the 8258's two lever steps are written and wait for the longer turn's reach and the guide. The guide and the 8464's rim change no result. The orchestrator's first run of this gate checked no set, the 30 names passed as one; it showed in a count that could not be right, and the gate was run again.
39. **The audit of the function lists is complete, and it found more faults in our reasoning than in the files.** All 186 sets were read against LEGO's booklets by an independent second reading (183 with a booklet; the 952, 858 and 8024 have none): where a functions page shows a function, the lists agree about 19 times in 20; about 39 functions differ, about 17 are missing, and about 520 are on no functions page and rest on build steps, inference or a video, for which about 134 video leads go to Erik. About 20 model-file findings came from it. Four assemblies that the reading has loose and that looked like file errors were none: the 8081's sill (the list named it for the door), the 42084's container (a load on a hook), the 42082's house (a separate building) and its roller ring (a bearing held by its neighbours). So a move that would make parts join is a lead to check in the build steps, never a finding, and an answer about a set from anyone, Erik included, is checked against the page. The seven reports are in docs/audits; one list for Erik's sign-off is being written from them. Second models have four kinds now: a frame that stands apart on the ground, a carried model that touches the main one and has joints of its own (fixed where it rests, but attached, so its functions count), a load, and an accessory. A Technic pin that turns in a part's round hole is not also a stud in it (the 8466's gull-wing door, whose strut's mount was fused to the frame that way): built, its reach being counted.
40. **The errata for official sets exist, and a correction waits for Erik's sign-off before it is applied.** Decision 30's file is built as designed: an entry for a set in data/errata names one line of the OMR file, moves a part or a submodel or adds parts after the last, moves no part number, is refused if its line is not found exactly once, and is applied by every reader of an OMR file. An unsigned entry is shown only on request, by the check's --proposed and the viewer's option=proposed. Three are proposed: the 42064's hatch (20 LDU high), the 8258's front outrigger drive gear (4 LDU off its hole), the 9396's left inner main wheel (missing); none changes a function's result by itself. The pin in a hole of decision 39 is in: 11 models read differently, the new freedoms being round bricks that turn on a stud and what they had held fast, with no function changed and no cost in solve time, so they stay free and not stiff.
41. **The springs: a shock absorber's slide is held as by its spring wherever the question is the car at rest, and a suspension function is worked lifted.** The creep of decision 37 was not in the loop closing. With the suspension free the solver found no steering bodies (each arm's swing turns its tyre a little, so nothing counted as steering) and the steering crept; and the rolling motion that scrubs least mixed in the chassis sinking and tipping. The solver owner's mend, on claude/solver-springs over the roots: a slide marked `springHeld` is held while the steering is found and while the parked and rolling motions are worked out, the suspension's own motions stay apart, and a spring's sliding sits in the friction pin tier, so it gives only where nothing else can. The worker's side is the mark on the slides its rule makes. When one arm is worked on the ground, other springs must give, since three held springs would hold the frame as three points hold a plane; so a function whose text works one arm or wheel through its travel means the model in the hand and gets `lifted` (40 functions in 29 sets by the worker's reading), and one that pushes the body down keeps the ground. Order into preview: the roots after their full check, the springs, then the sliding shock absorbers.
42. **Erik signs errata and function-list changes, and each needs the booklet's step, not geometry alone.** On 2026-11-14 he signed the first three errata (the 42064's hatch, the 8258's front drive gear, the 9396's left inner wheel), with which the 8258's outriggers work: that one function needed his corrected answer, the lever found by trial, the guide for its beams, the longer turn and the gear's erratum. He signed twelve items of the audit's list: nine function-list rows (the 41999's function 8, the 42080's 2 and 3, the 42064's 9 as not a function, the 8245's 1, the 42110's 5 and 15, the 8001's 5 and 8) and three functions to add (the 42004's backhoe, the 42066's canopy, the 42109's bonnet). The other seventeen rows of the list's first part are tried in the model by the worker before he signs: the orchestrator rates them about nine in ten, each resting on one reader, and an answer of the audit's own, on the 42069's trunk, was wrong and was caught by the model. Three groups that geometry called misplaced were not: the 42084's container and the 42024's skip are loads, the 42069's trunk is clamped between two parts. Parts 2 and 3 of the list, 205 rows, are not worked through by Erik; a row is used when its function's mend is built.
43. **More than one model in a file is read, and the main model's frame is a body that stands.** 637 functions work. Carried models (the 42060's excavator, the 42064's submarine and helicopter, the 42076's truck, the 42078's trailer) are attached where they rest, and their functions count; further frames (the 42055's mine truck, the 42062's handler, the 8245's vehicle, the 8257's second striker) stay partly with the cause second-model until the solver's side; the 8012's legs are joined at their click hips. Where a file has a further model, the main model's frame is chosen among the bodies that stand on the ground: by the count of structure parts alone, the 8245's frame was its robot's head and the 42055's its tower. Erik agreed that the frame should stand on the ground in every file; written by height it is wrong in 21 of the 36 models it changes (an axle or a stand takes the frame from the chassis), so two other forms are surveyed first: keep today's frame unless it does not stand, and the body nearest over the joints to all that touches the ground.
44. **The roots are checked and wait in the springs' branch.** Their full check: 621 against 619, six better (the 855's four racks, pushed along their slides; the 42129's function 3), the 42038's function 7 worse, its pendular axles each on a ball, for which a root held by a ball is now turned about the ball's centre in the check. They reach preview with the springs, after one full check of both.
45. **The roots, the springs and the ball-root mend are in: 640 functions work.** The solver owner's springs branch, checked in full against preview 8aee926 (624 against 621, six better, none worse by result) and merged into preview with the 47 sets rechecked that both sides had changed: six better (the 42063's handlebars on their ball, the 42129's function 3, the 855's racks pushed along their slides, the 8466's function 5), three further out of place and accepted (the 42038's function 7, the 42063's function 1, the 42055's function 9 on its second model). No joint is marked as spring-held yet, so the springs change nothing until the sliding shock absorbers mark theirs. The 42038's front axle, read as two bodies, is right in kind: a carrier that swings on its ball and a rack beam that slides sideways in it; the reading lacks that slide, and its function 1 works for the wrong reason until it has it.
46. **The general frame rule waits; a second tool was tried against our file faults.** Two forms of "the frame stands on the ground" were surveyed on the 190 models: keeping today's frame unless it stands clear changes 9 (4 right, 5 unclear, 4 plain cases missed), and the body nearest over the joints to all that touches the ground changes 40 (about 11 right, 20 wrong, landing on a roller or a pivot between the wheels). Neither is used; the narrow rule for files with a further model stays, and the eight models whose frame is plainly the upper body are noted in the worker's frame-forms.md for when a function fails for it. Kinebrick Studio 0.2.0-beta.1, another author's native app, was run on nine of our models (the 854's rear axle drives its engine at 3 to 1 there, its steering column reads as loose): of six faults we found in published files it found two, the 8258's gear and the 9396's rim, both as loose parts; its placement check looks only at 0.5 to 3 LDU. Its named Problems list and its headless report are ideas worth taking. The trials of the audit's first part in the model: seven rows agree with the booklet, two agree in kind, one disagrees (the 42108's boom knob), six cannot be told until a mend; with Erik.
47. **Erik's twelve signed changes to the function lists are in: 1,227 functions.** The five rows of the audit's first part that change words, name a part or are already borne out, and seven more he read against the booklet. Three functions are added, each at the end of its list: the 42004's backhoe swinging on its upright axle (partly, the drag stops early), the 42066's canopy lifted by hand (doesn't work, the control is jammed) and the 42109's bonnet lifted by hand (works). The 42064's hatch on the foredeck is no longer a function (it worked; now skipped), so the count of working functions stays at 640. The 42004 leaves the sets where every checked function works, 43 now.
48. **Errata can remove a part; the fork lifts have their lower axle; a model's own list of what moves exists.** Three of the worker's branches, merged into preview with no function better or worse (640 work) and one reading changed, the 950's. A removed part keeps its number and is drawn nowhere. The 950's lower axle and 8 tooth gear, which the model leaves out, are added in Kinebrick's copy (models/ is ours to edit). `npm run what-moves` lists what a model can do from the model alone, and `npm run what-moves-audit` puts it beside the audit's rows; it is a tool for a mend and for a model that has no list, not a replacement for the lists (decision 29). Proposed entries that wait for Erik: the 42109's three parts the set doesn't have, the 8838's axle with gears, the 850's lower axle. The 8838's axle with gears is ready too: placed by its line it turns in two beams, its 8-tooth gear meshes the 24-tooth gear on the swinging arm's pivot axle and its 14-tooth bevel the crankshaft's, as step 20 shows, and function 5 (the rear wheel works the engine) goes from partly to working. (The orchestrator first read it as meshing nothing, from a wrong query of its own; the worker's reading was right.) With their proposed entries the 42109's function 4 goes from partly to working and the 850's four functions work as before. The 42083's speed key stays proposed and is not put to Erik: a key lying beside the car is no fault in the file, function 6 works without the move, and which hole it goes into is read at medium confidence. A link that marks parts (`show=`) now starts at the right place whether or not the model has been drawn, and at where a removed part was.
49. **The viewer's motor takes larger steps when a step costs more than the frame.** Erik saw the monorail sets go choppy above 70 rpm. The train owner found the cause: a part kept turning turns at most 15 degrees a step, each step a full solve; the 6990's solve takes about 36 ms, so above 70 rpm a frame needs two steps, runs longer, and the next frame's turn is larger still, down to about 10 frames a second. It is our stepping rule, not the language's speed. The mend is general, not for trains only: the motor keeps its 15 degree step while the model keeps up, and where the steps a frame needs would cost more than about 30 ms it takes fewer, larger steps, none over 45 degrees; where that still cannot cover the frame's turn the motor runs slower than asked. A plain 45 degrees for every model is not taken, since a motor-driven lever could pass through a thin obstacle in one step. The train owner writes it in src/interaction.ts on preview-train, as one commit that the orchestrator takes into preview. The 42108's knobs (functions 5, 6, 7) turn anticlockwise, each seen from its own side, to raise the boom, lower the hook and extend the boom: Erik read page 267 himself, the audit had misread the middle arrow, and the model agrees with all three.
50. **Kinebrick Studio on the 43 green sets: it does 80 of our 151 green functions in full, and it is faster.** The second tryout (kinebrick-studio-green-sets.md in the shared notes), run headless: each function's control turned a quarter turn each way and the parts that moved judged by our check's own rule, without direction or ratio. 80 work in full, 42 partly, 29 not at all; every function works in 9 of the 43 sets. The cause is its reading of connections: in 29 functions the control is read as loose, in 41 it turns but what it should move stays still (racks 4 of 13, worms 2 of 10, pull-back motors 1 of 9, rubber bands 0 of 4; strings and model-local parts become loose pieces). Of five functions that fail in ours it mends none. It is faster: loading and connecting take 0.3 to 0.55 s for any size, where ours takes 80 ms to 2.5 s in Node and, in the browser on the 42114, 4.9 s to the model drawn and 20 s to the page done, 15 s of that the collision check, which blocks the page. What we take from it: the collision check must not block the page (to be mended in the viewer, after the function work in hand), and a named Problems list and a scriptable report stay on the list of ideas. Our reading of connections is the part that is ahead, and it stays in TypeScript; speed alone is no reason to change language while the slow parts are our own rules (decision 49) and one check.
51. **The shock absorbers slide: 649 functions work.** The worker's branch (decisions 33 and 37), with each shock absorber read as a body and a rod that slide along each other, the slide marked spring-held so that it gives only where nothing else can (decision 41), and the end stop rule. Checked in full on 184 sets against preview 095db1a3 and merged: 649 work (640 before), 275 partly, 124 don't; 22 functions better, 5 worse by result, 6 only further out of place after there and back (5 of those 6 now move at all and are better by result). The five worse are accepted, each with its cause and its next step: the 42000's function 5 and the 42129's function 6 jam or stop at a dead point at their strut cylinders (the pins to free, next in the worker's queue); the 42107's function 3 has a shock absorber (76537) with no slide data yet; the 42036's function 2 lists its shock absorber among the parts that stay, though it must move with the swing arm, a fault in the list's parts and not in the set; the 42038's function 7 lacks the rack beam's slide in its carrier (the guide family). They were partly or working before for the wrong reason, with shock absorbers that were rigid. The suspension functions' `lifted` field (decision 36) and these slides together are what the 40 suspension functions wait for; those still failing are sorted next.
52. **Erik signed the 42109's, the 850's and the 8838's corrections: 650 functions work.** His words, 2026-11-14: "Sign all three". Applied from now on in the viewer, the check and every script: the 42109 is read without the two friction pins and the bent beam that the set doesn't have, the 850 with its lower axle and 8 tooth gear and the handwheel on it, the 8838 with its axle with gears. The 8838's function 5 (the rear wheel works the engine) goes from partly to working; the 42109's function 4 already worked since the shock absorbers slide; the 850's four work as before. Six corrections to published files are signed now (42064, 8258, 9396, 42109, 850, 8838); the 42083's speed key and the 42040's seats stay proposed.
53. **A shock absorber swings on its pins: 652 functions work. The complete shock absorber read in pieces waits.** The worker's strut work came as two commits. The first is taken (cherry-picked, the ten sets whose reading it changes checked on preview): a friction pin through a shock absorber's cylinder or rod is locked to the other part it joins and turns stiffly in the shock absorber, never rigidly; the 42000's function 5 lists the moving parts of both sides, and the 42036's function 2 no longer lists the shock absorber among the parts that stay. 652 work (650 before), 273 partly, 123 don't; three better (the 42000's function 5 and the 42036's function 2 work, the 42063's handlebars come back in more ways), none worse. The second, the complete 6.5L shock absorber 76537 read as rod, cylinder and spring, waits: it gains the 42107's function 3 and the 42124's function 4 but loses the steering of the 42077 and of the 42124, and steering that stops at once is what a user sees first. It comes back, with the 73129 and the 41838, when a rod that the file places exactly at its end stop no longer stops the first motion (the worker's end stop rule) and when a motor's drag holds the spring-held slides at rest (the solver owner's; with it the 42129's function 6, whose axle hangs on four arms that fit only exactly in line). The worker's table has 39 suspension functions still failing, by cause (suspension-failing-2026-11-14.md in the shared notes).
54. **The viewer's first collision pass is to stop blocking the page, and its result is to be kept per set.** Agreed with Erik, 2026-11-14, for the end of the worker's current queue. On load the viewer finds the part pairs that already touch as built; on the 42114 that is about 15 of the 20 s to the page done, with the page frozen. The mend: the pass runs off the page's main line of work, the view turns as soon as the model is drawn, and a drag gains the collision stop when the pass ends. Erik asked whether such per-set calculations can be kept on disk: yes. What is kept must not depend on our reading, which changes daily: the pairs that touch at rest, for every near pair whatever bodies they are on, written at build time beside the packed model for the sets we ship and kept in the browser for a file the user opens, each under a key of the model's text after its errata, the parts library's version and the pass's constants; a key that does not match means the pass runs afresh, and a test fails for a shipped set. The worker measures where the time goes first, and lists what else is worth keeping (the shapes' trees, the reading, the pin worker's answers) without building it.
55. **Several frames in one mechanism: 663 functions work.** The solver owner's frames change: a file's further models (a trailer, a yard, a second machine) each get a frame of their own, and the stopgap that held a second model fixed, with its cause `second-model`, is gone. Checked in full over preview b32d688c (660 against 649), then merged with d5ad15c3 and the 13 sets checked again whose reading had changed since; on preview 663 work (652 before), 262 partly, 123 don't: 12 better (the 42055's functions 8, 9, 10, the 42062's 6, 7, 8, 10, the 8245's 2, the 8257's 5, 6, 8), 2 worse and accepted. The 42062's function 9 goes from partly to no: its control was stuck before too, and partly came only from the reach stacker drifting against the yard. The 42055's function 1 (the battery box switch) stays no and now ends out of place: its joint cannot turn, so the push rolls the whole model; the mend, a push that stops at once where the control's joint cannot turn, is written and waits for its full check, together with the rule that any drag that can go with the spring-held slides held keeps them held for its whole length (the 42124's steering with its complete shock absorbers in pieces).
56. **The 42129's axles are recorded as a loop that does not fit as built; no allowance yet.** Each axle hangs on four arms and two pinned shock absorbers, and can sway only if arms' balls open by 3.6 LDU for each radian, more than three times the play we allow; the real truck sways on the flex of its pins. Three ways were open: a tilt for a link or shock eye on one pin, an allowance for this set, or to record it. A per-set allowance is not taken (no per-set data for what the reading should find), and a general tilt is not taken on one case, since it loosens every model and may pass functions that should not. The solver owner counts, when it passes them, how many of the 13 suspension functions whose control jams are of this kind; a general rule is decided on that count.
57. **Motor tiers are dropped; the differential batch 3 is tried again on today's preview.** The solver owner made the tier (motors' outputs kept still before friction pins, friction pins before what the grabbed part turns against) and tried it: nothing changes on preview's 42109 and 42124, nor on batch 3, whose 42109 functions 2 and 3 fail with and without it. Batch 3's fault is a lock, the right half shaft read into the frame body, and an order of costs cannot mend a lock. The tier would touch every model with a motor and cost a full check for no gain, so it stays an unpushed commit with a note. Batch 3 was written before Erik signed the removal of the 42109's two friction pins and bent beam at that half shaft; the worker brings it up to preview and tries it again. The worker's errata pass is in (45b8078d): the 42108's knobs as Erik read them, and two proposed corrections that wait for him, the 42040's seats on their pins and the 42093's 8 tooth gear on the engine shaft.
58. **No general rule for more play; the collision pass is measured and its mend chosen.** The solver owner's count for decision 56: of the 13 suspension functions whose control jams, only the 42129's function 6 would move with a little more play (3.6 and 4.5 LDU for each radian, and even then only 0.03 rad to a dead point); six need a part to leave its connection by 36 to 123 LDU for each radian, four are locked by a firm connection, and two never try the play motion. So twelve are faults in the reading, traced one by one by the worker, the pendular axles and the bogie first (42070, 42122, 8273). The worker's measurements of the collision pass (decision 54), on the 42114, the 8012 and the 42077: the whole pass takes 8.6, 5.0 and 6.5 s; the shapes and their trees of boxes are cheap (0.1 to 1.2 s); the cost is a loop over every pair of parts (8.9 million pairs on the 42114, about 3 s) and the near tests on the pairs whose boxes meet (2.8 to 4.6 s). The mend: the loop finds meeting pairs by sorting the parts' boxes, the near tests run in a Web Worker, and the near pairs (513 on the 42114) are what is kept per set, which leaves about 1 s on the 42114. The trees of boxes, the reading and the pin worker's answers are not kept: the first is cheap to build and large, the other two go stale with every change to the inference.
59. **Twin tyres are dropped; a steered tyre may pivot on the ground about its upright; batch 3 stays out.** The solver owner tried twin tyres (two tyres side by side as one contact) as an experiment: neither case needs it (the 42066's nose wheel is locked in its leg, lifted or not; the 8285's drift is already small). It then ran lifted the 70 steering functions that fail or are partly: 63 are the same lifted, so grip is not their cause, and 6 are held by grip as they steer (42037 function 3, 8049 function 1, 8810 function 1, 8820 function 1, 9392 function 1, 42107 function 5): the contact must slide sideways 3.7 to 12.4 LDU and rise or sink 0.5 to 5.2 LDU for each radian of steer, from caster trail and kingpin tilt, and today's grip allows none with the car parked. The rule taken: a tyre on the ground whose wheel turns about an upright against its own model's frame may pivot on the ground about that upright's axis, so the slide allowed is the contact's distance from the axis, capped at one stud; an allowance and not a preference, nothing for the frame's own turning. Bounded by each wheel's geometry and not by a flat number, since a flat number passes a misread kingpin that the geometry would refuse; and every result that needed it says so in its stored result, so a works that leans on scrub is seen. It gets a full check of its own. The differential batch 3, brought up to preview by the worker, gives 657 against 663 (1 better, 10 worse: the 42109's half shaft still turns with the frame, the 42124's rear motor joins the frame once the differential is in pieces), so it stays out; one bounded trace of those two connections follows the collision pass.
60. **Pendular axles, bogies and swing arms are worked lifted: 666 functions work.** The worker traced the suspension functions whose control jams and found that several are no faults in the reading: an axle that swings about its own drive shaft makes its two wheels turn against each other through the differential, and the floor holds the tyres. They had not been marked `lifted` with the 40 before (decision 36) because their words name no wheel or arm ("rock side to side"). 17 functions in 13 sets get the mark, each with a note; it changes how the check works a function, not what the set does, so it needs no sign-off. 666 work (663 before), 263 partly, 119 don't: the 42063's function 5, the 42070's function 10 and the 8273's function 10 work; the 42122's function 3 goes from no to partly and ends out of place in 2 ways, the one worse line. Its two shock absorbers hang from links of two thin liftarms that turn freely on the frame, so a link swings where a shock absorber should slide; whether those links are held on the real Jeep is being read from the booklet. The 42122's function 2 is the solver's: the step's own motion and the loop closing disagree along a free motion, and the stray test reads that as a dead point. Five stay jammed lifted and are reading faults to trace (41999 function 3, 8272 function 3, 8261 function 2, 42039 function 6, 42111 function 6). The near test of the collision pass had a fault of its own, found by the worker's old-against-new test: the library's closest-point search returns its closest pair even beyond NEAR, and the test took any answer as near, so pairs up to more than 10 LDU apart were left unwatched by accident (311 on the 42114). The test now goes by the distance, NEAR stays 1 LDU, and all 26 motor cards run the same under both.
61. **The 42122's rear shock absorbers hang from links held only by contact; no rule yet.** The worker read the booklet (step 62 to 64, pages 35 and 36): each shock absorber's top hangs from a link of two thin liftarms that turns freely on a frame pin, as the file has it, so the reading is right. What holds the link on the real Jeep is that it lies flat against a liftarm of the swinging axle's carrier and the leaning shock absorber presses it there: held one way by contact and load, free the other. So function 3 stays partly, and neither a per-set hold nor a new rule is taken on one case. The solver owner is asked whether the rests rule fits a part pressed by a spring as it fits one laid down by gravity, which way each shock absorber goes on the real set, and how many functions have a spring whose far end hangs from a free link; the rule is decided on that.
62. **The springs rule and the push's bound are in; batch 3 is parked with its finding.** The solver owner's two mends, checked in full over 6ff88ff7 and merged with the twelve sets checked again that preview had changed since: a drag that can go with the spring-held slides held keeps them held for its whole length, the loops' closing included (in the viewer a car keeps its height while a motor or a wheel turns it), and the check's push stops at once with no motion where the control's joint cannot turn. 666 functions work as before, 262 partly, 120 don't; 2 better (the 42055's function 1 stops in 5 s where it took 1,200 and comes back; the 42036's function 3 comes back), 2 worse and accepted: the 42004's function 9 goes from partly to no, its partly having come only from the model rolling while the control turned nothing, and the 42063's handlebars turn their whole range now and come back a few degrees off in more ways. The differential batch 3: its "split" role renumbered the 42124's parts after p464, so the lists pointed at wrong parts; with the "pieces" role the numbers fit, but reading 65414c01 in pieces then stops the drives at once in four sets (13 worse, none better). It is parked there, the finding in gear-data.md, until the solver owner can look at why the drive through the pieces stops. The slowest functions cost what they cost for known reasons (the 9397: the choice among free motions at each step of 428 bodies; the 42100 and 42082: closing the loops of 500 bodies over long turns); none is mended now.
63. **No rule for a link pressed on its neighbour by a spring: one confirmed case.** The solver owner's answers to decision 61's three questions. The rests rule's row would fit the link's face on the liftarm, but it is two-sided, and the solver has no one-sided contact: it would have to be held while the shock absorber is short of its extended end and dropped at that end, and the inference would need a new finder for a body turning freely on one pin that holds a spring-held slide's end and lies flat on a part of another body. On the real Jeep the rising side's shock absorber compresses and presses its link on, and the falling side's extends its 2.3 LDU and then pulls its link off; the solver compresses the right side but swings the rising side's link too. A finder over the 42 sets with shock absorbers gives 12 free links in 5 sets, of which only the 42122's are confirmed to be of this kind and only 4 functions in 3 sets name such parts. So the rule is not built: two new pieces of solver and inference for one function. The 42122's function 3 stays partly. It is taken up again if the 42129's links or another set turn out the same.
64. **The collision pass no longer blocks the page, and its result is kept for every model.** The worker's branch (decisions 54 and 58), merged with no function better or worse and no reading changed. The pairs whose boxes meet are found by sorting the boxes; the near tests run in a Web Worker, so the model turns as soon as it is drawn; until the answer comes a drag or a motor is not stopped where parts meet, and the card says so; a pair that already overlaps when the answer comes counts as touching from the start, so a model is never trapped. `npm run collisions` keeps, for each of the 190 models, the pairs whose boxes meet and which of them are near, in data/collisions (3.4 MB), under a key of the model's text after its errata, the parts library's release and the pass's constants; a test fails when a key no longer matches, and a model whose key matches is skipped. The five largest models need about 8 GB each to make. In the browser, from the page's start, cold: the 8012 turns at 1.2 s and has its collision stop at 1.6 s (2.2 without a kept file), the 42077 at 2.6 and 2.6 (4.1), the 42114 at 4.6 and 5.1 (8.6). Before, the page was held until the pass ended: 14.5 s on the 42114 with the pane hidden. The near test's fault (decision 60) is mended with it. Kept file names use only letters, digits, spaces and . _ ( ) -, since the 42125's "#51" cut the import. The orchestrator made the new test pass in a checkout without ldraw/ (it uses the kept release there), and encoded the path the viewer fetches for `file=`; the dev server still cannot serve the 42125's own file, whose name has a #, which is noted for the worker.
65. **A steered tyre pivots on the ground about its upright: 668 functions work.** The solver owner's grip rule (decision 59), checked in full over d7598298 and merged: 668 work (666 before), 261 partly, 119 don't. Of the six it was made for, two gain: the 8049's function 1 and the 8820's function 1 go from partly to working and come back. The other four are held by something else (the 42037's function 3 moves wheels that should stay; the two bikes stop at a dead point; the 9392's tree never reaches the frame). Two are worse and accepted: the 8049's function 2 (the trailer on its hitch) goes from no to partly but comes back 178 degrees off, a flip that the pivot lets start and that is the solver owner's to mend; the 42020's function 2 still works and comes back 0.66 LDU off. 18 stored results carry the mark `pivoted`; the mark says the pivot was used, not that it was needed, and only three of the 18 changed by result. Each is within the cap of one stud for each radian of steer by construction. The nudge off a pose that is singular as built (the 42122's function 2) gains one function of the 20 that stop at once and gets no full check of its own; it waits on its branch.
66. **Four steering linkages have one link too many by LEGO's own design; no play rule frees them.** The solver owner counted the ten functions that stop at once at a dead point; seven are steering-like, and in each a pin would have to move across its hole. The worker read the booklets (42060 book 1 steps 22 to 24, 42084 book 1 steps 11 and 13) and measured the rest from the models: in the 42060, 42084, 42076 (function 6) and 42101 a crank on the upright shaft drives the track rod's middle by a pin, and its arm is not the knuckles' arm, so the rod's middle would have to lie on two circles that part by 10 to 40 LDU for each radian squared of the wheels' turn. The files match the booklets; the real sets steer on the give of several pins and a bending shaft. A trial of a loop's play as the sum of its joints' slack (0.2 LDU a joint, at most 1 LDU) did not work: it needs the play motion's rate set free, and then whatever is loosest moves (the 42107's function 8 ends 648 LDU apart, the 8081's function 4 jams), and none of the seven becomes works. It is dropped. The four are recorded as beyond a rigid-body reading, with a cause of their own, and belong to the dynamics phase. The other three are reading faults with owners: the 8868's function 1 is the motor on a pump drawn as one rigid part (the worker reads it in pieces), the 42122's function 1 has an axle in a round hole read as turning only (the worker lets it slide), the 42023's function 1 has a friction pin that hinges a T and is not freed (the solver owner). The grip rule's bound for the 8049's trailer is narrowed to a drag that turns a joint between two vehicles, since the wider bound took back the 8049's function 8.
67. **The small pump is read in pieces: 669 functions work. The kept collision files' key has a gap.** The worker's pump branch: the library's shortcut 74982 (a pneumatic pump drawn as one part) is read as its body and its piston with a spring-held slide, so the 8868's motor works its compressor (function 1, no to works; only the 8868 places the shortcut, and only its reading changes). The 42122's function 1 is of the design's kind after all, the fifth with one link too many: the part on its axle is held between the axle's body and a half bush and cannot slide. The gap: a kept collision file's key is made of the model's text, the library's release and the pass's constants, but a part read in pieces changes the list of parts without changing any of those, so the 8868's kept file went stale unseen; it is removed here, and the viewer does the pass itself for that set. The key is to take in what decides the parts list, and the sets of the complete shock absorbers get their files made again when that branch comes in. The role "axle" names only 11 axles, so the rules that read it (a part sliding along an axle, an upright axle resting) never see the 10, 11, 9 and 16 long axles or those with a stop; the worker widens it on a branch of its own.
68. **The complete shock absorbers are read in pieces, and tyres scrub only when a model is steered or rolled: 673 functions work.** One full check (the solver owner's, over 49627410) for two branches together, then merged with the pump and the 8868 checked again: 673 work (669 before), 261 partly, 114 don't; 6 better, 3 worse. The worker's branch reads the complete shock absorbers 76537, 73129 and 41838 as rod, cylinder and spring, and gives every shock absorber's slide 1 LDU of slack beyond each end stop: the 42107's function 3, the 42124's functions 4 and 6 and the 9392's function 5 go from no to works, the 8261's function 3 from no to partly, the 42124's function 2 comes back. The slack reaches further than the ten sets with a shock absorber at a stop as built: a shock absorber that reaches its stop during a drag goes 1 LDU further, so 13 functions in 12 more sets change by a step; one is worse (the 8448's function 5 comes back in fewer ways). The solver owner's bound: a steered tyre pivots for a drag that works the steering or rolls a wheel, and for nothing else; the 8049's trailer no longer flips (function 2 back to no) and its eight wheels still roll true. Three worse, accepted: the 8049's function 2 by design, the 8832's function 2 (its shock absorber slides now and ends 3 LDU out), the 8448's function 5. Five functions carry the new cause one-link-too-many (42023, 42060, 42084, 42101, 8868 as it stood in that check; the 8868's is mended by the pump). 17 results are marked pivoted. The 15 kept collision files of the sets whose parts changed are removed, as the new key shows them stale; the worker makes them again. The 42023's friction pin is not freed because the check asks for pins only where a control has no first-order motion; with it free the loops still bind, so it stays as it is.
69. **The 8110 and the 9397 checked slowly on preview ec7b2fa0; main is offered at this commit.** The slow check (2 hours 18 minutes): the 8110 has 8 functions working, 4 partly, 4 not, and the 9397 has 4 working and 4 partly, each as stored; one line is worse, the 8110's function 14 (the gears that swing the crane's subframe) comes back in 2 ways not back where it had 1. A first run was stopped three minutes in, when another session's stop command killed its children, and started again from the start; since then every session stops only the processes it started, by their numbers. Since ca95bf02, where main stands with 619 functions working, preview has gained 54: 673 work, 261 partly, 114 don't, of 1,227. Not in this offer, each on its own branch: the role "axle" on twelve more axles (the worker, in its gate), the bound on a motion in which an ungeared joint turns more than 50 times as fast as the control (the solver owner, clean on single runs), and the trial of a fixed lift for tyres on the ground, which follows the plain rule "tyres stay on the ground": that mended the 8020's tumble but jammed steering and rolling in nine functions, because tyres differ in height by a fraction of an LDU and leaning pivots lift the contact. The lists' own words decide where a function is worked lifted: the 42037's function 2 and the 42039's function 2 are, the 42039's with its differential carrier among the parts that stay.
70. **A motion in which an ungeared joint whirls is refused, and a result says whether a model came back torn: 672 functions work.** The solver owner's full check 27 over d69e86dd, merged. The bound: a motion in which a joint that gears do not tie to the driven joint turns more than 50 times as fast as it is a dead point; without it the 42125 tore at the viewer's small steps (20 bodies moved 35 degrees in a step of 0.002 rad). 22 working functions that run through high gearing are untouched. One function is worse by it and accepted: the 42066's function 2 (the motor raises and lowers the landing gear) goes from works to partly; it had worked while its wheels spun 230 to 300 times as fast as the drive and came back 390 LDU out, so the stop is the honest result. 672 work (673 before), 262 partly, 114 don't. `torn` is stored beside `back`: the furthest part against its own frame; of 107 functions that do not come back, 71 come back torn and 35 whole but in another place (the tumbles). Three forms of a rule against tumbling were tried and none is in: tyres that may not lift at all (jams rolling and steering, since tyres differ in height by a fraction of an LDU and leaning pivots lift the contact), a fixed lift (the drag mixes motions so that the travel cancels and the lifts add up), and a tier of its own for the least lift (it whirls joints to shave a hair of lift). The fourth, lift weighed inside tier 4 with every part's turning, is in trial. 52 models stand on fewer than three tyres as drawn (29 would lean under 2 degrees onto a third); that is set aside with the list as its record. The role "axle" on twelve more axles is not in: it made 8 functions worse, by slides given to parts that cannot swing and by tipping the 42099's frame in a near tie of the count that chooses it; the worker mends the slide rule and keeps the frame count as it was.
71. **The tumbles are set aside for the dynamics phase; where the 376 functions that do not work stand, by cause.** The fourth form of a rule against tumbling, the tyres' lift weighed inside tier 4 at 1 LDU for 1 radian, mends the 8020's function 2 and makes no result worse, but three rolling functions end further out, and further still as the weight grows (the 8270's function 6 from 0.81 to 57.8 LDU, the 8837's and the 42053's); it does not let the 42066's legs fold either. Four forms tried, each traced, none clean: what is missing is gravity, which the kinematic reading does not have. The tumbles stay labelled by `torn` as models that come back whole in another place. On preview c04c02fb, of 1,227 functions 672 work, 262 are partly and 114 don't; the 376 by the check's cause: not-driven 142 (129 partly, 13 no), part-fixed 56, loose-part 48, control-jammed 47, stopped-early 25, control-fixed 25, stay-chosen 14, stay-forced 9, one-link-too-many 4, others 6. So 318 are faults of the reading and the worker's, and the largest single group is a part that can move but that nothing ties to its control. The solver owner takes stay-chosen (14, each one step from working) and then the 71 functions that come back torn; the worker goes on by cause, the axle role first.
72. **Main is at d69e86dd; a decisions page.** Erik moved main on 2026-11-15 (ca95bf02 to d69e86dd: 673 functions work, 619 before). He asked for a page, kept open beside the chat, with everything that needs him; it is described under "What Erik sees" and is kept current from now on. The 42099's rear drive does not jam because of which body is the frame: both side gears of its rear differential are drawn 3 LDU off the casing's line in the published file (the only differential of the 190 models that is off), an erratum is being written, and behind it the rear CV joints' outer halves wander, which is the parked sliding CV ball. The 14 stay-chosen functions sorted: three are a hold of tier 4 that was not taken (the solver owner mends that, no tuning), three sit at that hold's edge (no tuning), two prefer less steering, two keep the frame still first, and four are really stay-forced and get their label mended.
73. **A part that only turned on its own free axis counts as back; full check 28.** A part that comes back to its place within 0.5 LDU but stands turned about the axis of a joint it is free to turn on (a pin in its hole, a wheel on its axle) is counted as back, unless a gear is on it: nothing holds such a part at an angle on the real model either, and a gear's angle does matter. The solver owner's full check of 97d7e2f0 (5e891946) keeps 672 functions working and makes none worse; 7 are better by ways not back: the 1972's function 1, the 42032's 2, the 42039's 5, the 42042's 4 and the 42111's 10 now come back in every way, and the 42054's 10 and the 42056's 11 leave fewer ways not back. Not back now: 71 torn, 30 moved whole (35 before). `--causes` now judges each control as the check did (it explained every control from the function's stored parts, and measured a control on a slide or a ball against nothing), and the cause test tells a control turned from a control carried round. Four labels move: the 42039's function 6 (control-jammed to part-fixed), the 42111's 6 (control-jammed to stopped-early), the 8880's 3 (control-jammed to not-driven) and the 8838's 3 (stay-chosen to stay-forced). By cause now: not-driven 143, part-fixed 57, loose-part 48, control-jammed 44, stopped-early 26, control-fixed 25, stay-chosen 13, stay-forced 10, one-link-too-many 4, others 6. Gate on the merge into preview 92dacae4: type check, 789 tests, the ratchet (7 better, 0 worse).
74. **A drive shaft that hangs only on CV balls is held in its sockets; a wheel turned against its differential is turned lifted.** With the proposed 42099 erratum the rear differential turns, and each rear drive shaft (a 52731 ball at both ends, in the side gear's socket and in the portal hub's) then has no joint at all: both balls are read as joined for drive only, nothing says where the shaft's axis is, and the shaft swings 62 to 66 LDU about the model's origin. This is decision 28's case at its extreme. The rule, for a body whose every connection is such a ball in a socket: the ball in the socket nearest the frame is held at its place in the socket, and each other ball is held on its socket's axis, free along it. One end held stops the drift along the shaft's own line that parked the bore rule; the other end free along its bore lets suspension change the distance between the sockets (the 42000's case). The rule is for these hanging bodies only; a shaft with a joint of its own keeps what it has. The solver owner tries it on the 42099 with the proposed erratum, the 42000 and every other set with a 52731, and it goes in with a full check if nothing is worse. Second: a function that turns one wheel to show the other wheel of the same axle turning the opposite way through a differential cannot be done with the model on the floor, where the other wheel grips; such functions are worked lifted, as decision 60's are, whether their words say so or not. The worker lists and marks them, each checked by name. The 42109's function 2 is one, and its stay (the 36 tooth gear on the motor's shaft stands still) is right. For batch 3 when it comes back: the split differential adds 6 to every later part number, so the 42109's signed errata (p381 to p383) must be renumbered with it, and with the car lifted least motion still turns the stiff motor output (0.674), which is the solver owner's to mend against decision 20.
75. **The way-back trail is dropped; the cause labels do not depend on how a function is run.** The trail gave the check's way back the poses recorded on the way out, as a hint where a step would not close. Tried on five functions that do not come back (42080 fn 1, 8002 fn 6, 42079 fn 2, 42124 fn 1, 8274 fn 2) none came back, and on the quick set no function changed its result while three ended worse (the 8837's function 6 from moved whole to torn, the 42107's function 5 and the 8074's function 1 further out). The ways back that stay out start at a dead point, where neither the plain closing nor one from the recorded poses closes, so the trail does not reach the cause; it gets no full check. The patch is kept as way-back-trail.patch in the notes folder. `--causes` over all sets at 5e891946 changes no label in any of the 1,227 functions.
76. **The axle role is in: 674 functions work.** Every plain axle the library has now reads as an axle (3737, 23948, 50450, 50451, 60485, 44294, the axles with a stop and the axle with a stud), so the axle slide and the upright-axle rests see them; an axle's stop is a wall nothing slides past; and a part slides on its axle only where it can really swing. The worker's branch (c1c65135), its 38 sets run again by name on preview 017bc025: the 42037's function 4 and the 42074's function 2 go from no to works, 4 better and none worse by the ratchet, and 44 sets now have every checked function working (43 before). The 8002's function 1 works but a hinged group (b65) comes back 89.7 LDU out, 18.8 before; it is with the solver owner's torn results. Gate on the merge into preview 529fb0da: type check, 789 tests, the ratchet. The solver owner's full check 29, started before this merge, does not hold the axle role; the 38 sets are run again when it is merged.
77. **Eleven differential functions are checked lifted; the casing in their lists is Erik's to sign.** Under decision 74 the worker marked 12 functions that turn one wheel against a differential (42041 fn 2, 42099 fns 5 and 6, 42109 fn 2, 42110 fn 4, 42111 fn 3, 42124 fn 3, 42129 fn 3, 8274 fn 4, 8466 fns 5 and 6, 8865 fn 4). Eleven are in (902fb604): 674 functions work as before, 2 better by ways not back (the 42099's functions 5 and 6 now come back to 0, from 57.9 and 499.9 LDU), none worse. The 42041's mark is held back: lifted, its casing p153 stands still as a differential's does, but its list names the casing among the parts that move, so it would go from works to partly; the old works was the floor case, the other wheel gripping. That list fault, and the same one in the 42099's functions 5 and 6, wait for Erik on the decisions page; the 8274's function 4 may have it too and the worker looks. Left unmarked as doubtful: 42029 fn 4, 42083 fn 5, 8110 fn 4, 8859 fn 3. Gate against preview 8d9e008c: type check, 789 tests, the ratchet.
78. **A part on two parallel axles slides along them; the jammed suspension functions sorted.** The worker traced the suspension functions that stay jammed when lifted. The 42063's function 4: its fork is one rigid body between two balls, where the set's fork telescopes (book 1 page 107): the top piece rides in round holes on the two leg axles, which the lower fork grips. The axle slide is made only for a part on one axle; it is extended to a part that rides in round holes on two or more parallel axles gripped by one other body, under the same test of need (the loop it closes cannot move otherwise) and with the axles' ends as stops. The worker builds it. The 42111's function 6 binds to second order (two links 40 LDU ahead of the axle's ball; the real model moves on the play in the ball cups): recorded as a loop that does not fit, as the 42129's function 6, since the play rule is dropped (decision 58). Its shock absorbers are not joined because the file draws the rod 1.3 LDU off the cylinder's axis: an erratum, not a wider tolerance. The 41999's function 3 needs its erratum adding the shock absorbers the file lacks (decision 30). Two list faults go to Erik: the 42039's function 6 (its control is on the drive shafts; the lower arms are) and the 8261's function 3 (the pivot axle turns with the arm). The reason to hold them is that such a part stays where it is built unless the mechanism needs it to slide. In trial on the axle role's 38 sets and the quick set.
