99. **The worm's mesh bound comes from the worm's shape: 695 functions work.** Decision 87's first job (claude/worm-mesh, src/inference/gears.ts). A worm's threads stand from its core (7.0 LDU on the 4716, 1.2 modules below its pitch radius of 10) out to 12.7; within the core's radius either side of the worm's axis they reach at least 3 LDU into a 24 tooth gear's teeth, more than a tooth's height. So a gear's middle plane may lie off the worm's axis by half the gear's width plus the worm's core radius, 11.75 LDU for a 24 tooth gear, where the rule allowed only half the gear's width. It is not an erratum: the 42070 holds its gear 5 LDU off the worm's axis with thin liftarms, a real detail of the build. Over all 190 models only two read differently: the 42070 (its slewing worm) and the 8094 (the worm of its base's slewing drive, 7 LDU off). The 42070's functions 6 and 8 and the 8094's function 1 go from partly to works; none worse. Gate: type check, 790 tests, the ratchet against 1434cab7.
100. **A friction pin that locks an actuator or a shock absorber is a pivot.** The 42070's front outriggers (function 7) cannot extend: each front actuator's arm pivots on a friction pin (p1385, p1416), which the reading holds rigid, so both ends of the actuator sit on one body and its piston cannot slide. The 42039's rear arms were the same case (decision 81 moved that function's control to get round it). The check frees friction pins only for a control that has no motion, and here the control, a motor's shaft, can always turn. The worker proposed that the check also free the pins that hold a listed part still; refused, because the check would then pass what the viewer does not show: the motor run in the viewer would still leave the outriggers standing. The rule goes into the reading: a linear actuator and a shock absorber exist to change their length, so where such a slide cannot move at all with the friction pins rigid, the friction pins that hold it (as pinsToFree finds them) stay joints, stiff ones, as the pins of a linkage built on friction pins already do when the whole model could not move otherwise. It is that same test made for each actuator and shock absorber slide, once per reading. The worker builds it and names the models that read differently before any run.
101. **The complete universal joint is read in pieces: 697 functions work.** The 61903 is LDraw's whole universal joint, two ends and a cross as one part; read whole it gripped both axles and was rigid. It now has the role "pieces", as the 76537 has (claude/uj-pieces, data only). Seven sets have it (41999, 42049, 42054, 42070, 42082, 42113, 42114) and no other model reads differently. The 42070's function 3 and the 42049's function 1 (steering) go from no to works; the 42082's function 1 (handwheel steering) from no to partly. Worse by ways not back, accepted: the 42082's functions 1 and 2, where the steering, now free as on the real crane (its four knuckles turn together), does not come fully back and leaves a rear wheel's axle 0.7 and 2.6 LDU out. The merge was held because the 42082's check took 3561 s where it had taken 1679; profiled alone both ways its function 4 takes 295 s and 315 s, 7% more, so the doubling was the machine's load. The profile found a real cost besides: a quarter of the time of a big model's check goes to the axle slide rule, which looks at every cylinder for every axle at every reading; the worker makes it index the cylinders once, with no change to any reading. On decision 100's rule the cost is being measured (reading all 190 models took 908 s with it against 396 s) before it is weighed. Gate: type check, 790 tests, the ratchet against 94968656.
102. **The axle slide rule is indexed: reading all models takes a third of the time.** The rule that finds parts sliding on axles searched back to the shaft through all of a model's connections for every candidate part, and held every axle against every cylinder; on the 42082 (421 axles, 8,481 connections) that was 64 s of a 75 s reading. It now builds its indexes once per reading: each part's and body's connections in their order, the bodies joined by turning or sliding connections, and the cylinders in a grid by their boxes, visited in their old order so that nothing found changes (claude/axle-index, src/inference/axleSlides.ts). No line of any of the 190 models' readings differs. One reading of the 42082: 75.5 s to 12.9 s; all 190 readings: 366 s to 123 s; the 42082's function 4: 295 s to 178 s. Every check and every load in the viewer gains. The cost of decision 100's rule is measured again on this faster reading. Full check 31, started before this merge, does not hold it; its results are the same by the equal readings, only its times are the old ones. Gate: type check, 790 tests.
103. **The friction-pin rule of decision 100 is limited to linear actuators and must read cheaply; shock absorbers are parked.** The worker ran the rule on its 20 sets (claude/actuator-pivots 8212aa7a on 61026466): 3 functions better (the 42023's function 2 and the 42081's function 3 from partly to works, the 42070's function 7 from no to partly), none worse, 795 tests pass. Two costs stop the merge. (1) The 42039 and the 42125 time out at 60 minutes: the rule frees their rear suspension arms' pivots (shock absorbers), and the solver's drag over those joints is slow (the 42125's function 2: 39 s before, over 900 s after). (2) A model with a stuck actuator or shock slide is built a second time with every pin free: the 42083 reads in 72.8 s where it took 2.8, the 42115 in 37.2 s (2.6), the 42078 in 24.4 s (6.6); 35 of the 190 models pay, and all 190 together take 234 s where they took 70. The viewer pays this at every load. So: the rule applies to linear actuators only; it frees only the friction pins on the bodies in the loop that holds the stuck slide, if that gives the same sets; the aim is that no reading grows by more than about half and all 190 stay under about 100 s. If that fails in two hours of work, the orchestrator decides on a kept per-model result. The shock absorber part waits for the solver owner (note shock-pivots-slow-drag.md in the shared notes): it is right in the real model, but the drag must be fast first.
104. **The 9392 Quad Bike's steering (function 1) has three causes, each with its own owner.** The worker traced it and changed no file. (1) The steering arm p111 hangs on the pin end of a frictionless axle-pin (p74 3749; the booklet agrees, step 13, page 36), so it can roll about a fore-aft axis; the drag mixes this roll into the steering and does not undo it on the way back (2.8 LDU, 7.9° not back). The reading is right; holding a loop's free motion that the drag does not need is the solver owner's work, after full check 32 (note 9392-steering-roll.md). (2) The hubs turn about 3 times as far as the column and reach a true dead point at about 60° of hub, 0.23 to 0.26 rad of column, where the check wants 0.75 rad of the control. New rule for the check, with no new bound: a control that stops at a true dead point of its linkage has reached its end of travel, and that is not "stopped early" when a part the function lists to move has itself turned by half of REACH or more. The solver must say that the stop is a dead point and not a jam. The worker builds it on claude/dead-point-travel, tries it on the sets with functions that stopped early, and it gets a full check before a merge (decision 96). (3) The part 43093 (axle pin with friction; 4,250 pins in 110 set models) has no role, so its pin end reads as frictionless. It gets the "friction pin" role as the real part has it; a digest first (claude/role-43093) shows how many models read differently, then the orchestrator gives it a check slot.
105. **The friction-pin rule for actuators gets a kept per-model result.** The worker narrowed the rule as decision 103 ordered (claude/actuator-pivots bef2af79): linear actuators only, and for each stuck actuator slide only the friction pins of the pieces within one joint of the actuator's ends are freed and tried. It reads exactly as the version with every pin free (equal digest over all 190 models); 12 sets read differently from preview (42000, 42006, 42023, 42024, 42038, 42042, 42050, 42054, 42061, 42070, 42078, 42081); all three gains remain; 795 tests pass. The search itself is now cheap (the 42083 reads in 2.7 s, where the first version took 73.5), and all 190 models together take 100 s where they took 70. But a model with a stuck actuator still pays one or two extra builds: the 42078 reads in 21.1 s where it took 6.6, and 13 models grow by more than half, so the aim of decision 103 is not met for the viewer. So the pins the rule frees are kept per model, in the pattern of the collision pass: a small file per model, a key that fails when the model or its errata change, one command that makes them again, a test against a stale file. A reading with a kept result does no extra build; a model without one runs the search live. Aim: each of the 190 models reads within a tenth of its time on preview. The orchestrator merges the rule with the kept results and the checked 12 sets.
106. **The 43093 gets the "friction pin" role by way of full check 33; the dead-point test is agreed.** The worker's digest of the role (claude/role-43093 a6b489fc, one line in data/roles.json, 795 tests pass): all 110 models that have the part read differently. 103 models have fewer bodies (2,164 fewer in all), 101 lose free motions (1,296 in all), 750 more friction connections are held rigid, and no model gains bodies or free motions. Of 1,114 controls, 969 can move before the check frees pins and 930 after: 43 lose their motion (most in the 42108, the 8273, the 42038 and the 42054) and 4 gain it (the 42066's function 7, the 42080's functions 2 and 3, the 42107's function 8). The check may give some of the 43 back by freeing pins. The real part has friction, so the reading with the role is the truer one, and only a full check shows what it does to the functions. Full check 33 is the worker's, with 6 jobs, after full check 32 is merged; it also holds the dead-point rule of decision 104 if that is ready. For that rule the solver could not tell a true dead point from a jam: its mark only says that a step was refused. The test now: the mark is set and the model has fewer free motions at the halt than at the start of the turn (the 9392: 22 while steering, 21 at the stop). From the stored results, 10 functions are partly because they stopped early, and 5 of them stopped at a dead point (the 42066's function 4, the 42070's function 9, the 42076's function 2, the 8466's function 8, the 9392's function 1).
107. **Full check 32 is merged: 700 functions work. The torn mends and the steering mends are in, each narrowed.** Full check 31 (claude/solver-grip c0f5c4bd) had 698 working with 23 better and 4 worse, each traced by single-function runs with one mend switched off in turn, and was not merged; the mends were narrowed and checked again in full as check 32 (7907b055, 75 minutes, 6 jobs). What is in: (a) a drag does not step into a pose it cannot leave (decision 84), and a step that is undone is tried again at half the size, down to 1/16 of the usual step, so that a control at a dead point still turns as far as it can; (b) a push back in two phases, which makes nothing worse; steering mend 1: the steering of a lifted model is found from all its tyres, but it is not kept still for a drag that rocks a steering part about a level axis (the 8849's rocking front axle), and the test asks for tyres first (four sets on skis or tracks gave an error without that; fixed in 1943fba9 and those four sets checked again, where nothing else can change); steering mend 2: a gear on a steering body is left out of the steering only if its mesh holds it still against the frame (the 42080's turntable p586 is the steering itself and stays in); the 42053's function 7 is checked lifted. Result against preview: 24 better, 1 worse. From partly to works: the 42053's function 7 and the 8285's function 1; from no to works: the 42122's function 2; 21 more come back where they did not or in fewer ways (the 42082's functions 1 and 2, 2 to 0 and 8 to 0; the 8285's function 2, 6 to 0; the 9397's functions 8 and 9; the 9395's functions 2 and 7). Worse and accepted as the cost of mend (a): the 8826's function 1 (the handlebars steer) from partly to no; even the smallest step from its start pose is undone, and before it was partly only with 2 ways not back. Two end further out with the same result: the 42066's function 2 (180 to 306 LDU, mend (a)) and the 8020's function 2 (11,714 to 11,748 LDU, steering mend 2; it works both ways). 52 sets have every checked function working, 25 of them fully, 78 are one or two short. Gate: type check, 791 tests, the ratchet against 69cecb38.
108. **Three trials that are not taken as they stand: the dead-point test, the holds for free loop motions, the shock absorber pivots.** (1) The dead-point rule of decisions 104 and 106, built by the worker (claude/dead-point-travel 14e6b5f6), changed no result over the 59 functions that stop at a dead point: the count of free motions is the same at the stop as at the start, because the drag undoes the step that would cross the dead point and so stops one step short. The free-motion test is dropped. No test at the stop can tell the real end of a linkage's travel from the end of a linkage read wrongly, since both are true dead points; what protects the result is the bound on what the function shows. The rule is now: the solver's dead-point mark, and a part the function lists to move has itself turned by half of REACH or more. The worker lists every function that changes by it and marks those whose control should turn without end in the real set (a crank, a motor), for which a stop is never an end of travel; the orchestrator rules on those before the full check. (2) The solver owner counted that 14 of the 25 functions that are partly with ways not back have a free motion of a loop that moves the strayed parts, and tried holding such bodies at a drag's start (claude/solver-roll 4ed53898, switched off). Narrowed so that no listed part is lost, 2 of the 14 come back fully (the 42038's function 2, the 8074's function 1) and 3 end further out (the 8844's function 1, 52 to 1,369 LDU; the 8049's function 8; the 8466's function 8), with 184 bodies held on the 9396. Set aside. The 9392's own roll is gone since check 32. (3) With the friction pins of shock absorber pivots free (the part of decision 100 left out by decision 103), the 42125's function 2 hung because a push step chose a motion through the loop frame–p326–p433–p327 in which the pushed point hardly moves (8,980 rad per LDU) and cut itself into about 256,000 pieces. A guard is kept for the next full check: a push step stops as stuck where a joint that gears do not tie to the pushed part would turn at a wild rate, as a drag step already does. With it the function ends in 181 s but 106 LDU from its start, so the shock part stays out; it gave no gain in the worker's run. The solver owner next sorts what is left for the solver on preview by group and gain, with the time of the checks as one group.
109. **Only 41 of the 347 functions that are partly or no are the solver's; the order for the solver owner.** The solver owner sorted preview a16baaa3 from the stored results. The solver's groups: stopped at a dead point (11; the worker's dead-point rule may gain 5 to 8), stopped with no motion (8), a push stopped as stuck (6), a part listed to stay chosen to move (8), loops too tight for want of play (4, and the 5 recorded earlier make 9), too little motion or a first control that failed (6), and ways not back (25 that are partly, most of them partly for another cause too). The other 306 are faults of reading or of a function list, which is the worker's side; the solver owner counts them by cause for the question of a second worker. The time of the checks: a full check is about 27,100 s of work (75 minutes on 6 jobs); the slow 8110 check is 19,300 s, and its function 3 alone takes 7,300 s, so it cannot end in under 2 hours however many jobs it has. The check works each control from the model as loaded, so a slow set can be split by control with the same results; the longest job is then about 2,100 s and the slow check ends in about 55 minutes on 6 jobs. About 80% of a large model's time is dense linear algebra (free motions worked out afresh at each step); splitting the free motions into a model's independent parts would cut it but moves the last digits, so it waits. Order: (1) the split of the slow check by control, proven equal against the orchestrator's slow run on a16baaa3; (2) the 8 stay-chosen functions traced, list faults to Erik; (3) a design for play, from the parts' real clearances, before any build; the other groups wait.
110. **The slow check split by control is built; no tolerance in the tiers; the second-worker question is put to Erik.** (1) The split (claude/solver-split c0d7f11a, only scripts/check-functions.ts): in the slow check a function with several controls not worked together goes a control to a process, and the parent combines the shares with the code one process uses; where combining would need the first control's solver state the function is checked whole. Forced on the quick set it gave all 210 functions the same but for the times. It is merged when one slow run with it equals the orchestrator's slow run on a16baaa3. (2) The 8 functions where a part listed to stay is chosen to move, traced: four are least motion choosing between two equally free motions (the 42043's function 5, the 42120's 3, the 8824's 1, the 8862's 2); two go to Erik (the 8820's function 2, whose list holds still a frictionless axle that is free to turn; the 8832's function 2, where the shock absorber's top mount cannot be told from the scan); the 9397's function 8 is a reading fault (the engine cylinder 2851 rides on its piston; the worker's); the 8202's function 1 belongs with the mesh stops. A tolerance in how the tiers are settled is refused: a band of a few percent is a tuned number, and it would reach only the 8862 (1.00 against 1.01), not the 8824 (0.875 against 1.13). What decides between two equally free motions in the real set is friction; a rule from that is designed later from these four cases (stay-chosen.md). (3) The solver owner counted the 303 functions that fail by reading or by a list: not-driven 136, part-fixed 49, loose-part 47, control-jammed 40, control-fixed 25, stay-forced 6, on about 150 to 200 distinct faults; seven part types recur over many sets (62274c01, 62821, 94925, 92695, 92696, 2851, 18947) and the worker takes them first. The question of a second worker is on Erik's page with the rate (21 and 13 more working functions on the last two days) and the usage (5% of the week about 8 hours after its reset, three sessions at work); the orchestrator's advice is not yet, and to move the solver owner to faults of reading when its three tasks are done.
111. **Play becomes an amount taken from the joints, not a rate: the design is accepted for a trial.** The solver has play today: where no exact motion goes, the joints that close a loop may open, at most 1 LDU per radian of the control and 0.1 LDU a step; pushes take none. The solver owner measured what the nine functions that fail for want of play need (play-design.md in the shared notes): the steering of the 42023, the 42084 and the 42060 needs 0.73, 1.19 and 2.04 LDU per radian and stops today after 0.01 to 0.02 rad; the 41999's function 3 needs 2.0 on a push; the 42129's function 6 needs 3.6; the 42111's function 6, the 42052's function 4 and the 8272's function 3 need 27, 98 and 114, which no real play gives, so they are faults of the file or the reading; the 42101's function 1 stops at a dead point. The design: every joint that closes a loop has a clearance by its kind (a pin or axle in a round hole about 0.1 LDU radially, a beam along its pin about 0.25 LDU, a towball in its socket about 0.05 LDU; none for gears, tyres, universal joints and holds), a loop's budget is the sum over its joints (1 to 2 LDU for a steering loop, under a millimetre), and a pose is allowed while every loop closes within its budget, at any rate; pushes take play too. A loop that needs 27 LDU per radian then moves 0.01 to 0.07 rad and stops, as now. LDraw draws pins and holes at one size, so the clearances are estimates of the real parts. They are not tuned: the trial runs at the estimates, at half and at double, and only if the gains hang on the value is Erik asked to measure. How much of a turn is taken on play is recorded; a function is not made partly for it, and the orchestrator rules on the list of functions that move only on play. The trial: the nine, the quick set, and every function that takes play today, since a long turn at 1 LDU per radian may stop under an amount. Likely gain 4 to 6 functions; a full check before any merge.
112. **Full check 33 is not merged: the 43093's friction role makes 52 functions worse.** With the role alone over preview a16baaa3 (claude/check-33 e08e7139, 796 tests pass) 670 functions work where 700 did: 10 better, 52 worse, all from the role. About 40 controls lose all their motion: steering wheels that turn on their pin (the 42033's function 5, the 42037's 7, the 42043's 9, the 42093's 4, the 8285's 4, the 8289's 2), knobs and gears (the 42038, the 42054, the 8273, the 42108), doors and covers (the 42043, the 42056, the 42077, the 42115, the 42078); five more come back in fewer ways and four go from works to partly. Of the 43 controls that the role leaves with no motion as read, the check gives back only 5 by freeing pins; for 38 it frees nothing. One case is traced so far: the 42093's steering wheel is read as part of the frame, and in the build with every pin free its body has free and stiff joints, yet no pin is found to free. The principle: a control that the real set lets the hand turn against a friction pin must turn in the viewer, so the role, though true of the real part, comes in only together with a mend that gives those controls back. The worker traces that one case with a time limit; a small general mend is then run over the 110 models, and otherwise the role is parked with a note. The full-check slot is kept for the push guard (decision 108) and the dead-point rule together.
113. **Erik's answers of 2026-11-16: the 42070's rear differential and the 8820's list are signed, and no second worker yet: 701 functions work.** In his words: "Sign 42070", "Sign 8820 list", "Second worker: not yet". (1) The 42070's erratum (the rear differential p845 turned half a turn, decision 97) is signed in data/errata and the set checked by name: no result changes (6 work, 2 partly, 2 don't). Its function 1, the drive to all six wheels, is still jammed with the differential right and the universal joints in pieces; the worker traces what still jams it. Its kept collision file is stale by the erratum and is removed for the worker to make again. (2) The 8820's function 2 (the rear swinging arm) no longer lists the shock absorber's top axle p31 among the parts that stay: the axle sits in round holes and is free to turn with the shock absorber (decision 110). Checked by name, the function goes from partly to works; 53 sets now have every checked function working. (3) No second worker for now. The solver owner takes faults of reading when its three solver tasks are done, the worker takes the recurring part types first, and the orchestrator asks again about 19 November with the usage and the rate measured. The slow 8110 check running on a16baaa3 still stands for the next offer of main: only data and plan text changed since. Gate: the ratchet (1 better, 0 worse).
114. **The 8832's function 2: Erik rules that the function works in the real set, so the file is wrong where it does not allow it.** His words of 2026-11-16: "this should be functional so if the file does not allow it, the file is wrong - given that, I believe the top sits on the seat". The rear swinging arm is locked today with the roll bar and its top axle held, and the scan does not show the shock absorber's top mount. The worker finds what locks the arm, puts the shock absorber's top on the seat with the fewest parts moved, checks the set with the correction, and Erik signs the exact correction when he sees it (an erratum if the published file is at fault; a mend of our list or of our own redrawing otherwise). The same reasoning may serve other functions where a booklet clearly shows a working function and the file locks it: the function's working is itself evidence about the file.
115. **The dead-point rule gains three functions on its trial and gets one more test: the control must turn the loop's own link.** With the rule as the solver's dead-point mark and the bound on a listed part (decision 108), 3 of the 59 functions that stop at a dead point change, all from partly to works, and none is worse: the 42066's function 4 (the knob turns the rudders through links: 0.37 rad of the control each way, the rudders 0.92), the 42076's function 2 (the gear on top steers the propeller pods: 0.70 rad, the pods 2.03) and the 9392's function 1 (the handlebars: 0.23 rad, the hubs 0.80). Each is a knob or handlebars on the linkage itself, whose travel ends at the linkage's limit in the real set. Twelve more functions only get the stop name "end of travel" with the same result, and four of those have a control that turns without end in the real set (the 8273's winch knob, the 42035's gear that turns the cranks under the dump body, probably the 42124's front motor and the 42044's bevel-gear knob); for them a stop at a dead point is never an end of travel. So the rule gets the worker's test: the stop is an end of travel only when the control turns a link of the loop that stops it directly; a control that reaches that loop only through gears, a worm, a chain or a motor keeps the stop name "dead point", since the real one would slip, stall or drive the crank over. It goes into full check 35 with the push guard, each with its own switch. Merged besides: the 42070's kept collision file, made again after its erratum (ae53882a).
116. **The part 2851 (engine piston) stands for three faults; a listed part counts as moved by the farthest it got, not by where it ends.** The worker sorted the 8 functions that name a 2851. (A) The check judges a part by where it ends: in the 42075's function 2 and the 42043's function 10 the crank turns 3.9 and 4.2 times as fast as the wheels, so over the check's 1.5 rad it makes about one whole turn, the pistons end almost where they started (they slide 26 LDU per radian on the way), and the check calls them still. The rule, for any listed part and not for pistons alone: a part counts as moved if at any step on the way it was MOVED or more from its start pose. The farthest distance is used and not the summed path, so that jitter cannot add up. It is tried on the 49 part-fixed functions and goes into full check 35 with the dead-point rule and the push guard. (B) In four functions the crankshaft is not driven (the 42056's function 2, the 42083's 4, the 9392's 2, the 8840's 2); the fault is earlier in each drive, and the piston only shows it; traced one at a time. (C) Two lists name a piston among the parts that stay where they mean the cylinder (the 9397's function 8, the 42022's function 2); a piston must move in a running engine, and it does. The worker prepares the list change for Erik to sign. Also on Erik's page since this decision: the 42070's second correction (the small bevel gear p848 moves with the differential casing that the signed erratum turned; with it the truck rolls and function 1 works) and the 8832's (the roll bar's two bracing axles drawn 1 LDU beside their holes; with it function 2 works, and the shock absorber's top stays on the roll bar's axle as drawn).
117. **The 8110's slow check at a16baaa3 passes as stored, and main is offered.** The slow check ran on a16baaa3 (full check 32 merged) in a temporary worktree: 8 functions work, 4 partly, 4 don't, the same as at e90ae7d8, and no function differs in its result, its cause, its way back or its stop. It took 25,618 s of work, about 4 hours 20 minutes beside two full checks. Since a16baaa3 no code changed on preview: only set data (the 42070's signed erratum, checked by name; the 8820's signed list, checked by name; two proposed errata that do not apply until signed), the 42070's kept collision file, the plan and the decisions page. So main is offered at preview's head with this result: 701 of 1,226 functions work, where main has 685. The run is also the reference for the slow check split by control (decision 110): the solver owner runs the slow check once with the split, and every result must equal this one. Erik moved `main` to 24366c8f on 2026-11-16.
118. **Full check 34 is not merged yet: the 43093's role with the mend in fixPins has 17 worse where check 33 had 52.** The cause of check 33's loss: fixPins treated every pin as joining two round holes and locked it rigid to one of them; an axle pin is already held by its axle end, so its only turning connection, the pin end, was the one locked, in every build, and nothing could free it. The mend: a pin that already has a rigid connection to a part is not locked to another; its turning connections are friction joints, held when the model can move otherwise and freed on demand. The digest reads the same 110 models differently and no other. Full check 34 over a16baaa3 (claude/check-34 d352012c, 797 tests pass): 694 functions work where 700 did, 10 better (from no to works: the 8285's function 14, the 42066's 7, the 42080's 2 and 3; six come back where they did not; the 42107's function 8 from no to partly), 17 worse. Of the 43 controls the role leaves with no motion as read, 31 now turn in the check (5 in check 33). Worse: 9 controls with no motion (the 42039's function 9, the 42043's 5, the 42077's 7, the 42078's 7 and 8, the 42079's 1 and 2, the 42080's 1 and 4, the 8274's 5), three from works to partly (the 42078's 4, the 42100's 5 and 6), four with more ways not back. First probes show three different causes (a motion that turns no pin the check counts; no motion even with every pin free; a loop one link too many after six pins are freed). The role is true of the real part and is merged when these are traced; the tracing goes to the solver owner after its play trial (note role-43093-check34.md), and the worker keeps its order. Full check 35, the worker's, holds the dead-point rule, the moved-on-the-way rule and the push guard, each with its own switch.
119. **Erik signed the 42070's second correction and the 8832's; the 42070's drive works: 702 functions work. The slow check is split by control.** In his words of 2026-11-16: "Sign 42070 spider", "Sign 8832". (1) The 42070: with the small bevel gear p848 moved with its casing, the truck rolls, and function 1 (the XL motor drives all six wheels) goes from no to works when the set is checked by name; 7 of its functions work, 2 partly, 1 doesn't. (2) The 8832: the two entries are signed. (3) The slow check's split by control (claude/solver-split, decision 110) is merged: one slow run with it gave all 16 functions of the 8110 equal to the reference run in every field but the times, in 84 minutes of wall time on a loaded machine, where function 3 alone took about 12,600 s of work. The 42070's kept collision file is made again with both signed entries. Gate: type check, 791 tests, the ratchet (1 better, 0 worse).
121. **Play as an amount is kept for a full check, with two mends and two changes to the check.** The first trial (claude/solver-play 9ec708d9, 54 sets, at half, full and double the estimated clearances) had the same gains at all three sizes but lost five working functions. Two causes, both mended. (a) The check and the viewer free friction pins only for a control with no motion at its start, and with play there was always a motion, so pins stayed and controls moved on play alone; now play is the last resort: exact motion first, then pins freed, then play, and the start test does not count play. (b) A fault of the build let a play step through when closing the loops undid the dragged joint's own turn (the 8272's function 3 seemed to move 6 rad on play while its control turned 1e-14 rad); now the dragged joint must turn as asked. With both, at the estimated clearances, over the 54 sets against a16baaa3: works 198 to 201, partly 82 to 84, no 39 to 34; function time +24%. From no to partly: the steering of the 42023, the 42060, the 42084 and the 42101, and the 42111's function 6 (its pins are freed now); from partly to works: the 42023's function 2, the 42081's 3, the 8275's 5 (pins freed, no play) and the 8880's 8; from works to partly and accepted as truer: the 42079's function 2, whose "works" came back 56 LDU out of place on the old play; seven functions keep their result with one to four more ways not back. The sizes matter for no gain at the estimates, so Erik is not asked to measure. Two changes to the check come with it, each with its switch: a way counts toward a result only if the control has turned or a push has moved (a listed part's motion with the control at rest had made the 8272's function 3 partly); and a listed part tied to the control at a small rate that is not zero has the cause too little and gets the longer turn (the 42052's doors pass their toggle inside the budget, 0.25 of 2.8 LDU, but move 0.002 rad for 1.5 rad of the control through the worm). Full check 36 holds play and these two, after check 35 is merged. The solver owner then takes the 17 functions worse with the 43093's role.

123. **Full check 35 is merged: 709 functions work. The dead-point rule, the moved-on-the-way rule and the push guard are in.** The worker's full check over preview 3591800b (claude/check-35 7d769c00) had 5 better, 0 worse and no time-out, each gain traced to its switch and each one foretold by its trial. The dead-point rule (decisions 108 and 115): a stop at a dead point is the end of travel when the solver marks a dead point, a listed part has turned half of REACH or more, and the control is no motor and sits on the loop itself; the 42066's function 4, the 42076's function 2 and the 9392's function 1 go from partly to works. The moved-on-the-way rule (decision 116): a listed part counts as moved by the farthest it got from its start, not by where it ends; the 42075's function 2 and the 42043's function 10 (pistons whose crank makes about a whole turn in the check's turn) go from partly to works. The push guard (decision 108) changes no result; it stops a push step that would turn a joint at a wild rate. Gate: type check, 793 tests, the ratchet against 9a4a7516 (5 better, 0 worse).
124. **The recurring part types, so far: a chain's reach from the gear's tooth, the hub driver, and the smooth driving ring connectors, which are data faults of ours and not faults of the files.** (1) The 9392's chain, placed link by link, found no wheels: its circles' middles are 2.2 and 3.0 LDU off the two 16 tooth gears' axes and the reach was 2 LDU. The worker's mend (claude/chain-reach) lets a circle be on a wheel only if that wheel has a gear within the circle, in its plane (a pivot the chain bends round is no wheel), and raised the reach to 4 LDU; functions 2 and 3 go from partly to works. The raise is not taken as a number: the reach is the whole depth of a gear's tooth (2.25 times the module of 1 mm, 5.6 LDU), since a chain further off than that would not sit on the teeth; the worker reads all models again with it. (2) The 8840's V2 engine: the file draws rods and pistons 4.3 and 4.6 LDU apart; a proposed erratum is with Erik. (3) The 42083's W16 is not driven because nothing joins the wheel spindle axle driver 32494 to the hub 35189 it is seated in, so all four hubs spin free; the worker adds that fit as connection data, from the parts' shapes. (4) The driving ring 18947 reads loose in five functions (the 42056's function 3, the 42107's 1, the 42114's 2, the 42129's 4, the 42131's 5) because it rides on a 26287 or a 42195, for which our data has only the inner axle hole. The worker proposed errata swapping them for the ridged 18948. Refused: Rebrickable names the 26287 and the 42195 "Technic Driving Ring Connector Smooth" (the 42195 with two flat sides), so the real sets from 2016 on do carry the ring on them, and LDraw's name "axle joiner" and round outside misled us. The two parts get the outer fit the ring slides on, as the 18948 has, without its detents. The 42056's function 2 (the flat six with the gearbox in neutral) follows from its function 3. Two more ring functions on the 18948 itself (the 42042's function 6, the 42112's 4) are traced after.
125. **The chain mend is merged with its reach derived: 712 functions work.** claude/chain-reach 6900e6b2: CHAIN_REACH is 2.25 times the module (2.5 LDU), 5.6 LDU, a tooth's whole depth; the parts' data hold no tip or root radius, so the formula is the source. A circle of a chain placed link by link is on a wheel only if that wheel has a gear within the circle, in its plane. Over all 190 models two read differently, both in their loops. The 9392: its chain runs on its two 16 tooth gears, and functions 2 and 3 (rolling works the engine; the engine turns the wheels) go from partly to works. The 8844 Helicopter: the chain's rear end, 5 LDU off, now runs on the rear leg's own 24 tooth gear, the gear that our errata note said it should go round (the file places a second copy 5 LDU away); function 1 (the crank raises the landing gear) goes from partly to works, function 4 comes back in two more ways, and no correction of the file is needed for it. So the derived bound found a function that the tuned one (4 LDU) would have missed. Gate: type check, 794 tests, the ratchet against 8cf9c5d5 (4 better, 0 worse).
126. **The 43093's role: the held pin ends are the whole cause of the 17 worse functions; pins are found by trial, for a part with no motion only.** The solver owner freed every 43093 as read on claude/check-34, and all 17 came back to preview's results or better, so the role's cost is in finding which pins to free. pinsToFree reads the build with every friction pin free and names the stiff connections that turn in a motion of the part; it finds nothing where that build has no motion of the part either (the 42080's function 1), or where the part's motion turns no stiff connection (the 42079's functions 1 and 2; the 42083's function 8 with the worker's hub driver). And the check frees pins only for a control with no motion or a listed part held fast; 6 of the 17 are not-driven. Decided, by the principle that the check passes only what the viewer shows and that a friction pin gives way where something forces it: (1) for a control with no motion and for a listed part held fast, the pins are found by trial in the as-read build (every held pin near the function's parts free, then halved down to the pins needed); (2) the viewer's drag does the same search for a dragged part with no exact motion, before play; the motor frees nothing; (3) for a part that does not follow (not-driven) the check frees no pin, since no drag in the viewer would. For those the pins are sorted first: if they carry bodies that gears or a motor drive (gear stubs), the mend is a rule of the reading, a driven friction pin end read as a turning joint, the same for viewer and check. Also from the worker: the hub driver for the 42083 (a ball socket at the 35189's spindle end, so a seated 32494 turns the hub; the drive reaches the differentials) waits on this, since its function 8 fails the same way.
127. **The smooth driving ring connectors are in; the rotary catch that shifts their rings is next.** claude/ring-connector: the 26287 and the 42195 get the outer fit of the 18948 in data/corrections.ldr (our data for the 18948 has no detents, so all three share one entry). 8 models read differently (42056, 42083, 42107, 42110, 42114, 42115, 42129, 42131), all with driving rings on smooth connectors; the 20 models that use a 26287 or a 42195 as a plain joiner read as before. Checked by name: no result changes yet, 0 worse. The five rings now ride their connectors and their cause moves on from loose-part: nothing shifts them. In these gearboxes the ring is moved by the 35188 rotary changeover catch, and the check's findCatches (src/shift.ts) knows only the 6641, whose fork swings; the 35188 turns on an axle parallel to the ring's shaft, 40 LDU across, and moves the ring along the shaft through a cam. The worker builds it as a second kind of catch, with the ring's travel per angle measured from the part's cam, for the viewer and the check alike. Ten catches in seven models (42083, 42107, 42110, 42114, 42115, 42129, 42131) wait on it, and with them the 42056's flat six and the 42083's W16. Gate: type check, 794 tests, the ratchet against 03939972 (0 better, 0 worse).
128. **The friction-pin rule for actuators is merged with its kept results: 715 functions work.** Decisions 100, 103 and 105, on claude/actuator-pivots-2: a friction pin that locks a linear actuator's slide stays a stiff joint; for each stuck actuator slide the friction pins of the pieces within one joint of the actuator's ends are freed and tried; the pins found are kept per model in data/pivots (190 files, 12 with pins; `npm run pivots` makes them, `--check` works each out afresh; a key covers the model's parts after errata, its part kinds' roles and connection entries, and the rule), so a reading with a kept file does no extra build, and a model without one runs the rule live. The 12 sets that read differently, checked by name: the 42023's function 2 and the 42081's function 3 from partly to works, the 42070's function 7 (the outriggers) from no to partly; the other nine keep their results; 3 better, 0 worse. On the merge 33 kept files had stale keys by the ring connectors' data (decision 127) and the signed errata; made again, their keys changed and no pin did. Shock absorbers stay out (decision 108). Gate: type check, 989 tests, the ratchet against 129df156 (3 better, 0 worse).
129. **The rotary changeover catch is in: 716 functions work; each catch's own drive is the next fault.** claude/rotary-catch, src/shift.ts: the 35188 is a second kind of catch. Its cam is measured from the LDraw shape (s\\35188s01.dat): the ring is in its middle at 0 and 180 degrees, at +10 LDU from about 50 to 130 degrees and at -10 from about 230 to 310; a catch holds every ring 40 LDU round it, two or three at different angles, and so steps a sequential gearbox. Of the 16 rings round the 10 rotary catches in the sets, 15 stand where the cam puts them (the 42083's p398 is drawn in its middle at 90 degrees). In the viewer the 42107's gear lever slides its ring 10 LDU each way and back when the drag ends, as for the 6641. The 42107's function 1 goes from partly to works; the other gearboxes still fail because their catch does not turn (the 42114, the 42129, the 42083, the 42115, and the 42056's paddle shifters): each catch's drive from its control is a fault of its own. The older catch is untouched: findCatches gives the same 49 catches in the 31 sets with a 6641 and catchShifts runs the old code for them (the worker's static check), and the orchestrator checked eight of those sets by name with no change, in place of a full check. The 42083's function 4 on a trial merge with the hub driver: a gear is in as built, the differentials turn, and the drive still stops between them and the engine. Gate: type check, 992 tests, the ratchet against 35e318bc (1 better, 0 worse).
130. **What turns the rotary catches: a paddle stepper shared by three sets, and a fault of its own in each gearbox.** The worker's test on preview a2155085: in all five gearboxes that still fail, the catch's joint moves with its control's joint held, so every catch spins free of its control; no gear mesh is simply missing (no pair of gears within 70 LDU across any gap), and knob wheels are read well where they mesh. Shared by the 42056 (function 3), the 42083 (function 2) and the 42115 (function 2): a paddle swings a lever or pawl that steps a wheel a quarter turn per pull (a knob wheel, or the pin wheel 24505 in the 42083). That is a ratchet, an intermittent drive, and the solver reads only continuous joints. It needs a new kind of mechanism, a stepper; the worker writes a design note first (what identifies it in the geometry, what the viewer shows, what the check does), and it is built on the orchestrator's word. Each set's own: the 42115's second catch (its knob wheel p441 is drawn 20 LDU along the axle from the wheel it should mesh with) and the 42083's second catch (its shaft is joined to nothing that turns) go to the booklets, and to Erik as errata if a file is at fault; the 42114's function 2 and the 42129's function 4, where a motor's drive does not reach the catch's shaft (the 42129 through slip clutches), are traced first, as direct gains now that the cam works.
131. **Two gearbox faults traced: the check worked the wrong motor, and the two halves of a slip clutch were not joined; the H gate waits.** (1) The 42114's function 2: the model is right, but the L motor's case shares a body with the XL motor's case, and the check's worked() took the first motor joint on the case's body, which is the XL motor's; so the function ran the wrong motor and the catch never turned. The mend: a motor control turns the joint of its own motor connection, in the check, in pinsToFree and in the what-moves audit. In place of a full check the worker lists every function with a motor control and which joint the old and the new lookup choose; the sets where they differ are checked by name. (2) The 42129's function 4: its two slip clutches are pairs of a 46834 and a 46835, and our connection data gives each half only its own axle hole, so the halves are two bodies and pass no turning. They get a fit at their meeting face in data/corrections.ldr, as a friction tie if the data can say it (the halves turn together and give way on demand, so a catch at its end does not jam the motor), and rigid with a note otherwise. The part is also in the 42100 (3 pairs), the 42112, the 42113 and the 42131. (3) The 18947's last two functions: the 42042's function 6 is an H gate, where each 6641 sits 20 LDU to the side of two rings and slides along its pivot to choose one; only the 42042 has that layout, so it waits behind the part types. The 42112's function 4 has a lever that does not move at all and is traced with them. The stepper's design note is written (stepper-design.md).
132. **A motor control turns its own motor's output: 717 functions work.** claude/own-motor: motorOutput in src/mechanism.ts takes the body a motor turns through the motor connection that names the motor itself (the old lookup, the first motor joint on the case's body, is the fallback); the check's worked(), pinsToFree and the what-moves audit use it. The proof in place of a full check: of the 50 motor controls in the sets' functions the old and the new lookup differ for 6, in 3 sets, each where two motors' cases sit on one body; for the other 44 nothing can change. Those three sets checked by name: the 42109's function 3 (the L motor steers) goes from partly to works; 0 worse. The 42114's function 2 now turns its rotary catch and ring and is short by one part (p53, a 35186); the 42129's function 4 needs the slip clutch too. Gate: type check, 992 tests, the ratchet against f2d2c583 (1 better, 0 worse).
133. **The old linear actuator's piston (62274c01) names a break before the actuator: universal joints drawn bent, and gear trains.** In all 8 of its functions the actuator's screw is read and its input turns free of the control. Three breaks are a universal joint of three parts (two ends 62520, a cross 62519) drawn bent, which our reading does not join: the 42131's function 8 (a cross arm 3.9 degrees off its end's pin hole, 0.68 LDU off axis), the 9397's function 4 (28 degrees) and the 8258's function 3 (the far end's axle hole at a right angle to the drive axle). The rule, with no new number: a cross between two ends is a universal joint when each end's fork centre lies at the cross's centre, within the tolerance already used for a ball in its socket; three such parts can be nothing else, so how far an arm is drawn off its hole does not matter. An angle bound was refused as a number chosen for the case. What is then still wrong in the 8258 and the 9397 is looked at in the file before the booklet. The other five (the 42078's function 9, the 8258's 5, the 42131's 6 and 7, the 42081's 2) have inputs that carry gears and are traced one at a time. Also on its way: the slip clutch gets a friction fit (a fit read as a turning connection that is held and freed on demand, with neither part locked), since a clutch that cannot slip would jam a motor at a catch's end.
134. **Full check 36 (play) is not merged; its faults are mended and one loss is traced before full check 37. The 43093's role stays out; the pin search is tried without it.** Check 36 over aea7387d had 711 working against 709, 12 better and 25 worse, 24 of them only by ways not back, and the 42055 timed out. The causes, each mended on claude/solver-play: with play on, a push's exact step was judged by the budgets, so an axle slid out of place (the 42032's functions 2 and 3, 80 and 130 LDU); the orchestrator's rule "an exact step from closed loops is judged as without play" is right for pushes only, since for turns the steering gains come from exact steps that hold to first order and leave the loops a hair open, which is the real give. The check's test of a control's motion before a push, and a push step's aim, counted play, so the 42055's battery switch was pushed on play for 2,526 s; they ask for an exact motion now, and a push step on play must move the pushed point at least half its step. A step after a play step counted a last step from an earlier drag. Cost: play off and on take the same time on the same load (the 42043's function 8: 195 s and 182 s); the sets that grew most in check 36 took almost no play, so their growth was the machine's load. Left: 21 better than preview (seven to works, the steering of the 42060, the 42084 and the 42101 to partly) and 6 worse by ways not back; four are accepted as the budget letting a real motion go further (the 42082's function 1, the 8245's 1, the 8274's 5, the 8466's 8); the 42024's function 3, which reaches 0.23 rad where it reached 1.5, is traced before full check 37. The 43093's role: with pins found by trial (claude/solver-pins) five of the 17 come back, three controls still have no motion, and the not-driven cases are of mixed kinds, so no rule of the reading is built and the role stays out. The trial search alone is tried on preview's control-jammed, control-fixed and part-fixed functions without the role; in the viewer it must try the dragged part's own held pins first, be bounded, and show that it is looking, since a drag's start waited 5 to 40 s and once 188 s.
135. **The slip clutch is in, as a friction fit: 718 functions work.** claude/slip-clutch ed641cc3: the two halves 46834 and 46835 meet in a round fit at their faces (data/corrections.ldr) and have the role "slip clutch"; slipClutches in src/inference/pins.ts, next to fixPins, holds the fit as a friction pin's joints are held (rigid, held) and lets it turn stiffly where the model cannot move otherwise or where the outer half is freed. All 8 seated pairs in the sets are read as held. The five sets with the part (42100, 42112, 42113, 42129, 42131) checked by name: the 42100's function 4 goes from partly to works, 0 worse. The 42129's function 4 stays partly: its centre catch now turns, the rear catch and both rings do not yet. On the merge a stray scratch file of the worker's was dropped, and the 42129 kept preview's results and was checked again with the merged code. Gate: type check, 994 tests, the ratchet against 2bf80b22 (1 better, 0 worse).
136. **The trial search for friction pins gains on preview without the 43093's role, and goes to full check 38.** On preview 2bf80b22 with the search and without the role (claude/solver-pinsearch aee99784), the 103 functions that are control-jammed, control-fixed or part-fixed were run one at a time: 7 better, none worse. From no to works: the 41999's function 7, the 42080's functions 2 and 3, the 8272's functions 2 and 3; from no to partly: the 42040's functions 6 and 7; each by one freed pin that the reading of the all-free build had not named. The 8272's skis were counted in decision 111 as faults of reading that should stay stopped; they work once each ski's hinge pin is freed, so that was wrong. Four more get a body for their control but still no motion (the 42036's function 5, the 42121's 2, the 8081's 6, the 8880's 10). Every wait is 8 s or less and 9 builds at most, so the bound stays as built (the part's own body first, then the nearest held pins in growing sets, up to 64) and the long waits were the role's alone. The search, in the check and in the viewer's drag, gets full check 38 on preview after play's full check 37 is merged. The role is weighed again after that.
137. **Three merges from the part types: 722 functions work. Three corrections wait for Erik.** (1) claude/through-motor: a motor whose output goes through it (69730, the 42131's drive motors) joins an axle in each face; the 42131's function 4 goes from partly to works. (2) claude/uj-centres (decision 133): 6 sets read differently (42029, 42038, 42068, 42125, 42131, 9397), 11 more couplings, none wrongly found; the 42038's functions 2 and 3 and the 42125's function 1 go from partly to works. Gate on the three: type check, 996 tests, the ratchet (4 better; the 9397's two as said).
138. **Full check 37 is merged: play is an amount taken from the joints. 726 functions work, and 95 do not work at all (105 before).** claude/solver-play 58241ea9, over preview 2bf80b22, 76 minutes, 6 jobs: 9 better and 13 worse by the ratchet, every one of the 13 only in ways not back with the same or a better result; no function goes from works or partly to no. What is in (decisions 111, 121, 134): a pose is allowed while every loop closes within its budget, the sum of its joints' clearances by kind (a pin or axle in a round hole 0.1 LDU, a beam along its pin 0.25, a towball 0.05; none for gears, tyres, universal joints, holds); play is the last resort, after exact motion and after friction pins are freed, and the start test does not count it; the dragged joint must turn at least half its step; a push's exact step is judged as without play, and a push's tests and aim ask for an exact motion; the viewer's drag frees pins before it takes play. Two changes to the check: a way counts only if the control turned or a push moved; a listed part tied at a small rate has the cause too little and gets the longer turn. To works: the 42039's function 5, the 8275's 5, the 8880's 8, the 8848's 4. From no to partly: the steering of the 42060, the 42084 and the 42101, and the 42023's function 1, the 42024's 2, the 42054's 2, the 42078's 6, the 42100's 7 and 8, the 42115's 8, the 42129's 6, the 9395's 3. Ways not back that grew: with those gains (eight functions), and four accepted as the budget letting a real motion go further (the 42082's function 1, the 8245's 1, the 8274's 5, the 8466's 8), and the 9395's function 4 (works; a pin ends turned on its own axis). Cost: the functions' work is 60,433 s against 61,449 s, no more than before. The clearances are estimates and no gain hangs on their size (decision 121). On the merge ten sets changed on preview since (the slip clutch's, the through-motor's and the universal joint rule's) kept preview's results and are checked again by name with the merged code. Gate: type check, 998 tests, the ratchet against f56f93ac. The ten sets were checked again by name on cb1d6145 (the 9397 with a limit of 200 minutes): with play the 42100's functions 7 and 8 and the 42129's function 6 go from no to partly, each with one or two ways not back, and the 9397's function 4 comes back in one more way; 726 work, 229 partly, 92 do not.
139. **Full check 38 (the pin search) is done and gains; the rule for when the search runs is being settled before the merge.** On preview with play (claude/solver-pinsearch bba69c45): 731 functions work against 726, 6 better and 3 worse, the worse only in ways not back; the functions' work fell by 5%. To works: the 41999's function 7, the 42080's functions 2 and 3, the 8272's functions 2 and 3, each by one freed pin; to partly: the 42040's functions 6 and 7 and the 42036's function 5. The 42080's two are accepted with 2 ways not back each. A real loss: the 42062's function 3 (the wheels roll freely) worked on play without any pin freed, and the search, which came before play, freed a long friction pin so that a part swings 157 LDU away. The rule to settle it, with the solver owner's two offers weighed (pins before play as built, which keeps the loss; or the search only where a part cannot move even on play, which loses three gains): the search runs for a part that cannot move even on play, which in the check is the control, and also a part the function lists to move that stays held fast after the drag (the check's existing second trigger); in the viewer, a dragged part that cannot move even on play. pinsToFree keeps its place before play. The reason: pins were put before play because play could keep a function's part from ever moving, and the second trigger answers that case; where play alone gives the function its motion, a freed pin that lets a part dangle is the greater harm. The 15 functions in which the search fired are run with it; only they can change.

141. **The pin search is merged: 736 functions work, and 84 do not work at all.** claude/solver-pinsearch 7216344d, full check 38 with the rule of decision 139 as built: where pinsToFree names no pin, the friction pins that hold a part are found by trial in the as-read build (the part's own body first, then the nearest held pins in growing sets, up to 64, halved down to those needed).
