142. **The viewer does not yet show two kinds of the pin search's gains; it is mended to the check's rule before anything else.** Asked to drag by hand in the viewer at a926fab5, the solver owner found: the 8272's ski (function 2) shows "Finding the pins that hold it…", gets no pin and does not move, because the viewer's test "moves on play" says yes where the check's says no; and the 41999's body shell (function 7) starts a push that goes nowhere and looks for no pins, because the viewer has no test for a push with no point to push, which the check has. So for up to eight functions counted as gains in decision 141 the check passes what the viewer does not show, against the project's own rule. Not reverted, since the mend is small and the reading is right: the viewer gets the search for a push with no point to push, and the two tests of "moves on play" become one function that the check and the viewer both call; where they differ in the holds a drag chooses, the check follows the viewer if the viewer is the truer one. All eight gains are then dragged by hand with the pane shown, and a gain that cannot be made to show in the viewer is taken back. The lesson for the gate: a change to how a drag starts is tried by hand in the viewer before the merge, not after.
143. **The changeover catch is known by a role, and the 42112's lever is in: 737 functions work.** claude/catch-51149 9b7781f0, signed by Erik on 2026-11-17 ("Sign all seven", the 42112's lever). The 6641 and the 51149 have the role "changeover catch" in data/roles.json, and findCatches goes by the role with one reach of 60 LDU for both; the 51149's LDraw file is built from the 6641's own subpart, so fork, reach and width are the same, and it adds an axle hole along its arm, which data/corrections.ldr gives it (the shadow library lacks the part). A catch counts as structure where the frame is chosen, or the 8002, whose shell is ten catches, would change its frame. Only the 42112 and the 42113 place a 51149. The 42112's function 4 has the white connector p384 as its control (the booklet's steps 38 to 43) and goes from no to works. The kept pins of the 33 sets with a 6641 have new keys by the role and the same pins. Gate: type check, 993 tests, the ratchet against 3016a988 (1 better, 0 worse).
144. **The check does not apply the viewer's collision stops; the 42112's lever shows it, and the gap is to be counted.** Tried by hand in the viewer at d70db530, the 42112's white lever p384 and its catch turn together, but the drag stops at about 2 degrees, both ways, and the ring never slides (it needs about 5 degrees); the check turned the lever 1.5 rad and called function 4 working (decision 143). The check uses the collision pass only for steering's stops with --lock, and only reports those; everywhere else it turns a control through parts that the viewer stops at. So a function can pass the check and not show in the viewer, which is against the rule that the check passes only what the viewer shows; with decision 142 this is the second such gap found by trying a gain by hand. Ordered: the worker traces what stops the lever (a true contact in the set, a fault of the file, or a false stop of the near test); the solver owner, after the viewer's mend for the pin search, measures the gap over all sets without changing any result: for each function that works or is partly, how far each control goes in the viewer's way, with collision stops, against how far the check took it, and whether the listed parts still move. The orchestrator decides from that count whether the check applies the stops, and what happens to functions that then fail. Until then the 42112's function 4 is counted as working with this note; it is taken back if the lever cannot be made to show. Merged besides: the worker's retry of a failed download of a part (claude/fetch-retry). Corrected the same day: the lever is not stopped by a collision. The worker's first drags ran across the lever's path, with its upright axle nearly edge-on to the view, so 170 pixels gave 2 degrees; dragged along the tip's path the lever and catch turn together and the ring slides 10 LDU at the drag's end, and nothing blocks a turn of 17 degrees each way. So function 4 shows in the viewer and no function is known to fail by a collision stop. The measurement is still made, without urgency, since the check does leave the stops out. One fault was seen on the way back (the ring slides at -11 degrees and returns only at +9), which the worker traces in src/shift.ts, and one question is put to the solver owner: the check frees pins for a motor control, while the viewer's motor frees nothing. Corrected a second time, the same day, and this stands: the stop at 2 degrees is real. In the viewer a drag goes without collision stops until the collision pass is ready, a few seconds after loading, and the worker's test that found no stop ran before that. Once the pass is ready the lever stops at about 2 degrees every time, the ring against the catch: a false stop of the near test, since a catch's fork sits in its ring's groove by design and presses into the ring until the drag ends and the ring slides; connectedPairs skips meshing gears and other designed contacts but not a catch and its ring. So this was not the 42112's alone: in every set with a 6641, since the collision pass came in (decision 64), a lever that shifts a ring did not shift it in the viewer, while the check passed it. The mend (claude/catch-way-back): the viewer no longer tests a catch against its ring and the ring's riders; and findCatches keeps the fork a catch had, where it measured the fork afresh after each shift and so brought the ring back late or not at all (the 8258's catch stayed out). The 33 sets with a 6641 are checked by name on it before the merge. The measurement of the collision stops over all sets is urgent again, and the worker lists the other kinds of parts that sit inside one another by design.
145. **The hub driver is in: the 42083's drive reaches its differentials; no result changes yet.** claude/hub-driver 5f1267ed, which waited on the pin search (decisions 126 and 137): the hub 35189 has a ball socket at the inner end of its spindle (data/corrections.ldr, as the 92909 has), so a seated wheel spindle driver 32494 turns it at any angle. Only the 42083 and the 42115 have the pair. Checked by name on preview with play and the search: 0 better, 0 worse; the 42083's function 8 is as before. The 42083's function 4 (the W16) stays partly: both differentials now turn with the wheels, and the crankshafts hardly do, because the drive passes the 8-speed gearbox and the function's entry chooses no gear; that waits on the paddle stepper. By hand in the viewer the worker could not reach the suspension arm of function 8 through the bodywork, and saw that a drag on one front wheel turns it lifted and not at all on the ground, where the check turns all four wheels together; what a drag on one wheel of a car on the ground should do is put to the worker. Kept pins of five sets have new keys and the same pins. Gate: type check, 1,001 tests, the ratchet against f7ee6340 (0 better, 0 worse).
146. **The catch mend is in: a changeover catch brings its ring back, and the viewer no longer stops it against its own ring.** claude/catch-way-back dbeaca60 (decision 144's second correction). Two faults, both only in the viewer: (1) after a shift the viewer reads the model as built with the ring slid, and the catch's fork, measured afresh there, was off by the shift, so the ring came back only when the lever went as far past its middle; a catch now keeps the fork it had (`findCatches` in src/shift.ts takes the catches found before; the check passes them in too). (2) The viewer's collision stop tested a catch against the ring its fork sits in; the ring slides only when the drag ends, so the fork pressed into its groove and stopped the lever about 2 degrees out once the collision pass was ready. That pair is now left out, as connected parts are (src/main.ts). Proof for decision 96: the one changed line in scripts/check-functions.ts acts only where a model has a catch that keeps a fork (6641, 51149); 33 sets place one; 32 were checked by name on the merged code: 0 better, 0 worse, 737 work. The 33rd, the 8110, hit the 60-minute limit and keeps its stored result; a slow check of it on this code runs now. What the false stop hid: 56 functions on preview are checked through a 6641/51149 shift and 23 of them are counted as working; the viewer did not show those 23 until this mend (the check leaves the viewer's collision stops out, the open gap of decision 144). Tried by hand in the viewer by the orchestrator, with the collision pass ready, before the merge: the 42112's lever p384 (catch 51149) and the 42038's lever p84 (catch 6641) each turn 14 degrees both ways with no stop; the ring slides 10 LDU at 8 degrees and is back at 7, both ways; on the 42038 the knob p190 then turns with the ring at each side (one way from the built place it is at an end, the other way it turns a full radian). The worker tried the 42112's lever and the 8258's catch p171 on the branch. The worker's list of designed contacts and whether the viewer's collision stop leaves them out: left out are direct connections (slides, balls, turntables, racks in housings, pistons), parts that share a pin or axle, all gear pairs (worms and racks too), treads and rails, train couplings, and now a catch and its ring; a rotary catch 35188 against its ring is covered by the same line and worked both ways already (42107); a driving ring beside a clutch gear it is not engaged with is not left out, and gave no stop by hand on the 42038. Also from the worker: the viewer does roll a car on the ground from one wheel (42096: wheel p1686 turned half a radian rolls the car 43 LDU, the differential turns), but the 42083's front wheel p3516 turns 0, stopped by a part against itself (p3529, a friction pin in the right rear wheel's body); the cause is not found yet and is on the worker's list.
147. **The 77590 constant velocity ball is a grooved ball, as the 52731 is; no result changes.** claude/cv-77590 0a5bc275. The 77590 (Technic Steering Constant Velocity 8mm Joint Male with Sliding Axle Hole) is the newer form of the 52731 and sits in the same socket 52730. The shadow library gives its ball as 10 across in a group of its own, so it met no socket; data/corrections.ldr now gives it the 52731's ball (8 across, at its origin) with the shadow library's axle hole, and data/roles.json the role "grooved ball". This is a fact of the part (the same 8 mm joint), not a tuned number. Two sets place it (42129, 42131); both checked by name: 0 better, 0 worse, their kept pivots written afresh; the kept collision files keep their keys (the tests pass). In the 42129 the drive from the medium motor now reaches the rear rotary catch p1064 through the CV joint behind the second slip clutch (it turns 0.267 of a radian, as the centre one p517 does); function 4 stays partly (part-fixed at the rear ring p1030): within the check's turn neither catch reaches the angle that shifts its ring. Not shown by hand: the worker ran the motor p550 in the viewer and the whole truck rolled back and forth with the catches' angles unchanged, so the hand test settles nothing; since no result changes, nothing is claimed for the viewer.
148. **The slow check of the 8110 at f56ba77b: the roof gear's steering now works; 738 functions work.** The slow check ran on f56ba77b (the catch mend and the 77590 merged) in a temporary worktree, 21,835 s of work on the 8110. It settles decision 146's open point: no function of the 8110 changes by the catch mend. One function differs from the stored result of a16baaa3: function 1 (the gear on the cab roof steers the front wheels) goes from no (stopped at a dead point, nothing turned) to works: the roof gear p1504 turns 1.5 radians each way and every listed part follows (the hubs at half its rate, the rear wheels stay). The result says it moves on play (5.45), so the gain is from play and the pin search (decisions 138, 139), which came in after a16baaa3; the 8110 is checked only by the slow check, so it shows only now. The other 15 functions are as stored (8 work, 4 partly, 3 don't), so the 8110 now has 9 that work. The ratchet counts one thing worse: both of function 1's ways come back torn (18 and 24 LDU), where it had none because nothing moved; that is on the list of ways not back. Tried by hand in the viewer by the orchestrator, with the collision pass ready: the roof gear turned 8.6 degrees, both front hubs and their wheels turned 3.2 degrees about their steering pivots, a rear wheel stayed, and all came back to 0 on the way back, with no stop. But the drag is too slow to use: a step of 0.05 to 0.15 of a radian took 11 to 21 seconds (about 2.5 s for each degree), so by the mouse the page freezes. So the viewer shows the function, and the speed of a drag on play in a model of 4,039 parts is an open fault of its own (as the shock absorber pivots' slow drag is, see shock-pivots-slow-drag.md); it goes to the solver owner. main is not offered on this check: the gaps between the check and the viewer of decisions 142 and 144 are still open.
149. **The 35186 ring extension is read as the 32187 is; the 42043's function 11 list change goes to Erik; five traces from the worker.** claude/next-five 0f1c0535. (1) Merged: data/corrections.ldr gives the 35186 (Technic Transmission Driving Ring Extension with Eight Clutch Teeth, the 3L ring system's extension) a clutch face at its wide end (4 LDU deep, for a shifted ring) and clutch teeth at its narrow end (8 LDU, seated in the next clutch gear's face), measured from the LDraw shapes and the 42114's gearbox. So the 42114's p53 turns with gear p52. Five models read differently (42083, 42110, 42114, 42115, 42131), each checked by name: 0 better, 0 worse, 738 work; six kept pivots keys written again. Seen in the viewer by the orchestrator: the 42114 loads, its XL motor p427 runs, and p53 turns with p52. No function gains, so nothing more is claimed. (2) Not merged, for Erik (42043-fn11-list): function 11's list named the differential's case p2036 as moving while the drive shaft is held; the case stays and the two wheels turn opposite ways. The change stays on the branch until he signs; preview keeps the old list and result. (3) Traced, not mended, each with its next step: the 9397's function 2 (the list's first control is a pin in the catch's own body and its 20 was meant as a slide in LDU; the real selector, body 52, is locked through the small actuator's rod by two L-beams on three pins; book 3 decides which pin is wrong); the 42078's function 9 (the rack housing's strut is a locked triangle of three bodies on parallel pins; the bag 6 steps decide); the 42131's function 7 (the rod-end pins p4271 and p4197 are drawn turned 90 degrees, along the rod and not across its eye: an erratum to propose with the booklet step); the 42129's function 4 (each rotary catch needs 52 degrees, 3.4 radians of the control, and the check turns 1.5; decision 138's longer turn does not reach it because a ring moves only by a shift after the turn); the 42083's front wheel (not the collision stop: the start key, a separate assembly of 21 parts lying beside the car, is loose and rides on its nearest part, the right rear tyre, so turning a wheel swings the key into the ground). (4) Decided on these: the check's turn is not lengthened by hand for the 42129; instead the worker builds, as its own branch with decision 96's proof, that a listed ring whose rotary catch turns at a rate counts as a slow listed part and the turn's test applies the shifts. For the start key, first a count over all sets, changing nothing: how many loose parts or groups touch nothing of the model as built (by the first collision pass's pairs), and which; a loose assembly that touches nothing should lie still and not ride on its nearest part, but the rule touches every model, so the count comes first. (5) The orchestrator's own fault on this merge: it ran `npm run connections` in the preview worktree, which has no parts library, and that wrote an empty data/connections.json (191 tests failed); restored from git before the gate. That script runs only where `ldraw` is.
150. **The 9397's OMR file is the set's second model, the snowplough; its function list is rewritten for it. The 42131's erratum is held until the blade's lock is traced.** (1) The worker, looking for the 9397's outrigger in book 3, found that the file holds one model, "9397 - 9397B.ldr": a truck with a yellow blade in front, with no crane and no outrigger legs. The OMR's page for the set (library.ldraw.org/omr/sets/863) lists one model, "Snowplow", and the file's address in models/technic-sets.csv ends in 9397-1_Snowplow.mpd; the orchestrator checked both. Our list was written from the booklets, which build the logging truck, so functions 2 to 6 name parts the file does not have (function 5, the jib, counts as working by chance of what its listed part numbers are in this file; the earlier sign-off of the piston list for function 8 stands, the engine is shared). Decision: the list is rewritten for the snowplough, from LEGO's instructions for the second model, because the goal is the models the OMR publishes; no erratum can change which model a file is. The worker drafts the list; it is a function-list change, so Erik signs it; he is told now (9397-b-model on the decisions page, for his look). Erik agreed on 2026-11-17: "9397 - go with the snowplow". The lock traced before (two L-beams on three pins) is the snowplough's blade linkage and is judged against the second model's instructions. Seven other sets' files are named for one model of a set of several (8020, 8022, 8024, 8054, 8074, 8094, 42023); the worker checks that each list names only functions of the model in the file. (2) The 42131's proposed erratum (claude/42131-rod-pins d86cbc76: both blade lift actuators are drawn leaning, the rod 2.0 degrees in its cylinder and the cylinder 1.5 degrees on its holder, which a real actuator cannot do; the worker's earlier reading that the rod-end pins were turned was wrong, and it said so) is accepted as a proposal and held: no function's result changes with it, because the blade is locked on its support. It goes to Erik once that lock is traced, as one item with its whole effect. The 2 degree limit is not changed. (3) The 42078's function 9: the leg very likely swings by the big actuator through the crank b230; which hold is at fault (b231's, or the frame's grip on the crank's axle) is not settled; the worker goes on with steps 131 to 139 after the 9397.
151. **A loose part never stops a motion; whether a loose group on the ground stays there is decided after one more count.** The worker's count (loose-touching-nothing.md, preview 8e942091, 190 models, nothing changed): 1,938 loose groups (4,604 parts); 1,757 touch something as built (by the first collision pass's pairs); 181 groups (885 parts) in 44 sets touch nothing, 34 of them of three parts or more. Those are of two kinds that the count cannot tell apart: pieces that stand beside the model (the 42083's start key and bag, the 42082's house, the 42108's cargo, the 8062's goal, the 42115's signs, the 42094's log, the 42121's cones) and parts drawn a little off what they belong to (pneumatic hoses in the 42053, 42080, 8049 and 8854, the 42070's hook, the 42078's chain studs, cables). So the first intent, "a loose group that touches nothing lies still", would be right for the first kind and wrong for the second, and a distance to tell them apart would be a tuned number. Decided instead, by what a loose part is: its place against the mechanism is a guess (it rides on its nearest part only so that it is drawn somewhere), so a stop that comes from it is never evidence. Rule: a loose part is left out of the viewer's below-ground test and collision stop, and out of the same tests wherever the check makes them. That ends the harm (the 42083's front wheel turns 0 because the start key, riding on a rear tyre, swings into the ground) without choosing what the part rides on. What it does not mend is the look: the key still swings round with the tyre. For that, one more count first: of the 181 groups, which meet the ground plane the viewer derives from the tyres (a shape that meets, as for parts). If the pieces beside the model are the ones that meet the ground and the hoses are not, the second rule is "a loose group that touches nothing but the ground stays on the ground"; if not, the look is left as it is. The worker builds the first rule on a branch of its own, with decision 96's proof if the check changes, and tries the 42083's wheel by hand.
152. **The viewer works a motor part differently from the check: it pushes the motor's body, and rings do not shift while a motor runs. Both are mended in the viewer before any more gains by a motor are counted as shown.** Found by the orchestrator's hand test of the 42114's function 2 (the L motor p602 shifts the gearbox) and traced by the worker on preview, the collision pass ready. (1) The check works a motor control through its output: `motorOutput(mech, part)` gives the body the motor drives, and the check turns that body's joint (decision 132). The viewer's `startMotor` (src/interaction.ts) works the clicked part's own body; a motor's housing sits in a large body that does not turn like a knob, so the viewer falls through to a push and pushes that body: on the 42114 the whole truck travels back and forth (10.7 LDU and 2.5 degrees in half a second, reversing at each stop) and the catch turns 0 degrees against its frame. Run on the motor's output axle p406 instead, the catch turns 45 degrees in half a second and the truck stays. (2) Rings shift only when a drag ends (`meshAfresh`): with the output running, the catch passed its shift angle and the rings stayed until one drag end, on the 42114 and on the 42129. The check applies the shifts after its turn. (3) How far this reaches: 47 functions have a motor part as their control (19 work, 27 partly, 1 skipped; the 19: 42043 functions 1, 3 and 4; 42052 functions 1, 2, 3 and 5; 42054 function 1; 42109 function 3; 42113 function 1; 42124 function 1; 42129 function 5; 8110 functions 6, 7 and 10; 8258 functions 2 and 3; 9397 functions 1 and 5). For each, the viewer shows the function only if the user spins the motor's output axle, not the motor; a link with autospin on the motor part shows the model being pushed. The count of working functions is not changed for this: the mechanism is read right and the fault is in what a click on a motor does; but it is a gap between the check and the viewer of the kind decision 142 names, and main is not offered before it is mended. (4) Decided: in the viewer, to spin a part with the role motor (ctrl-click, autospin, the motor's controls) runs its output, by the same `motorOutput` the check uses, so the two cannot differ; a plain drag on a motor's housing still moves the body it is in. And while a motor runs, a ring shifts when its catch passes its shift angle, by the same step a drag end makes, done only when a ring's place changes (not every frame). The worker builds both on one branch, ahead of the rest of its list, and tries by hand: the 42114's L motor (rings slide, truck stays), the 42129's medium motor, the 42043's drive motor, and one older motor (the 8110's), each with the collision pass ready. (5) Noted, not settled: turning the 42114's catch by hand stops at about 100 degrees where the catch shaft's part p125 (49283) meets the frame's beam p41; whether that is the selector's real end of travel is open. The 42114's function 2 list change (claude/42114-fn2-list 966a449c: the extension p53 does not slide, booklet step 17) is right as a fact and is held until the motor shows the shift in the viewer.
153. **The loose-part rule of decision 151 is in: the 42083 rolls from a front wheel.** claude/loose-no-stop 1275bae6. `Collisions.ignored` (src/collisions.ts) holds the model's loose parts; the collision stop skips any pair with one, and the ground test skips them; src/main.ts sets it each time the model is read. What a loose part rides on is unchanged. Decision 96's proof: the check makes neither test in its usual run; only its `--lock` path (steering's stops, reported, deciding no result) builds the collision stop, and it sets the same field, so no function's result can change; the ratchet shows 738 work, 0 better, 0 worse, and all tests pass. Tried by hand in the viewer by the orchestrator on the merged code, the collision pass ready: the 42083's front wheel p3516 turns the 0.3 of a radian asked, the car rolls 30.6 LDU, nothing stops it (it turned 0 before, stopped by the start key's pin below the ground); the step took 6 s, which is the slow drag of a large model again. The worker tried the same, and the 42096, where a loose hub cap that touches its rim still rides with the wheel and the car rolls exactly as before. Still open from decision 151: the count of which loose groups stand on the ground, for the look.
154. **The 42078's outrigger legs are held by one friction pin each that should pivot; the 9397's snowplough instructions need Erik's yes to a download; the 42129's check rule is held for the viewer.** (1) 42078 function 9: the file is right. Each leg is a rack housing, its rack, a 9L beam and a 5L beam; the 9L turns on the crane base, the 5L on the end of a crank that the big actuator's rod drives, and the two beams are joined by one friction pin (p2071 in the rear leg, p2290 in the front). Read rigid, frame, crank, 5L and 9L are a triangle with no motion; pivoting, as step 138 builds it (book 1 page 332), they are a four-bar that swings the leg. With both pins free both housings move with the control. No erratum. It is the case of decisions 128 and 139 (a friction pin that holds a driven linkage still), and the pin search does not reach it; before any rule is changed the worker says exactly what the search tries for this function and why it stops, because the search is the solver owner's and a wider search can make functions work for the wrong reason. (2) The 9397's second-model instructions are two PDFs on lego.com (19.3 and 19.7 MB, sizes read from the server's headers, not downloaded). A session does not download without Erik's word; asked on the decisions page (9397-download). Until then the snowplough's list is not drafted. (3) claude/rotary-slow-ring bd75bda6 (a listed ring held by a rotary catch that turns counts as a slow part, and the longer turn's test applies the catch's shifts; only the 42129's function 4 changes, partly to works; all seven sets with a 35188 checked by name) is accepted and held until the viewer shifts rings while a motor runs (decision 152), so that the gain shows.
155. **The viewer finds the same pins as the check, but only 3 of the 8 gains show by hand; two more changes to the drag are built before a full check, and what still does not show then is taken back. The 43093's role stays out.** The solver owner is working again (Erik answered its request and showed the pane). (1) claude/viewer-pins e523e893 (from a926fab5, 995 tests): one test for each question in Kinematics, called by both the check and the viewer (`dragMoves`: whether a drag with its holds moves a part, exactly or on play; `hingeStuck`: whether a part a drag would push cannot start its push), and the viewer's `pinsForDrag` (src/pinSearch.ts) knows the grab point and whether the model is lifted. The check's results do not change. By hand, the pane shown: the pins agree with the check in 4 of the 6 cases pressed; shown in full: 42080 function 2; in part: 41999 function 7 (pins freed, the shell's hinge does not open); not shown: 42080 function 3, 8272 function 2, 42040 functions 6 and 7, 42036 function 5; not tried: 8272 function 3. (2) Two differences remain, and both are built on that branch: (a) a push: the check aims each push step along the hinge's own way, the viewer follows the pointer and rolls the whole model where that is nearer the pointer's way. Decided: a part that has a motion of its own against the model moves by that motion before the whole model moves (a hand on a door opens the door); the model rolls only when the part has none. (b) "Moves on play" where a play motion exists but no step can be taken (8272 function 2): both sides count that as no motion, so the search for pins goes on. (b) can change the check's results, so the branch gets a full check (decision 96), then the eight are dragged by hand again, the collision pass ready; any of them that does not show then is taken back out of the count at that merge. Until then the count keeps them, with this said (decision 142). (3) The 43093's friction role with the search in place (claude/solver-role43 8da5e788, 110 sets): 730 work where 736 did, 11 better, 20 worse. Still a loss, so it stays out, and the 32054's with it. Among the 20 worse are the 42078's functions 4, 6, 7 and 8; the worker has just traced the 42078's function 9 to a friction pin that holds a driven four-bar rigid while the control turns, which the search does not reach because the control does turn. On preview 36 functions are in that state by their stored results (the control turned, a listed part stayed, cause part-fixed). So a third change is to be designed, not yet built: (c) the search also runs for a listed part that stays still while the control turns. The solver owner says what (c) would try and where it must stop, and sorts the role's 20 worse by whether (c) would reach them; the roles are decided again after that.
156. **A loose group that touches nothing but the ground stays on the ground; the 42131's blade lift is set aside.** (1) The worker's ground column (loose-touching-nothing.md): of the 181 loose groups that touch nothing of their model, 31 (214 parts) meet the ground as built, by the viewer's own test for parts that touch the ground at the start (belowGround's NEAR, no new distance); 67 (540 parts) do not; 83 (131 parts) are in models with no ground. The groups of three parts or more that meet the ground are the pieces that stand beside a model: the 42083's start key, the 42082's house, the 42080's trunk and chainsaw, the 42115's sign, the 42121's cones, five parts of the 42070. No hose meets the ground: each hose group is drawn 22 to 65 LDU off its fittings (the fittings' points are in our data), so they are drawn apart, not misread. So the second rule of decision 151 is decided as foreseen: a loose group that touches nothing of the model and meets the ground stays where it stands (it rides on nothing that moves). The other loose groups ride on their nearest part as before (the 42083's bag, the 42108's cargo, the 42125's wheel arch among them), and by decision 153 none of them can stop a motion. It changes the look only; the worker builds it after the motor mend, with a hand test of the 42083 (the car rolls away, the key stays). (2) The 42131's function 7 (the blade lift) is set aside. The worker's corrections for the two lift actuators could not be made consistent part by part: the cylinders are drawn leaning 1.5 degrees against their own holders and inputs; leaning the input's gear with the cylinder loses its mesh with the bevel that drives it, and leaning the bevel's shaft too puts its two bearings 1.3 LDU apart. And the drive to the blade lift is locked in the file as drawn, in all four selector settings, before any correction. The worker's first erratum (d86cbc76) was wrong and its "blade lock" was that erratum's own doing; the worker said so. Nothing goes to Erik. The work is kept on claude/42131-rod-pins as work in progress with a note; the set is taken up again when the motor mend, the catch rule and the pin work are in, since a bulldozer with four functions through one gearbox will read differently then.
157. **The motor mend is in: a spun motor runs its output, and rings shift while a motor runs.** claude/viewer-motor 88ba3af7 (decision 152). `motorRunPart(mech, part)` in src/mechanism.ts, beside `motorOutput`, gives for a part with the role motor the part of its output's body that the motor's connection names; `startMotor` and `cycleMotor` in src/interaction.ts call it (ctrl-click and autospin both go through them); drags are untouched. `catchShiftDue` in src/shift.ts says after each motor step that turned something whether a catch now holds its ring at another place (0.1 ms); if so the motor makes the step a drag end makes and goes on, its holds worked out afresh. A step with a shift takes 2.0 to 2.7 s on the 42114 and the 42129 because the model is read afresh; that is the same cost a drag end has. No check code changes: 738 work, 0 better, 0 worse; 1,011 tests pass (4 new). All 47 functions with a motor control (48 motor controls) have an output that motorOutput finds. Tried by hand in the viewer by the orchestrator on the merged code, the collision pass ready, stepping the motor from the console: the 42114 opened with autospin=602 runs the output axle p406; over 160 steps the truck and the motor's housing stay where they are (0 LDU), ring p122 slides 10 LDU at step 25, rings p120 and p121 at step 34, and they go back at steps 98 and 107; the extension p53 does not move; no stop. The worker tried on its branch: the 42114 (five shifts in 157 steps), the 42129's medium motor p550 lifted (both rings shift while it runs), the 42043's function 1 motor p169 (the listed gears turn, the truck stays), the 8110's p60 (function 6's part turns, the truck stays; a step takes 6 s there, the slow solver on a large model, not this change). The stop at about 100 degrees on the 42114's catch is very likely the selector's real end stop (the motor turns back there, and all four gears and the tipping position are reached the free way); not counted against the booklet yet. With this in, the two held branches (claude/rotary-slow-ring, claude/42114-fn2-list) are checked again on preview and tried by their motors.
158. **The rotary catch's rule in the check is in: the 42129's differential locks work, 739 functions work. The 42114's function 2 list change goes to Erik.** (1) claude/rotary-slow-ring 17d6b050 (accepted in decision 154, held for the viewer): in scripts/check-functions.ts a listed ring that a rotary catch 35188 holds, left still, counts as a slow part when that catch turned against it at a rate during the turn, so decision 138's longer turn is tried; and the longer turn's test applies the rotary catches' shifts, so the ring counts as moved once its catch passes its shift angle, and the turn ends there. The plain turn of 1.5 radians and the cap are unchanged. 162. **Five more traces from the worker; two changes to the check's slow-part rule are ordered, two booklet look-ups, one case for the solver owner.** On preview a40e0ad3, nothing mended. Change: a still listed part that moves at a rate counts as slow under either cause. 42081 function 2: two knobs not worked together share the listed parts; each knob's way counts as done once it moves one part, so no control's own cause is too-little and the longer turn never runs, though the function's combined cause is too-little (p442 at 0.594 LDU a radian). Change: for a shared function the longer turn is tried when the combined cause is too-little. Neither changes the plain turn or the cap. (2) 8081 function 6 (tailgate) and 8880 function 10 (hatch), both control-jammed: not friction pins. The 8081's tailgate beam is joined to its surroundings only about vertical axes at several places and no hinge at the roof is read; the 8880's hatch is joined to the frame on three parallel lines across the car (hinge plates 2429/2430, half pins in 4263s, 3L pins in 2444 plates), and three lines cannot all be hinges, so one is a latch or a rest. (3) 42112 function 5: with the selector set for driving, the wheels turn the drum and every listed part moves, but the hand crank p1039, listed as staying, turns at 0.373 with the drive although as read it is tied to neither the wheels nor the drum. So the motion chosen moves a part that could stay: the stay-chosen cause, the solver owner's; added to its list.

166. **The drag's two changes hold in a full check; five of the eight pin-search gains show by hand and three go back; the wider search (c) is not built; two rules are ordered so that the check itself refuses what the viewer cannot show.** claude/viewer-pins 1e15cf06. (1) Full check of c927635e (a push goes by the part's own motions first; play counts only where a step can be taken): 738 work against 153e0bbe's 738, 0 better, 0 worse, 121 minutes. (2) The eight by hand on the branch with the motor mend merged in, the pane shown, collisions on. Shown: 8272 function 2 (lifted; the pin p20 freed; the control moves only on play, about 7 degrees a drag, three drags reach 13.7 of its 17 degrees: slowly); 8272 function 3 (the pin p24 freed, the control turns 0.3 radians to its limit and comes back, but only through the viewer's own search and turn: the control cannot be grabbed from the starting camera); 42040 functions 6 and 7 (p331 freed, the joystick tilts; shown as partly, as the check has them); 42036 function 5 (p257 freed, the handlebars turn 0.03 radians and stop; partly, as the check). Not shown: 41999 function 7 (the same four pins are freed, but every push of the shell is stopped by a collision, p1197 against p1334 one way and p53 against p1307 the other; with collisions off it swings about 20 degrees); 42080 functions 2 and 3 (the freed pin p348 joins the machine's two halves, so each knob then swings a whole half and the listed parts do not move against their knob). Those three go back to their stored results from before the pin search, works to no; preview then has 739 where it had 742. (3) The stored results of those three are set by hand, and the check's code would give them again. That cannot stand, so two rules are ordered. For the 42080's kind: a freed pin counts only if the control then moves against the body it sits in; a pin whose freeing only lets a larger body swing with the control in it is refused, in the shared function, so the viewer refuses it too. For the 41999's kind: the check does not make the viewer's collision stop; the measurement over all sets (decision 144's open item) comes next, and with it the question whether the check makes that stop always. (4) The wider search (c) of decision 155 is not built: on the 36 functions it gives one gain (42082 function 9, the cab door, held in the cab's body by its own two hinge pins) for 85% more time, and the viewer cannot know it from the control's drag, because that drag does move something (the cab turns). What the door's case shows is the same principle as the push: a hand on a door opens the door. Ordered as a design note first, no code: Shift-press on any part frees the friction pins that hold that part in its body (today only for frame parts), and the check's search for a control starts also when the control moves only together with its body; with the cost, since the door's search took 15.5 s. (5) Collision stops seen by hand, for the measurement: 41999 (1197/1334, 53/1307), 42040 (304/339, 304/328), 42080's knobs (1180/1947, 1398/1399), 42082's door (3725/3789). The slow drag: 5 to 8 s for a pointer drag on the 42080. (6) Pointer drags run only while the browser pane is shown; it was hidden twice and Erik had to show it. A headless browser run from the shell would need no screen; it is a download, so it is put to Erik (playwright on the decisions page).

172. **No friction pin is freed for a part that sits in the frame even with every pin free: the 42080's two knobs are "no" by the check's own code. The first drag scripts.** claude/pin-own-joint 4503ef3f (decision 166's first rule). (1) The rule, with no size or count: with every friction pin free, a part that is still in the frame can move only if a larger body with it inside swings on a freed pin, so it never moves against what it sits in; no pin is freed for it. `freeWithEveryPin` in src/inference/friction.ts, used by pinsToFree, by the viewer's pinsForDrag and by the check before its trial search for a control. Proof for decision 96: of the 73 stored functions that free pins, the control is in the frame with every pin free in three: the 42080's functions 2 and 3, and the 8448's function 6, which is "no" already; each of the seven real gains has its control in a body of its own there. Checked by name: 42080, 8448, 41999, 8272, 42040, 42036; 742 work, 0 better, 0 worse; 1,019 tests pass. The 42080's two results are no longer set by hand. The 41999's function 7 comes out "works" by the check again and is kept at "no" by hand until the collision-stop measurement decides (the shell is stopped after 2.4 degrees by 1197/1334, 53/1307 and 54/1307). (2) scripts/drags/: one script for each of the eight functions and a shared drag.ts. Each opens the viewer in the headless browser, finds its part by the viewer's own hover mark, presses and drags with the real mouse, and measures each listed part against the frame; it says yes if the pins freed are the check's and the viewer shows at least the check's result. The viewer's debug hook gives each part's matrix for that. Seven say yes (8272 functions 2 and 3; 42040 functions 6 and 7, the latter working in the viewer where the check has partly; 42036 function 5; the 42080's two, where a Shift-press says the part is held by more than friction pins and nothing is freed); the 41999's says no. The 8272's function 3 can be grabbed from the starting camera in a 1000 by 700 window; in the pane it was only out of sight. Run by the orchestrator against 5174 on the merged code: the 42080's function 2, yes in 22 s; the 8272's function 3, yes in 414 s (the viewer: works). (3) Cost: 19 to 52 s for a press, 2 to 7 minutes for a script that drags, the time going into slow play steps. So the drag scripts do not join the tests that run at every merge; they are run for the functions a change can touch, and all of them before main is offered. They need the viewer running.
173. **Shift-press on any part frees the friction pins that hold it in its body, in the viewer and, as a second try, in the check.** The solver owner's design note (shift-press-any-part.md), accepted for building; it replaces the wider search (c) of decision 155. Viewer: Shift-press on a part frees the pins that hold it in its body (pinsToFree on the model with every pin free), as for frame parts today; nothing is freed, and it says so, for a part that sits in the frame even with every pin free (decision 172) or that no friction pin holds. Check: the search for a control also starts when the control moves, but only together with a larger body that comes apart with every pin free; only where the function does not work as read; and the result with the pins freed is kept only if it is better, and then says which pins were freed and that it took the Shift-press. That is not shopping for a result: a user has two ways to work such a part, a plain drag and a Shift-press then a drag, and the function shows in the viewer if either shows it; the stored result names the way. On preview 06dfe180, of 1,468 controls that move as read, 81 move only with a larger body: 55 in working functions (where the larger body's motion is the function; untouched), and 26 controls of 23 functions in 21 sets that would be tried (the 42082's cab door, the 42115's function 10, the 42108's function 4 among them; the 41999's function 7 too, which stays "no" for its collision stops). Cost: for a Shift-press, one solve, one more for each friction pin that turns in a motion moving the part, then one rebuild and one test (15.5 s for the 42082's door on a 4,000-part model); in the check, each tried function is worked once more. Proof for decision 96: only those 23 functions can change; their 21 sets are checked by name. Each gain is then shown by a drag script (Shift-press, then drag) before it counts.
174. **Where a slow drag's time goes: the whole-model motion solves; the step's look ahead is to be kept across calls.** The solver owner timed viewer-like turns with every solver call counted (the machine loaded, so the shares matter, not the seconds). 8110 roof gear on play (661 bodies, 3,966 unknowns): each step two whole-model motion solves, exact first and then on play, about 85% of the step, loop closing the rest; the first step also tries and undoes three exact steps. 8110 motor: two motion solves a solver step, 97%. 42043 motor: motion solves 65%, loop closing 30%. 42083 wheel (exact, no play): loop closing 74% (about 23 equation builds a step), motion solves 25%. Collisions take none of it. One waste found: after each step `turnJoint` works out the motion for the next step, to see that the step did not leave it jammed, and keeps it as `ahead`; but the viewer turns one small step a call, so `ahead` is thrown away and worked out again by the next call. Ordered: keep it across calls where the joint, the way and every angle are unchanged (about −48% on the 8110's motor, −40% on its roof gear's play steps, −13% on the 42083's wheel), with a test that the poses are the same number for number with and without it, since the check uses the same code. That halves the cost and does not end it; a design note is asked for on the solve itself: what bounds it, and whether it can be made over only the part of the model a drag with its holds can move.

So the path where pins are freed could not be tried on any set; it is there for the day a friction role (the 32054's, the 43093's) puts a held pin in a motor's drive. 185. **A set whose track is not read as a track is never green; six sets are so, two of them were green.** Erik, 2026-11-18, with the 9391 Crawler Crane as the example: turning a track's sprocket moved only part of the track and tore it from the rest, yet the set was green; "any model without a properly working track should not be green". Cause: Kinebrick reads a track from the path it is drawn along (LDCad's or LSynth's); the links are then one loop that runs round its wheels (the 856 Bulldozer: two loops of 50 links). A track drawn link by link, each link a part placed by hand, has no path: the mechanism has no loop at all (the 9391: no loop, its 66 links in ten bodies or loose), so each link rides with whatever it touches. Only chains of the chain link 3711 placed by hand are found today (`chainPaths` in src/ldr.ts). Counted over every set: 30 sets have chain or track links; in six the track links are on no path: 42006 (68 links 57518), 42055 (66 of its 204), 42069 (108 links 88323), 8272 (27), 8275 (86), 9391 (66 links 3873). Two of them were green: the 9391 (its function 5, "the two tracks run round their sprockets", lists no part and passed) and the 8272 (its function 5 lists two links, which move because they ride along). The rule, built by the orchestrator: scripts/unread-tracks.ts finds a set's track links (LDraw's Technic Chain Tread parts) that are on no path; more than one makes a track that is not read. The function check stores it with a set's results as generated.unreadTracks (no result changes: decision 96's proof is that only a field is added), and the same script writes it into every set's file by itself. The status page and its history put such a set in the 75 to 99% colour at best, never in "all work", and the set's panel says why. Sets with every checked function working: 56, where 58 were counted. The count of working functions is not changed by this rule (742): the functions that pass wrongly are mended by reading the tracks, which is ordered from the worker as its next piece: a track placed link by link is found as a chain placed link by link is, each kind of link by its own pitch from its shape, and the track functions then list links so that a track that does not run cannot pass.
186. **The viewer's collision stop ends 694 ways of 330 working or partly working functions before the check's turn ends; the check is to make that stop, after the designed contacts are told apart by where the parts meet.** The solver owner's measurement (claude/collision-stops, report only, 228 minutes, 184 sets and the three with a removed part by name; every stop in collision-stops.csv in the notes folder; the 8110 and 9397 not in it). Where the stops come, as a share of the check's turn: 6 at 1% or less, 177 at 1 to 10%, 316 at 10 to 50%, 184 past half. 205 working functions have every stopped way stop before half the check's turn. By a first rule (the two parts' bodies share a joint and one of the parts makes that joint): 92 stops in 59 functions are between parts designed to touch (gears and differentials on their axles, bushes, changeover catches against their connectors, connecting rods), and 602 in 293 functions look real; but the rule is crude: 148 of the 602 are two structure parts on bodies that share a joint, which may be faces meeting at the hinge or a real end of travel, and a piston in its cylinder (2852 in 2850a, 12 stops) is counted as real because neither part has a role. Many of the real-looking stops are surely real ends of travel: the check turns 1.5 radians whatever a mechanism's own travel is. The cases named before: the 41999's function 7 stops at 0.01 of its turn at a pair that looks real; the 42040's functions 6 and 7 at 0.43 and 0.33; the 9396's knob at an axle pin passing a liftarm, which touches by design though the rule misses it. Decided, as the solver owner recommends: (1) the collision test gives where two parts meet, and a pair that meets at the joint between its two bodies is designed to touch and is left out of the stop, in the viewer and the check alike, as a catch and its ring are; the test of "at the joint" comes from the joint's place and the shapes, with no distance chosen; (2) the check then makes the viewer's stop, and a way that a remaining collision stops is an end of travel, judged as a limit is judged today; (3) that runs as a full check on a branch and the ratchet is read before anything is merged, since up to 205 functions could change. The check is not made to stop at collisions before (1), because a third of the working functions would be judged by a stop that is partly false. The joint full check of the three solver changes (decisions 173, 174 and the loop-closing stop) runs first.
187. **The 9397's plough: a proposed erratum makes its double parallelogram exact; for Erik, with two parts for function 1.** claude/9397-plough 835864a3: the erratum is merged unsigned (read only with `--proposed`; 742 work, 0 better, 0 worse); the list change for function 1 stays on the branch until signed. The plough lift is a double parallelogram (book 1 of the snowplough, steps 36 to 40, pages 73 to 77): a grey 5L link joins the middle holes of the two yellow arms, which pivot on one frame beam two holes apart, so the link is parallel to that beam. In the file the link p489 is tipped 0.09 degrees and its pin p490 is 0.10 LDU off the L-beam's hole. As read the linkage has no exact motion; the motor's turn then goes only on play, and the play motion slides two shafts along their axes into the ends of their slides, where the limit refuses the step after 0.07 of a radian (the general fault is with the solver owner: play spent against a slide's end). With the two entries the whole set, checked with `--proposed`: function 3 goes from partly to works with no way not back; the other five as stored. In the viewer by the orchestrator on the merged code, headless, the collision pass ready, the lever's joint turned 0.5 and the drag ended, the motor run six steps: with option=proposed the plough p1340 rises evenly, 0.21 LDU a step to 1.29, the tipping frame and the truck staying; as read it moves 0.4 LDU and the motor stops. The worker saw the same on its branch, and the plough come back. Function 1's list gets the two ring shafts p157 and p160 (6538a) as parts that move; it was signed with none. Sign-off item 9397-plough.
188. **Designed contact is told by whether two parts slide along each other or push into each other where they meet; the size of what the check's stop will change is about 120 functions.** Before step 2 of decision 186, from the solver owner. (1) The limit's test today: a way stopped at a limit is far enough if the control turned more than 0.001 (radian, or LDU for a slide), and it works if every listed part has moved at least 1 degree or 1 LDU and no part that should stay has moved. (2) An estimate from the measurement's file, no new run: of the 205 working functions whose every stopped way stops before half the check's turn, about 84 would stay "works" under that test and about 121 would not (a listed part counted as moved by the stop if its stored rate times the control's turn at the stop reaches 1 degree or 1 LDU; rates are averages over the whole turn, so it is an estimate). So once the check makes the viewer's stop, the count of working functions may fall by something like 120 before the false stops are taken out, and the true figure for what the viewer shows today is nearer 620 than 742. That is said to Erik as it is. (3) The test for a designed contact. "At the joint between the two bodies", as decision 186 had it, cannot pass an axle pin that passes a liftarm (their bodies share no joint) or a bush that rubs a beam's face far from the axis. Accepted instead, on the solver owner's proposal: where the two shapes meet (the triangles that intersect), compare the parts' relative motion in the step that brought them together with the meeting surface. If the motion lies in the surface, the parts slide along each other: a piston along its cylinder's wall, a bush turning on a face, a pin turning in the hole it passes; that is no stop. If the motion has a part along the surface's normal, one part pushes into the other: a stop. Every pair of triangles that meets must be sliding for the contact to count as sliding. The one bound comes from how LDraw draws a round, as a polygon of 16 sides (48 in the high-detail primitives): a face's normal is off the true radial line by at most half a side, 11.25 degrees, and "in the surface" means within that. It is the same test in the viewer and in the check. (4) Order: after the joint full check ends and is read, the three steps of decision 186 with this test, as a full check on a branch.
189. **The sliding test calls every named case a push, and rightly: the stops are true of the model as read, so they are sorted into work, not argued away.** The solver owner built the test of decision 188 (claude/meeting-stops) and traced the named cases. The connecting rod 2852 in the cylinder 2850a moves 24 LDU in the step at 22 degrees to the cylinder's wall: a rod tilts as its crank turns, and the LDraw shapes have no clearance. A bush meets a plate at its corner, moving into it. The 9396's axle pin p347 moves 0.83 LDU a step into the liftarm's face: in the real set it turns in that liftarm's hole, and as read it is not connected there, so it swings into the arm. A changeover catch's fork presses into its ring's groove wall while the ring waits. The 41999's shell pushes, as wanted. So what the orchestrator had called designed contact is mostly a missing connection in the reading, or shapes drawn with no clearance; a rule in the collision stop that let such pairs through would hide those faults. Decided: no rule that treats parts as designed to touch because they would be connected or nearly so. The sliding test is kept only if it takes out a real share of the 694 stops (the solver owner counts them); the catch's own line in src/main.ts stays. The check makes the viewer's stop as decided (step 2), run as a full check on a branch (step 3), and every stop that changes a result is sorted into four kinds with their owners: a real end of travel (nothing to mend); a missing or wrong connection (the worker's); shapes with no clearance where the real parts have some (a rule to decide: perhaps an overlap no deeper than the play of the joints that carry the parts, decision 138's clearances); and the rest, named. The merge is decided from that list.
190. **The loop stop comes out; the slow-drag mend and the Shift-press go in on a branch of their own, on a by-name recheck, not a second full check.** The joint full check (claude/joint-check 98cd921c: preview ad4038bb with claude/slow-drag, claude/loop-floor and claude/shift-press; 124 minutes) gives 744 functions working against 742, 2 better and 0 worse: the 42060's function 8 from no to works and the 42082's function 9 from partly to works, both by the Shift-press. Three functions change in a cause or a way back only (42020 function 2, 42079 function 2, 8274 function 2), all laid at slow-drag by running each alone with each change off. The 148 functions with any number different from preview were each run again with the loop stop off: 127 the same to the last digit, 14 different by rounding, 7 by more (42080 function 1, 42099 functions 1 and 3, 9394 function 3, 9396 function 2, 8257 function 7, 42037 function 1), with no result, cause or count of ways back changed. A stop that makes steps go differently is not a stop for rounding only, so claude/loop-floor is not merged; it is tried again after the ties of decision 191. Why no second full check (decision 96's second way): a function whose every stored number is the same as preview's with all three changes in is one where no change left a mark, and taking one change out cannot leave one; so only the 148 can differ without the loop stop, and those are checked by name on the branch that is merged (preview with slow-drag and shift-press only), their records stored from that run. The full check before `main` covers the rest once more. The 41999's function 7 stays 'no', held by hand, until decision 189's step 2 is in. Viewer times were not measurable under the machine's load (the same drag gave 382 s and 47 s); from Node, slow-drag alone: the 8110's motor step 57 s to 31 s, its roof gear 66 s to 58 s, the 42083's wheel 16.1 s to 14.8 s. They are measured again on a quiet machine, the motor by autospin and the wheel by a push drag; that does not hold the merge.
191. **Ties: every tier gets one answer, and the two new numbers are derived from the precision, not picked.** The solver owner's finding: rounding chooses in two places. At a cut-off (TIER_TOLERANCE, NOISE, RANK_TOLERANCE), a direction whose cost is near the threshold counts as free on one run and costly on another, and the motion jumps by a whole direction. In the sparse (friction) tier the least sum of sizes is often a set, and the reweighting's path picks a point of it. That is why a difference of 10⁻¹⁶ in a kept motion came out as 2×10⁻⁶ in play. Its proposal is taken: a gap test at the cut-offs (a direction is free only if the next eigenvalue up is clearly larger; with no gap the tier is solved as costly), and a small least-squares term in the sparse tier so its answer is unique and tying joints share the slip evenly. Not taken as written: 'about 100 times' and 'about 10⁻⁶' are picked numbers. Both come from the precision instead. The least-squares term moves the answer by about its own size ε and lets rounding through magnified by 1/ε, so the error is about ε + 2⁻⁵²/ε, least at ε = 2⁻²⁶ (the square root of the rounding unit, 1.5×10⁻⁸ of the tier's scale). The gap follows the same bound: the free directions turn under rounding by about rounding ÷ gap, so the gap asked is the one that keeps that turn at 2⁻²⁶. If the solver owner finds a reason a derived number is wrong for a tier, it says so with the measure. Built on a branch after decision 189's step 3; gate: a full check, every changed function named, and the kept motion against the motion worked out again on the 148 functions of decision 190 (the measure that it worked). Then the loop stop is tried again. Corrected the same day on the solver owner's measure: the gap at 2⁻²⁶ was in the wrong units for the quadratic tiers. The eigenvalues the gap is taken over are costs squared, so a gap g leaves free motions that cost up to √g (at 2⁻²⁶ that is 2⁻¹³, a real slip: the 8880's gearbox ratio 2.25 came out 2.251 and two tests failed). In cost units the error is √g + 2⁻⁵²/g, least at g = (2·2⁻⁵²)^(2/3) = 2⁻³⁴. With that gap all tests pass. The least-squares term stays at 2⁻²⁶: it and its rounding are measured on the same matrix. 2⁻³⁴ (5.8×10⁻¹¹) is close to the old TIER_TOLERANCE of 10⁻¹⁰, which was picked by hand, so the gap test moves the cut only where eigenvalues cluster across it.
192. **A slide at its end is a one-way stop, for play and for the main motion alike.** Today playMotion, jointMotion and the tiers know nothing of a slide's range; only atLimit does, after the step, and its only answer is to stop. So when the cheapest play pushes a slide that stands at its end, the step is refused though another motion would go (the 9397's plough). The solver owner's active set is taken: before the solve, list the slides standing at an end; if the motion pushes one outward, add that slide's along-axis rate row at rate 0 to the firm rows and solve again, until none is pushed or the joint cannot turn (a real end, stopped as now). The list is made afresh each step, so a slide pulled inward leaves its end freely. One rule for both, so play and the main motion never disagree on what an end is. Built on its own branch beside decision 191's, the two checked in one full check and laid apart by name as in decision 190, with the 9397 in the slow check.
193. **The slow-drag mend and the Shift-press are in: 743 functions work; the 42082's cab door is taken back, because the viewer does not show it.** claude/joint-merge e23344d8 merged (decision 190): preview 16384f09 with claude/slow-drag and claude/shift-press, without the loop stop. The solver owner's by-name run of the 148 functions and the two gains on the branch's own code (57 minutes) matches its stop-off runs number for number. Its remark on my reason for no second full check is recorded: stored numbers are rounded (rates to 3 figures), so 'every stored number equals preview's' holds to that precision only; a mark below it, cancelled exactly by the loop stop, could show without it. Neither of us expects it, and the full check before `main` covers it. Gate: type check clean, 1,025 tests, ratchet 744 with 2 better and 0 worse. By hand in the viewer (the solver owner's drag scripts, on the merged code on port 5174, the collision pass ready): (1) the 42060's function 8, the excavator's bucket: the Shift-press frees pin 293 and the bucket p294 turns 32°, then the collision of p167 with p294 stops it; the check has it turn 86° each way. It moves where it did not, and how far it goes is the collision question of decision 189, so it stays 'works' and is judged again in step 3. (2) the 42082's function 9, the cab door: the Shift-press frees the hinge pins 3792 and 3793, but the door p3789 does not move at all: collisions stop the first step (p3725 with p3789 and with the pin p3793, p3802 with p3838, p3687 with p3754). The check passes only what the viewer shows, so its record is put back to preview's 'partly', held by hand as the 41999's function 7 is, until the check stops where the viewer stops; the door's four pairs go into the sorting of step 3. So 743 work, 218 partly, 84 don't; 1 better (the 42060's bucket), 0 worse. Noted for the solver owner: its way of storing the by-name records dropped the counts' tallies ('not back', 'torn', 'moved whole', 'pivoted') in the set files it touched and wrote small numbers as 1.12e-07 where the check writes 1.12e-7. Nothing reads those tallies and the next full check writes the files afresh, so it does not hold the merge; records are to be stored by the check itself from now on. The drag scripts' default address is now port 5174 and scripts/drags/all.ts runs them all. The loop stop (claude/loop-floor) stays on GitHub for a second try after decision 191.
194. **The track mend is taken in two parts: reading a track placed link by link now, tracks newly on the ground after their faults are mended. A track function fails by the check's own rule when its track does not run.** claude/tracks-by-hand 2d68cc12 (decision 185's order; the worker). What it does: chainPaths reads each kind of link by its own pin, pitch and half-width (from the shadow library's snaps and the shapes), sprockets 57519, 57520 and 32007 by teeth and tooth depth measured from their shapes, and LoopPath takes two circles that touch. The 9391, 42006, 8275, 8272 and 42021 run every link round in the viewer (headless, lifted: 33 of 33, 34 of 34, 43 of 43, 27 of 27, 41 of 41), and five of the six flagged sets clear by themselves. Against 2164c3ac: 738 work (743), 6 works lost and 1 gained (the 8275's function 6), 23 sets checked by name (every set with a hand-placed link, a newly measured link or a crawler-tracks function). Decided: (1) The rule in the check is taken: a function tagged crawler-tracks fails with cause track-not-read unless a track is read round a wheel that its control or a listed part turns, and every track of the model is read round a wheel. It is Erik's order of decision 185 made code, and better than listing a link or two, which measures the link's carrier. Four works are lost by it and rightly: the 42038's function 9 and the 42042's functions 2 and 9 (their LDCad tracks are drawn off their wheels: file questions, proposed errata for Erik with the booklet pages), and the 8257's function 1. (2) One-piece rubber treads (71372c01 on the 8257, 680c01 on the 8419) get no exemption: the wheels turn and the tread stands, which is not a track that runs. Reading and drawing a one-piece tread as a loop is a later design question, the orchestrator's. (3) Not merged as it stands: the new depths (57518 and 88323 14, 69900 29.8, and the lookup by kind that gives 15379 the 3873's 8) put tracks on the ground that hung above it before, and two functions that work today stop working there: the 42038's steering (function 1: each wheel of a steered track unit is read as a tyre that cannot slip sideways, so two in line cannot pivot) and the 42042's slewing (function 3: the undercarriage turns under a still upper works, cause stay-chosen). Twelve more functions come back 1.5 to 9 LDU from where they started after rolling there and back (the 856, on an LDCad track, 0.18), and the two snowmobiles hardly go because nothing lets a ski slide. So part one, merged first: everything but the new depths and the lookup by kind. Part two, claude/tracks-on-ground: the depths, with the faults they show mended or laid at their owner. (4) The orchestrator's design for part two, to be tried: a track on the ground is firm along its run and slides across it at friction's cost, not forbidden, because every tracked vehicle turns by skidding; a ski slides along the ground at the same cost. Rolling there and back must end where it started: the cause of the 1.5 to 9 LDU is found before anything else, and no bound is changed for it. (5) The 42069's eight links left flagged are two closed squares of four links, decoration: a closed loop with fewer links than the smallest sprocket has teeth (6) can run round no wheel and is not counted as an unread track. (6) The frame part is taken from a reading without the hand-placed tracks, so the 9391's frame stays its undercarriage; that is a stopgap until the frame rule is mended. (7) For Erik, prepared by the worker as proposals: the two errata (42042 treads at z 60 and z 70, 42038 rear treads 9.96 LDU off their units) and the 8272's function 5 without its two listed links p166 and p180. Chain variants 14696, 273, 3249 and 53551 read link by link: a follow-up.
195. **Part one of the track mend is in: a track placed link by link runs round its wheels; 739 functions work, the four lost being track functions whose tracks do not run.** claude/tracks-by-hand 39841ef6 merged (decision 194), without the new depths and the lookup by kind. Gate: type check clean, 1,025 tests, ratchet against 1264c4f5: 739 work (743), 1 better, 5 worse. The worse: the 42038's function 9, the 42042's functions 2 and 9 and the 8257's function 1, works to partly by the rule (track-not-read); and the 9391's function 5, still works, but with 8 ways not back (2.1 LDU from its start after rolling there and back: part two's first question). The 42038's steering and the 42042's slewing work as before. No set is flagged for an unread track any more: the 42069's two squares of four links are not counted (a closed ring with fewer links than the fewest sprocket teeth, the 6 read from the sprockets' table). By hand in the viewer (the worker's scripts/drags/tracks.ts on the merged code on port 5174, a sprocket turned half a turn, with pictures looked at): the 9391 lifted and on the ground, the tracks whole round their wheels and the model rolled along the ground; the 42006, all 34 of 34 links went 85.7 to 109 LDU, neighbours' worst gap from a pitch 0.70 LDU as built and after, lifted and on the ground, but the frame went 0.0 LDU on the ground, because its links have no depth until part two. In 58 sets every checked function works, 27 of them in full. Erik's example, the 9391, is one of the 58 again, now with a track that runs.
196. **Three track proposals are for Erik: the 42042's left tread, the 42038's two rear treads with function 9's controls, and the 8272's function 5 without its links.** claude/track-files 0b7de4d9 (the worker; decision 194's point 7). 197. **Step 3's full check: with the check stopping where the viewer stops, 732 functions work where 744 did; the sliding test goes; the stops are sorted, and the next rule is an overlap that the joints' play can take up.** claude/meeting-stops c5efdee3 (3556d301 with claude/joint-merge e23344d8; 228 minutes, 3 jobs; every record stored by the check itself), against e23344d8: 732 work (744), 31 better and 23 worse; 56 functions differ in result, cause or ways back (works to no 7, works to partly 8, partly to no 6, partly to works 3, and 32 in ways back or cause only). (1) The estimate of decision 186 was far too dark: of its 205 functions, 192 still work and 10 don't (121 estimated). It counted a part as moved by its average rate; the check counts any point going 1 LDU or a turn of 1°. (2) The sliding test of decision 188 goes, as ordered in decision 189: it lets 4 of the 694 stops through (2 designed, 2 real) and does not pass the case it was built for (the 8868's rod in its cylinder). (3) The sorting, a first pass by where in the turn a pair stops and how many joints lie between the two bodies (note step3-collision-stop-sorting.md): (a) real ends, about 13 functions (the 41999's function 7, now 'no' by the check's own code, the 42063's steering lock, the 8081's suspension travel); (b) missing connections, the worker's: the 42000's functions 4 and 5 (axle driver 32494 in steering bearing 11950), the 8880's 7 and 8 (ball 2907 in steering arm 6540b), the 8466's 8 (bush or 32494 against shock rod 2910), and uncertain the 8448's 5 and the 42110's 4; (c) shapes with no clearance, the largest group, about 25 functions: pairs at a hinge or on a shared pin or axle that meet at the first step or within 0.1 radian; (d) the rest: the 42099 (its motion moves between runs, the ties), a flex hose taken as rigid, two friction pins on bodies no joints join. (4) The catch, recorded and not mended by a number: the limit's test (decision 188, point 1) is right for a real end, but it also passes a stop at 0.01 to 0.1 radian at a pair of kind (c), which is no end of travel: the 8002's functions 1 and 6, the 8840's 2, and as gains the 8274's 5 and the 42129's 6. No bound is set for it: which stops are real is told by the next rule, not by how far the control got. Until then a result that got better only at such a stop is held at its old result by hand, as gains not shown in the viewer always are, and the solver owner counts the working functions with a way stopped at a pair of kind (c), so the number that is provisional is known. (5) The next rule, the orchestrator's, to be measured before it is built: an overlap is no stop if a shift of one part by no more than the play of the joints that carry the two parts takes it up. The play is the sum of the clearances of decision 138 along the chain of joints between the two parts, so no new number; a pair that no chain joins gets none. The shift is measured against the shift the pair needs as built, because nominal shapes that touch can cross a little before anything moves. Measured with the crossing test the collisions already have: the smallest shift, along the contact's normals and the step's direction, that clears the crossing, by bisection; no convex pieces. First a measurement on the pairs of kinds (a) and (c): the shift each needs at its stop beside its chain's play. If kind (c) falls under and kind (a) over, the rule is built for viewer and check as one code and INSET (0.25) and the 1 LDU skip at load, both picked, are reviewed against it. (6) The merge of step 2 and 3 into preview waits for three things from the solver owner: preview a33f8f09 merged in with the sets the track mend touched rechecked by name; the 42082's function 9 squared with the viewer (the check has the door at 0.42 radian, the orchestrator's drag in the viewer had 0°); and the three results that got better (the 42040's function 7, the 8274's 5, the 42129's 6) tried by drag in the viewer.
198. **The check now stops where the viewer stops: 726 functions work where 739 did; the play rule of decision 197 is not built; the early stops are sorted by cause, one function at a time.** claude/meeting-stops 9c8fa0ee merged (steps 2 and 3 of decision 186, the sliding test taken out in d7aba1e6, preview e2a2b597 merged in and the 23 sets the track mend touched plus the six whose stops the sliding test let through checked again by name). Gate: type check, 1,025 tests; against e2a2b597 728 work (739), 32 better, 26 worse. Two results are held at partly by hand, so 726: the 42082's function 9 (the door goes 18 LDU sideways on two links and turns 0°; the check passes it by distance, the list says it swings on its hinge) and the 8274's function 5 (the orchestrator's drag on port 5174: no; the knob does not turn, the auger slides). The other two gains are shown in the viewer by drag on port 5174 and stand: the 42040's function 7 and the 42129's function 6. (1) The measurement ordered in decision 197 (note shift-measure-197.md; 13 functions of kind (a), 29 of kind (c), 78 pairs): the shift that clears a pair passes the play of its chain of joints within 0.05 radian more for 26 of 28 real ends and for 44 of 50 pairs of kind (c); only 3 pairs stay under their play over the rest of the way. The two kinds do not separate, so the rule would move a stop a little later and remove almost none. It is not built. No rule for 'the overlap stops growing' either: the few bounded pairs are 1 to 4 LDU deep, which no clearance explains, so a bound would be a picked number. (2) What the measurement shows instead: an early stop is nearly always the motion driving one part through another, so each has a cause with its own mend. Kind (c) of decision 197, 'shapes with no clearance', is withdrawn as a kind. The solver owner sorts every first stop under 0.1 radian at a pair joined directly or through one part (note early-stops-by-cause.md, 100 functions). Of the 31 that got worse or were of kind (c): 6 a missing or wrong connection (the 42000's functions 4 and 5, the 42110's 4, the 8880's 7 and 8, the 42082's door), the worker's; 2 a file fault (the 8868's functions 3 and 4: four of its six connecting rods are drawn 4 to 6.6 LDU off their pistons, so no ball joint is read and each rod swings into its cylinder; a proposed erratum, the worker's); 5 a wrong motion, the solver owner's (the 42062's function 3, the 42099's 1 and 3, the 42122's 3, the 8840's 2); 3 a real end at the start; 3 other; 12 not settled. Of the 69 working functions with such a stop: 46 are real ends at the start (doors, bonnets, lids and tippers built closed), 1 a wrong motion, 22 not settled, 15 of which stop both ways within 0.1 radian and work only by the limit's test. Those 15 are the provisional part of the count, not the 77 to 137 first counted. (3) INSET (0.25) and the 1 LDU skip at load stay as picked numbers, named so in ARCHITECTURE.md: the measurement gives nothing to put in their place. (4) Whether a part listed for a hinge must turn, and not only go 1 LDU, is left open: on the stored records too many listed parts of hinge functions are rightly judged by distance. The worker looks up the 42082's door in the booklet first. (5) The full check of the ties and the slide ends together (claude/ties-ends) runs on a base that holds this merge; a first start on a base without it was stopped before it stored a record.

The worker's first batch on the missing connections sorts five cases.** claude/missing-connections 8c1d2ba3 merged unsigned (read only with `--proposed`; no result changes on preview: 726 work, 0 better, 0 worse; type check, 1,025 tests, pivots as kept). (1) The 42000's function 5: the rear axle drivers p351 and p356 (32494) are drawn 5 LDU short of the socket of their portal hubs (92909); in the seven other sets with this pair (42029, 42037, 42039, 42056, 42077, 42096, 42125) every ball is in the socket, so the part data is right and the file is not. In the viewer on port 5174 with option=proposed, by the orchestrator's drag: the rear arm p278 turns 5.3° (0.7° as drawn, stopped at the driver), the half shaft p355 swings with it, the hub p358 rises 14.7 LDU and does not turn, and the stop is 464/466, the shock absorber's rod at the end of its cylinder. The orchestrator's rule, for the solver owner after its full check, branch claude/plunging-shafts: the ball at the wheel holds the shaft's place; at the universal joint the shaft's line goes through the joint's centre and the shaft may slide along itself, no further than the depth of the joint's axle hole from where it is built. The length change the 8880 and the 8466 need is measured first; more than the hole's depth is a file question. (6) The worker's headless drags died in the middle of a turn: its own 25-minute limit closed the browser, with a quarter turn taking 95 seconds on the loaded machine. The orchestrator's drag of the 8880's arm on port 5174 showed no crash and flat memory. The drag scripts for this branch now take one control and run without a limit.
