202. **The day's sorting of the early stops, and three orders from it: a contact may guide a motion (measured first), the check gets the machine's memory, and the ties and the slide ends are not merged as they are.** (1) claude/check-heap 7c149d81 merged (scripts/check-functions.ts only; type check, 1,025 tests, ratchet 0 and 0; the 42082 and the 8848 by name give preview's records, the 855-1 by name runs one set). Cause: every full check ran with a 10 GB heap set by hand, a run by name got V8's 4 GB, and the 42082's function 9 takes 7.6 GB with the collision stop on (0.5 GB with it off). A set whose process fails now keeps its file and the run ends with 1; each process gets the machine's memory as its limit; a named set is matched by its whole name. Why the collision stop takes 7 GB there is to be traced after the ties' gate. (2) The 42078's function 7: the check and its kept collision data are right (the stored pair numbers are the viewer's part numbers; a kept file whose key does not match is not used). The trailer's pin p1886 swings 32 LDU in the first step into p2454 of the container, whose 241 parts are read in body 0 with the tractor through one false rigid connection (p2619 32184 to p180 15573). The worker's: the container rides on the trailer. The 42124's p318 and p265 are 2 LDU apart as built, so rightly watched. (3) Contact as a guide, the orchestrator's rule, to be measured before it is built: a pair that would cross is first a one-way constraint for that step (the two parts may not come closer at the contact, along its normal), as a slide at its end is in decision 192; if the control still moves, the motion goes on, guided; if not, that is the stop, as today. No picked number. The cases that led to it: the 42021's function 1 (the coupler 32184 on one pin, as the booklet builds it, guided by the frame's face), the 42124's function 1 (the same shape), the 8272's function 1 (the file's column is as the booklet builds it; the arm hangs on one pin and a blue axle pin's bush most likely holds it by contact; note 8272-steering-arm.md), the 42114's function 7 (the hood meets a part beside its own hinge; the booklet's pin joins nothing in the file) and the 8283's function 3 (the inner boom slides between the outer boom's beams; it also needs a pin freed between crank and boom). The solver owner measures it on the rows of early-stops-by-cause.md and the 13 real ends: guided, real end, or neither. Built only if the wrongly stopped rows fall under guided and the real ends stay stopped. (4) The full check of claude/ties-ends aa035bf5 (ties of decision 191 with slide ends of decision 192, on a base that holds the collision stop; 213 minutes): 718 work against 728, 0 better, 17 worse (nine suspension functions from works to partly: 42036 fn2, 42037 fn4, 42039 fn5, 42124 fn4 and fn6, 8261 fn2, 8272 fn2 and fn3, 8448 fn5, 9392 fn5; eight with a way not back). Neither branch is merged as it is. The solver owner tells the two apart on the 17. (5) The 42082's function 9, by the orchestrator's drag on port 5174 with the distance kept: after the Shift-press frees p3792 and p3793 the door goes out 18.4 LDU at 0° and stops at 3802/3838; the other way 1.1 LDU to 3725/3789. That is the check's record (0.42 radian of the link, 44.7 LDU per radian). With the worker's list draft (claude/42082-door 65c4b8f9: slides open on two parallel links) the function is what the viewer shows; sign-off item 42082-door. Until Erik signs it stays held at partly. The stop at 3802/3838 is a rub that stays within 1.7 LDU (the shift measurement), so the real door most likely goes further. (6) The 42000's function 4: the worker proposes an erratum for the front rockers' angle (a rigid 160 LDU pushrod between balls 154.4 apart; the rocker turned 15° closes it), on the condition that the shock absorber is inside its travel at that pose.
203. **The 42000's front pushrods: a proposed erratum turns both front rockers 8.9° so each rigid pushrod reaches both its balls; for Erik with the rear drivers. A loose group may ride on another loose group.** (1) claude/missing-connections 3ca553e3 merged unsigned (read only with `--proposed`; 726 work, 0 better, 0 worse; type check, 1,025 tests, pivots as kept). The file draws a pose its parts cannot take: each front pushrod (32293, 160 LDU between its sockets) lies between balls drawn 154.4 LDU apart, so one socket is joined and the other is 6.8 LDU off its ball. The wishbone and its towball are on the grid; the rocker, which turns on its upright axle (j125, along y) and carries the shock absorber, is drawn at a chosen angle. Turned 8.9° about that axle (mirrored on the right) its ball lies 160 LDU from the towball. The shock absorber (4254 in 4255, travel 90 to 110) goes from 100 as drawn to 93.8, inside its travel; the rear shocks are drawn at 100. 20 lines of 42000.L3B.ldr move, 10 a side: the rocker's 8 parts, the pushrod, the shock's cylinder. The booklet (book 2 pages 15 and 16) shows the rods on both balls and cannot show the angle; the ground is the geometry. Which of two poses is right (the rocker turned, or the wishbone dropped 13°) the booklet does not tell: this one keeps the wishbones level as the file draws them. With all 22 entries the check has functions 4 and 5 at works (function 4 turns 0.07 radian each way to the shock's end and to the cylinder against the frame's T-beam, 499/566). In the viewer on port 5174 with option=proposed, by the orchestrator's drag: the left arm p591 turns 4.0° (5.4° at most), the pushrod p571 4.6° and 4.9 LDU, the rocker p559 7.0°, the shock's cylinder p566 4.9 LDU; stops 565/566 (the rod at the cylinder's end) and 499/566. The sign-off item 42000-drivers now covers all 22 entries. (2) The 42078's container: it stands on the trailer and nothing pins it there (the booklet's page 421; its pins lie 10 LDU above the trailer's holes). The reading's ride-along lets a loose group ride only on a part that is not loose; the trailer is itself loose at that stage, so the container went to the tractor's bumper p180, 290 LDU away, with the trailer's p1872 14 LDU under it. The orchestrator's rule, the worker's to build on claude/ride-on-loose: a loose group rides on the nearest part outside its own group, in the main group or in another loose group, in an order grown from the main group outward (at each turn the loose group whose nearest anchor is nearest of all is welded, and its parts become anchors). No picked number; the same-submodel preference and single loose parts stay as written. Proof by a comparison of every set's welds before and after; the worker's scan expects the 42078, the 42024 and the 42106 (stop above about ten sets); those checked by name, with drags.

One thing worse beside them: the 42000's function 5 works but two of its ways come back off their start (0.13 to 1.24), where the check with the rear drivers alone had none; it turns 0.07 to 0.1 radian each way to the shock's ends. (4) GitHub's CI, found red by Erik the same day: since main moved on 2026-11-16 a test on main read omr/ files, which a checkout on GitHub does not have, so main's workflow failed before each deploy and none of the three sites was published after 2026-11-15. 205. **A second model rides on the second model it stands on: the 42078's trailer swings with its container; 737 functions work.** claude/ride-on-loose 6af162e6 merged. The rule, after two wider versions were tried and set aside: only a carried group (a second model, mech.carried) may ride on another carried group, welded in an order grown from the main group outward (at each turn the one whose nearest anchor is nearest of all; its parts then become anchors). Every other loose group and every single loose part rides as before, on the main group only. Between second models 'the same submodel' is judged by the copy, not by the name, so two containers of one design are two models (the 42062's standing container rode on the held one and three functions got worse until this). The reason for the line: a second model is a model in its own right that the file's author placed on another, so what it stands on is known to carry; a loose group inside one model is most often a reading gap. The wider rules stay in the branch's history (df978eec: any loose thing on any nearer loose thing, bodies differ in 33 sets; adc91b2f: groups of more than one part, 19 sets), with their lists in the note ride-on-loose-bodies.md, for the day the loose groups are looked at set by set; there the 42024 and the 42106 are decided by the same-submodel preference, not by this rule. Proof by decision 96: the partition of parts into bodies compared over all 187 sets, one set differs, the 42078 (243 parts leave the frame for the trailer); the 42078 and the 42062 checked by name. Gate: type check, 1,024 tests (5 new in tests/ride-along.test.ts, which read no file), ratchet 738 against 736, pivots as kept; the kept collision file's key does not depend on bodies. The 42078's function 7 (the trailer on its king pin) goes from no to works: ±0.6 radian to 186/1680 and 185/1685, the tractor's rear bumper corners against the trailer's front, a jackknife limit and a real end. In the viewer on port 5174, by the orchestrator's run of scripts/drags/42078-trailer.ts: the trailer turns 11.97° against the tractor and the container 11.97° with it, no stops. The 42078's function 12 (the container's doors) reads works in the check and is held at no by hand: the door does not open in the viewer; dragging it swings the trailer, and the list names no part to measure. The booklet (page 418, step 69) hangs each door on an upright hinge line and the file has the hinge parts there, but the reading fuses the door to the trailer's body. The worker drafts a list change (the door against the container) and finds what holds the door, on claude/42078-door-list.
206. **A slide is read only where it is asked for: the 42078's lock bars. The ties are merged if their gate passes; the slide ends are mended first and wait for the contact measurement.** (1) The 42078's function 12 (the container's doors). The file has a fault: door 2 is door 1 mirrored, where the booklet builds it without the 13L beam and the blue pins (page 409 step 43 against page 415 steps 58 and 59), so door 2's beam p2672 lies in door 1's and its pins p2669 and p2670 tie the two doors; a proposed erratum removes the three parts and adds two black pins (claude/42078-door-list 59f041ed, with the list change: both doors as controls, each measured against the container). The doors still do not open: each has a lock bar whose pin stands 16 LDU deep in the container, as the booklet builds it (page 418, step 69), and the bar must be lifted first. With the two pins out, as a trial, each door swings 12.7° to crane 2's beams. (2) The worker's measure of a general slide (note sliding-axles.md): 196 groups of parts in 68 sets could slide more than 1 LDU along an axle in round holes, 127 more than 10 LDU; of ten looked at, four are play of a hole's width, one decoration, three stopped off the line, two file questions, none a latch. So no general slide is read. (3) The rule, the worker's line taken: a slide is read only for a part that a function's `first` step names or that the user names in the viewer (a Shift-press on it, then a drag along its line). The part, with all that is fixed to it, must be carried only by axles or pins in round holes on one line; a grip (45590) or a bush on that line holds by friction and does not tie it. It slides until its shapes meet another part's, by the collision stop; no travel is stored and no number is picked. The list's `first` gets a third kind: a named part slid along its line by a distance in LDU. The check does it with the viewer's own code. No set's reading changes until a list or a user asks, which is the proof by decision 96. Built by the worker on claude/slide-on-demand, with tests on a model made in the test; the 42078's door then goes to Erik as one item (the erratum for door 2 and function 12 with its lift step). The 42114's bumper pin (pulled before the hood tilts, page 438) is the next case; a rubber grip holds it. The chain's studs read as rigid to the parts they pass through are noted as a fault of their own. (4) The solver owner's telling apart of the 17 worse results of claude/ties-ends: all 17 come from the slide ends alone, none from the ties. Nine went from works to partly with the control turning exactly as far as before: the step that pushed a shock past its end used to be refused and named 'limit'; with the slide held, the turn ended as 'no motion', 'stuck' or 'dead point', which the check does not count as an end of travel. Eight went on where they used to stop and did not come back. Its trial mend (a step whose own motion pushes a slide past its end is an end of travel, named 'limit'; the hold is for slides that play pushes; when the held slides leave no motion but one exists without them, that is an end of travel too) gives back 14 of the 17 and keeps the 9397's gain. Decided: claude/ties is merged as it is if its gate passes (kept against afresh on the 148). claude/slide-ends is mended so, run by name on the 17 and the 9397; the 42055's function 7 and the 42108's function 5 (a way that stopped at once now turns 2.5 to 3.1 radian and does not come back) are dragged in the viewer before anything is accepted. No full check of the slide ends yet: with the 9397's plough mended by its signed erratum they bring no gain of their own, and their worth is as the ground for the contact rule of decision 202; one full check for both together if the contact measurement says to build it.
207. **The ties are in: no result changes, a picked number is gone; they did not make the motion less dependent on what was kept.** claude/ties cd484fec merged (decision 191: the gap test at the tiers' cut-offs with the derived tolerance 2⁻³⁴, the least-norm term 2⁻²⁶ in the sparse tier, the reweighted rounds' picked cut at 10⁻¹² gone; src/linalg.ts and its test). The solver owner's gate, 150 functions each run kept and afresh (300 runs, about 3.8 hours): the result is the same kept and afresh in all 150; the kept motion equals preview's record in 149; the one that differs is the 42066's function 2, partly on both, whose − way turns 1.92 radian and comes back 298 LDU off where preview has 1.81 and 221. The full check of claude/ties-ends had no change from the ties either (all 17 were the slide ends'). By hand, the orchestrator's nine drag scripts on port 5174 with the merge in the folder: all yes (42036-fn5, 42040-fn6, 42040-fn7, 42078-trailer, 42080-fn2, 42080-fn3, 8272-fn2, 8272-fn3, 8868-rods). What the gate did not show: kept and afresh still differ in turn or back in 30 of the 150, 13 of them by more than 1 LDU or 1°, and on the four functions measured before the ties the differences are the same number for number. So the motion depends on what was kept as much as before. In some of the 13 the difference is the function itself (the 42075's function 2 stops at 0.3 radian kept and turns the full 1.5 afresh; the 42096's 1 0.5 against 0.87; the 42077's 2 comes back afresh and not kept; the 42029's 3 the other way). The loop stop (claude/loop-floor) is not retried until that is known. Also recorded: the 42000's function 5 comes back off on preview because the car stands on its wheels for it and settles whole by under 1 LDU, with a free drive train left turned 1.24°; nothing is torn (every part within BACK against the frame). Neither a 'lifted' mark nor a discount for geared bodies is made for one case; counted again if more functions show the same.
208. **A linear actuator at its end is a limit; every other slide at its end stays held. No second worker this week.** (1) The mended slide ends (claude/slide-ends 1d169fb5, with the ties) match 9c8fa0ee in 15 of the 17 functions tried by name, and the 9397's function 3 comes back as on preview (its fault was the hold of slides the motion does not move, now gone). The two that differ, the 42055's function 7 (− way) and the 42108's function 5 (+ way), start with a linear actuator's piston at the end of its travel and the control turned the way that pushes it on. With the piston held, the solver finds another motion: in the 42055 the crank turns 3.1 radian while the boom moves 0.006 LDU and the actuator's input comes back 12° off; in the 42108 the boom rises 1.86° with the actuator's length held and a part comes back turned 101°. A real actuator slips its clutch there and nothing else moves; the solver has no such clutch, so these motions are ones the real model cannot make, and the viewer does not show the 42055's. The rule: a slide at its end that the motion pushes on is held and the motion chosen again (decision 192, kept); but where that slide is a screw's own slide and the motion turns the screw's input, the step is refused as a limit. The test is the slide's kind, a part's role, so no number is picked: the strict form of rule (a) for all slides needed a threshold (it broke the 42056's function 6 on tiny rates) and is not built. Both functions stay works by their other way. If a set shows one drive working two actuators of which one is at its end, the slip itself is modelled then (the screw's coupling let go while its slide is held); none is known. The solver owner builds it, runs the 17 again, and says what carried the two motions. The full check waits for the contact measurement, as decision 206 says. (2) The second worker, due to be asked again today: not put to Erik. The account's week stands at 48% used with 39% of the week gone (2 days 18 hours of 7; it resets on 2026-11-23), so the three sessions alone are on course to reach the limit about a day early; a fourth would bring that forward. Working functions went from 701 to 737 in the three days since 2026-11-16, 12 a day. The question is looked at again after the reset, with a week's usage measured; until then the sessions are told to keep turns few, and if the week reaches 80% by 2026-11-21 the worker pauses first.
209. **Slides on demand are in; the 42078's doors go to Erik as one item.** claude/slide-on-demand 8baf6222 merged (decision 206: src/inference/slideOnDemand.ts, src/slide.ts, the check's `first` step with `slide`, the viewer's Shift-press and `?slide=`). A slide is read only for a part a function's `first` step or the user names; the part, with all fixed to it, must be carried only by smooth axles in round holes on parallel lines; a grip or bush that also holds something else holds by friction and does not tie it; it slides until its shapes meet another part's. One change of mine in the merge: the new code's own 2° test for parallel lines uses the shared ANGLE_TOLERANCE, the same value, so no second copy of the number exists. Gate: type check; 1,038 tests, and none fail in a bare checkout; pivots as kept. Proof by decision 96: the worker read all 187 sets with no slide asked and found bodies, 22,397 joints and collision pairs identical to preview 58c50b2f; no list on preview has a slide step, so no record can change; four sets with `first` steps of the other kinds (42006, 42024, 8063, 952) checked by name on the merged code give records identical to those stored. By hand in the viewer on port 5174 (scripts/drags/42078-door.ts, with the proposed erratum): each lock bar, pressed with Shift and dragged, lifts 20 LDU until its connector meets the door's pin; each door, pressed with Shift, then swings 12.7° and 12.8° outward to crane 2's beams and about 1° inward to the container's top. The erratum for door 2 (five entries: the 13L beam and two blue pins out, two black pins in) is merged unsigned as a proposal; I read pages 409, 415 and 418 of the booklet myself and they bear it out. Function 12's list change (both doors as controls, each lock bar lifted first) stays on claude/42078-door-list 1d1d764d until Erik signs; with both signed the function goes from no to works. The doors open only 12.7° with the container on the trailer, because crane 2 stands 40 LDU in front of them; the function's words stay as they are, since the booklet's functions pages (420 and 421) do not show the doors open at all and a small swing is still the door's own motion. The 42114's bumper pin slides out 80 LDU when asked; its hood stays not driven for other reasons. No result changes: 737 work.
210. **The 8110's function 8: the first steps are wrong, and the booklet says so; the red knob is looked for before the pump's dead point.** The worker found that functions 7 and 8 have the same first steps (p512 and p516 both by +0.4), which drive the winch, not the crane; with p516 by −0.4 the drive reaches the crane, and the motor then stops after 0.43 radian at the compressor crank's dead point. I read the functions page myself (printed book 5, page 43, https://www.lego.com/cdn/product-assets/product.bi.core.pdf/4660503.pdf#page=43): yellow lever up and red lever down works the winch; both up slews the crane; yellow down with the red knob on the side pushed out works the pneumatics; yellow down with the knob in works a fourth function. So functions 7 and 8 differ only by the red lever, p516 is the red lever's catch and p512 the yellow lever's (function 7's note has them the wrong way round), and the worker's setting is the booklet's. Decided: (a) the worker prepares the list change on claude/8110-shaft for Erik, with the signs proved from the file (which lever each catch carries, which way +0.4 moves its tip), not from which setting works; (b) it finds the red knob, where it stands as built and what it slides, since by the page the pump is not driven in the crane's setting, and a `first` slide step (decision 209) can set the knob for function 9 and the fourth output; (c) the crank's dead point where its throw equals the pump's stroke waits for (b): if the pump is rightly out of the crane's drive it does not matter for function 8, and for function 9 the worker tries it once on claude/slide-ends, which holds a slide at its end only when the motion pushes it out. A fault found on the way: the 8110's booklet files are not in printed order (8110-1-4.pdf is printed book 5; 8110-1-5.pdf is printed book 2), so a link made from the book's number goes to the wrong file; the worker reads all five footers and reports the order.
211. **The slide ends get their full check now, apart from the contact rule; the contact rule is measured once more as a true one-way constraint.** The solver owner measured the contact guide (note contact-guide-measure.md): 108 functions by name with and without a scratch guide that holds a contact as an equality row once two parts cross (the step undone, the contact held, the step taken again). Guided / neither / still blocked: the 48 real ends 1 / 3 / 44; the 13 of kind (a) 3 / 1 / 9; the 33 not settled 11 / 2 / 20; wrong motion 4 / 1 / 1; connections 1 / 3 / 1. Results: 84 stay works, 10 better (3 no to works, 4 partly to works, 3 no to partly), 5 worse (3 only by the scratch code's naming of the stop; the 8002's function 6 guided the wrong way; the 8002's function 3). So real ends mostly stay ends, which is what the rule must not break, but the gain is modest and the equality hold is not the rule: a contact held as an equality cannot separate, and it pulled one function the wrong way. Decided: (1) the contact rule is not built on this measurement. It is measured once more with the release test (a held contact the motion pulls apart is let go), on the code with decision 208, and is built only if the real ends stay ends, nothing gets worse without a traced cause, and the gains are at least the equality run's 7 to works; the cap of 12 held contacts is a picked number, so the run reports whether it ever decides a result. (2) The slide ends (claude/slide-ends, 17 of 17 by name with decision 208) no longer wait for it, as decision 206 had them: preview is merged in and their full check runs now, in the solver owner's folder; gains are dragged in the viewer and losses traced before the merge. (3) The plunging shafts follow.
212. **Decision 210's reading of the 8110's levers was wrong; the gearbox is traced in all four settings before any list changes.** The worker proved from the file which catch carries which lever: p512 carries the red lever (p559, p561) and p516 the yellow (p556, p558), so function 7's note was right and my deduction in decision 210 (p516 the red lever's catch) was wrong. With LDraw's y pointing down, +0.4 on p512 moves the red handle's tip down and +0.4 on p516 moves the yellow handle's tip up. Against page 43: the winch (yellow up, red down) is p512 +0.4 and p516 +0.4, function 7's step, which works; the crane (both up) is p512 −0.4 and p516 +0.4, and there the check finds nothing driven; the setting that did reach the crane (p512 +0.4, p516 −0.4) is both levers down, which the booklet does not use for the crane. So the model as read disagrees with the booklet, and the fault is in the file or in the reading of the gearbox, not in the list alone. Function 9's step also moves the wrong lever (the red one up, where the page wants the yellow one down). Decided: no list change yet. The worker traces the gearbox in the four settings (which ring locks which clutch gear, which outputs turn, against the page), finds where the drive stops in the crane's setting, and finds the red knob in the same pass; then one proposed erratum or one mend of the reading, and one list change for functions 7 to 9 and the fourth output, for Erik. The 8110's files in printed order: 8110-1-1.pdf is book 1, 8110-1-5.pdf book 2, 8110-1-2.pdf book 3, 8110-1-3.pdf book 4, 8110-1-4.pdf book 5; the worker records it with the set.
213. **The 8110's crane works with the booklet's lever setting; the fault was the list's first steps. One item for Erik after a drag in the viewer.** The worker's trace of the four settings (claude/8110-shaft cc5a0c6e, read with its two proposed errata for the crane's shaft): the red lever's ring p320 locks p325 when down (the winch) and p321 when up (the crane); the yellow lever's ring p330 locks p332 when up (the red lever's line) and p336 when down (the compressor's crank p422). Red down and yellow up drives the winch; both up drives the crane (the check by name gives works: p767 and the worm 1:1, the turntable 0.006 per motor turn, nothing listed to stay moves); yellow down drives the pump's crank whichever way the red lever stands. All as page 43 of printed book 5 shows. The stored not-driven came from function 8 having the winch's steps; my 'both down reaches the crane' reading in decision 210 came from free gears the solver gave some turn. The red knob is p832, on the lever of the pneumatic valve p826 (function 16): it switches the air, not the drive, so page 43's two lower pictures have one drive, and no slide step is needed. Decided: (1) the two errata (the universal joint at the gearbox's output made whole: book 2, step 65, page 3; the shaft's ball moved 6.2 LDU into its cup: step 77, page 24) are merged unsigned as proposals; I read both pages. (2) The list change stays on the branch for Erik: function 8's steps p512 −0.4 and p516 +0.4; function 9's step p516 −0.4 alone; function 10 keeps its place, since the booklet counts it, with the words that it is the compressor again with the valve's other setting, and the pump's parts to move; function 6 says three outputs; notes corrected. (3) Function 10 will go from works to partly with it, and that is right: its works was hollow (nothing listed to move). So the count stays 737 until the pump is mended: function 8 up, function 10 down. (4) The pump: with yellow down the motor stops after 0.43 radian both ways, named a dead point, on preview's code and on claude/slide-ends alike; at that turn the pump is 1.6 LDU into its 40 LDU stroke, so it is not the stroke's end. For the solver owner to trace, after its full check and the one-way measure, before the plunging shafts. (5) Before the item goes to Erik, the worker writes a drag for the crane's setting and I run it on port 5174.
214. **The 8110's crane is shown in the viewer and goes to Erik; the 8081's tailgate is parked; the 8880's hatch gets one collision run.** (1) The worker's drag (scripts/drags/8110-crane.ts, now in preview) run on port 5174 with the proposed errata: the red lever dragged up by its arm turned its catch p512 by −0.52 radian and the yellow one its catch p516 by +0.53 (the list's steps: −0.4 and +0.4); the rings went 5 LDU to the places that lock p321 and p332; with the motor running 60 s its gear turned 1.41 radian, the turntable's gear 0.0093 (0.0066 of the motor; the check has 0.00595), and the winch's drum stayed still. So the 8110's item is put to Erik: the two errata of decision 213 and the list change for functions 6 to 10 and 16. (2) The 8081's tailgate (function 6): the file is right; the tailgate hinges at the roof (book 3, steps 5 and 6) and its lower beam p415 sits from above on two upright pins in the rear light panels, which are pinned to the chassis on a second line, so two parallel lines lock it as read. In the real set the beam lifts off the pins, about 6° off their axis. No mend now: a rule for it needs a bound on how far off its axis a pin may leave its hole, which no shape gives yet, and a picked angle is not allowed; slides on demand are not widened for one function. The cause is noted as 'lifts off pins: not read' and taken up again at three functions. (3) The 8880's hatch (function 10): three parallel lines along x tie it to the car (A, the list's hinge, axles from step 31; B, two smooth pins 3673 on the roof arch from step 30; C, the hatch's own friction pins in the body's sides), plus the prop rod's rigid foot, so it turns on none. The worker turns it about each line with the other ties dropped, with the collision test, and says which line the scans support; no erratum without a step or photo that shows the parts placed otherwise.
215. **The 8880's hatch hinges at the roof's top (line B), not on the list's axles; two checks from the file before anything goes to Erik. A viewer fault with marked parts goes to the worker.** (1) The worker turned the hatch about each of its three parallel lines with the other ties dropped, with the viewer's collision test: about A (the list's hinge, the step-31 axles) it rises 4° and meets the roof; about C (its friction pins) 6°; about B (two smooth pins 3673 on the roof arch, step 30) 44.5°, until a bush meets a body-side beam. I looked at the scans myself: the photo (scan 053) shows the hatch up about 45° turning at the roof's rear top, and a grey axle end still on the body's side block, pointing sideways. So B is the hinge, and the step-31 axles are bolts the user slides or stop short of the hatch: a hatch swinging about a line along x cannot thread an axle along x. Decided: no question to Erik yet. The worker finds from the file what holds the two 3895 beams of line C to the car (step 33 adds two long beams and six pins with the hatch, so they may be the hatch's own), how far each axle can slide in its block and whether that clears the hatch, and then runs function 10 by name on a scratch list with B as the hinge and a slide step for each bolt (decision 206). The list change goes to Erik as one item once it works or leaves one clear question. (2) Found while testing the 8110's link: on the 8110 a link that marks certain parts (show=559,556,60; show=2216) draws nothing, and every frame throws in three's renderer; show=60 or show=559,556 alone draws, and so does the 42078 with four marks; it fails without the proposed errata too. The worker finds and mends it on claude/show-null before the hatch; the 8110's link on the decisions page marks only the lever arms until then.
216. **The viewer fault with marked parts is mended: a colour written in the wrong form.** claude/show-null 03ef358a merged. The cause, found by the worker: one line of the OMR's 8110 gives an axle (p1365) a direct colour in decimal (44278436, LDraw's 0x2A3A2A4); when a model is packed, every colour was written in decimal, and three's loader knows a direct colour only in its 0x2 form, so that part's conditional edges got no material, and every frame whose camera saw them threw and drew nothing. It has been so since the viewer first packed OMR files (2026-10-19); among all the files only this one line has such a colour. The mend writes a direct colour back in hex (colourCode in src/ldr.ts); both forms read as the same number, so no reading or record changes. Gate: type check, 1,040 tests, none fail in a bare checkout, pivots as kept; on port 5174 the 8110 loads with all four parts marked and no page error, where before it drew nothing. The 8110's link on the decisions page marks the lever arms, the motor and the turntable's gear again.
217. **Decision 215 was too quick: the 8880's hatch is a linkage with several pivot lines, not one body on one hinge. No question goes to Erik.** The worker's second pass found two more ties that no slide can undo (the step-31 axles run through the hatch's bricks and are held by bushes, with 2 LDU of play; the step-35 side beams hang on chassis pins and take the hatch's pins), and that the file's author built the hatch around line A. Before asking Erik which ties are real, I read Technicopedia's page on the set (https://technicopedia.com/8880.html): the boot opens by a linkage; the bulk of the structure is pinned about a point on the body; the links that stand for the rear window have a different pivot axis; another pair of links goes down to runners along the rear edge of the door sills; a collapsible link holds it raised by going over centre. So lines A, B and C are probably all real (the bulk's pivot, the window links' pivot, the sill links), the prop rod is the collapsible link, and the file may be right: the fault is likely the reading, which makes the 191-part submodel one rigid body, as the worker's collision run also did. Decided: the worker reads the hatch with every friction pin free (its bodies, the joints between them and to the car), counts whether that linkage moves, and says what would make function 10 work: pins to free, a tie read rigid that is a pivot, a file fault with its step, or a position solve for the solver owner. The note on claude/8880-hatch that names B as the only hinge is taken back. A rule of work from this: before a question about a real set goes to Erik, a review's description of the function is read (as the function sources rule says); here it changed the question.
218. **The 8880's hatch opens in the file as it is: the fault is the pin search. Reports are batched to keep usage down.** The worker read the hatch with every friction pin free: the 191-part submodel forms 34 bodies, in three groups: the bulk (roof and rear-window frame, the tail, the window links' carrier, the prop), the two side frames on the step-31 axles and the step-35 beams, and two link bricks (p1363, p1364) that join the bulk to the side frames by four friction pins (p1267, p1281, p1321, p1354). With only those four free, the bulk turns about the roof line B by 41.9° and stops at a dead point with the prop over centre, as the photo and Technicopedia's words have it; the side frames stay on the body. So the file is right, no erratum and no list change is needed, and no position solve is missing. What fails: none of the three pin searches finds the four pins (the check's finds none and frees two by trial; Shift's says the part is held by more than friction pins; the drag's picks six click hinge plates and two other pins, because with every pin free the hinge plates make the body floppy). Decided: the worker diagnoses each search's miss and proposes the narrowest rule without a picked number (one candidate: a click hinge plate shapes a body and is never a pin to free), with the sets it could change; the mend rides with the next full check (decision 96). The solver owner is not interrupted. Usage: the account is at 50% of its week with 42% gone, and this session's own model at 45%; the sessions now batch their reports (a branch ready for the gate, a decision needed, or three look-ups), so that fewer turns are spent here.
219. **The pin search: a part in the frame is judged against the frame, and every friction pin the found motion turns is freed.** The worker's diagnosis for the 8880's hatch (note pin-search-8880.md): pinsToFree asks whether the control moves against its parent in the all-free joint tree, and that parent is another body of the hatch's bulk, so the lift about the roof line counts as no motion and no pin is named; Shift's search only repeats that; the drag's search then finds a local set by trial (two pins and six hinge plates) with which the tail flaps on its own pins. Decided: (1) the worker's rule: where the part's body as read is the frame, a motion moves the part when the part moves against the frame in the all-free build. With it pinsToFree finds the lift about B. (2) The cut-down changes. Today it keeps the fewest pins that pass a first-order test, which keeps two of the four: with the other two fused the hatch turns on two lines that are nearly one, passes at first order and binds at 6°, where four free give 41.9°. New: every friction pin whose own joint turns in the found motion is kept free; a pin stays fused only if its joint does not turn. The test is turns or not, with the zero-rate tolerance the code has, so no number is picked; the reason is that a friction pin a function's motion turns does turn in the real set. (3) No rule for hinge plates: doors and hoods hinge on them. (4) Built by the worker on claude/pin-search after claude/slow-rates, with a test on a model made in the test, and by-name runs of the 8880's function 10, the 12 sets with kept pivot files and ten more of the 80 sets whose functions use the searches (126 functions, the upper bound of what can change); the full check rides with the next one (decision 96).
220. **The slide ends are merged after their full check: no result changes, 737 work. Two false gains are held; a holds file replaces my hand; no rule in the check for a works with little motion.** claude/slide-ends ca8862a4 (decisions 192, 208, 211; the full check of 38c70beb, preview 26e7c410 merged in, 129 minutes). Four functions differ from the stored records. Only one is from the slide ends: the 42079's function 2 stays works, its + way comes back 100.7° off against 70.7° (a 5L axle is held at the end of its slide and the path differs; that way was not back before either). Two are gains the viewer does not show, from preview's own code and older than this branch: the 42078's function 12 (works without its lock bars lifted or door 2's erratum signed; it waits for Erik's sign-off of the doors item) and the 8274's function 5 (both ways stop where parts meet at the built position; in the viewer the auger shifts 6 to 14 LDU and does not turn). Both are held at their stored records (no and partly), as before. The fourth is the 42066's function 2, the near tie of decision 207. No new limit on a function that should go on (the two-actuators case of decision 208). Gate: no conflicts; type check; 1,040 tests; pivots as kept; ratchet 739 against 737, and 737 against 737 with the two held; nine drag scripts on port 5174 all yes (42036 fn5, 42040 fn6 and fn7, the 42078's trailer, 42080 fn2 and fn3, 8272 fn2 and fn3, the 8868's rods). The solver owner asked whether the check should refuse works where both ways stop before they turn: not as a general rule. 18 stored works have every way turned 0.11 radian or less and stopped at a limit; some are real short travels and some false stops, and a cut-off would be a picked number; they are the rows the contact measurement sorts. Instead the solver owner builds data/holds.json (set, function, the result to hold at, reason, date), which the check applies itself: a held function that comes out better is stored at its held result with what the check found beside it; one that comes out equal or worse is stored as found and the run says the hold is no longer needed.
221. **Taken back by decision 223: the pin search's cut-down by an exact motion.** After the worker found that 'every pin the motion turns' frees 31 to 51 pins on the 8880's hatch (the all-free build has a 29-dimensional free space, so there is no one motion), I set the test for holding a pin again to 'the part still moves in an exact motion, without play', thinking the hatch bound at 6° on two hinge lines that are nearly one. The worker checked first: the cut-down already asks for an exact motion, the two lines are 340 LDU apart, and with two pins free the hatch has a real exact motion through its own linkage that reaches the end of its travel at 6°. So the test cannot tell the cases apart, and nothing was built.
222. **The tracks' roll there and back: the worker traces the cause itself, read-only.** A roll of the 42006's tracked chassis there and back ends 9 to 25 LDU off, and neither the step size nor the scrub allowance changes it. The solver owner has a queue, and the worker holds the case, so the worker traced it in src/kinematics.ts without changing it (the result is decision 224).
223. **The pin search: when a turn with freed pins stops at a dead point, the pins are looked for again from where the model stands.** The frame rule of decision 219 stays, and the cut-down stays as it is (the fewest pins with an exact motion at the pose searched). New: if a function's turn stops at a dead point short of the turn asked and pins were freed or searched for, the search runs again at the stopped pose with the freed pins kept free; further pins it names are freed and the turn goes on; if it names none the stop stands. It ends because each round frees at least one pin. The reason: a friction pin turns when the mechanism forces it; at the start two pins are enough for the 8880's hatch, at 6° the linkage is at the end of what two allow, and the real set then turns the next ones. It matches the viewer, where the user presses Shift again where a drag stops, and it picks no number: the trigger is the stop. The worker probes first (at the 6° stop the search should name p1321 and p1354 only, and the hatch then reach about 41.9°) and builds only if the probe holds; proof as in decision 219.
224. **The origin is set again before each step: a motion must depend on the pose alone.** The worker's trace: nearOrigin moves the model so its frame is at the origin, because far from the origin turning weighs more in the least-motion sums (the 956's wandering), and turnJoint calls it once for a whole call. Within one call of 1.5 radian the 42006 goes 137 LDU, so on the way out the origin is the start and on the way back the far end, and the free track's roll, a least-motion choice, drifts: there and back in calls of 1.5 radian the frame ends 24.63 LDU off, in calls of 0.1 radian 1.55, in calls of 0.01 radian 0.01. Ruled out: the step size inside a call, the scrub allowance, which treads touch, a steering pivot, the tracks' own rows. Decided: the origin is set before each step inside a call (and in pushStep if it has the same), so that the motion picked depends on the pose alone and never on where a call began; only then can a motion retrace itself. For the solver owner, on claude/origin-per-step, after the 8110's pump trace and before the plunging shafts, with a test (one long call and many short ones end at the same place), by-name runs of the 42006's function 6, the 9391's function 5 and the 14 functions the worker's note lists, and a full check shared with the pin search if both are ready. A further fact for the kept-against-afresh task: at one state the motion kept from the last turn moved the frame 86.10 LDU per radian where a fresh solve gives 91.99.
225. **The pin search tries pins where a turn stops: no test at one pose can see the stop.** The worker's probe of decision 223 failed and showed why: at a dead point the linkage still has a first-order motion with the pins it has (the free motions are a linear space, so a motion back, negated, is a motion forward); the stop comes from second-order terms, which no test on the motions at one pose sees, exact or signed. So the test must be a step of the turn itself. The rule, the worker's scheme with a cut-down added: where a function's turn stops at a dead point short of the turn asked and pins were freed or searched for, the friction pins that turn in the all-free motions moving the part against the frame, at the stopped pose, are tried nearest first in growing groups, as pinsByTrial does, until the turn goes one more step past the stop; then the newly freed pins are held again one by one, farthest first, each kept held if the turn still goes on; what is left is freed and the turn goes on. One step of the check's own turn is the test, so no number is picked. In the probe, at the 8880's 6° stop it adds p1321 and p1354 and nothing else, and the hatch reaches 41.9°.
226. **A control judged against the frame is worked against the frame: pushed, not turned on the joint its body hangs on.** Built into the check, the trial still failed on the 8880's hatch: with the two pins free the check turns the control (the tail p1240) on the joint its body hangs on, which is a joint inside the hatch and not the roof line, and the trial there frees the wrong pins (p287, p404). The worker offered a list change (the control a part on the hinge) or a trigger where a push sticks. Decided: no list change; a list names the part a user takes hold of. A control that the pin search judged against the frame is worked by a push of the part, as a user lifts the tail, and the trial of decision 225 also triggers where such a push sticks, its test one more push step. This is the last pass on the 8880 for now: if the probe fails, its function 10 is parked with its cause named and the branch is kept only for what the sample gains elsewhere.
227. **The contact rule is dropped; the holds file is built; the 8110's pump is locked by its mount, not at a dead point.** (1) The one-way contact, measured on the 108 functions on the slide-ends code with a release test (note contact-guide-measure.md): 4 no to works, 4 partly to works, 2 no to partly, 3 works to partly; by the ratchet 33 worse and none better, every gain with ways not back; no real end becomes works, but 8 of the 48 come back worse; the cap of 12 held contacts is reached on 8 ways and decides no result. One cause in every trace: with a contact held, the motion slides along it and moves parts that should not move. It fails the second of decision 211's three tests, so the rule is not built and nothing more is measured; the stops not settled stay sorted one cause at a time. (2) claude/holds 3490687b (decision 220) is ready for the gate. (3) The 8110's function 9: the solver owner's trace shows the motor has no exact motion from the first step and turns only on play until a step is refused with a joint 2.1 LDU apart, which the check names a dead point. The pump loop is a slider-crank about z, and its mount (the pump base p378 with the cross blocks p49 and p50) should swing about z, but it also hangs about x on the 7L beam p46 and the long pin p47, so it cannot swing and the crank is locked; with the crank pin made a ball, and only then, the loop moves. Whether the file or the reading is wrong is a booklet question, given to the worker (note 8110-fn9-pump-lock.md).
228. **The longer turn for slow parts is widened: 739 functions work.** claude/slow-rates 38fc7a4e merged (decision 162; only scripts/check-functions.ts and a drag script). A listed part that the control moves at a rate counts as slow, not as not driven, by its turning or its sliding, whichever is larger (the 8258's piston slides 0.2 LDU for each radian of the control, where its own joint in the tree turns at 9 × 10⁻⁸); a function whose controls share out its parts and whose cause together is too little is worked again with each control at the longer turn. By decision 96 six functions can change, from the stored records (the 42064's 2, the 42083's 4, the 42099's 1, the 42100's 7, the 8258's 5, the 42081's 2). Their six sets, checked by name on the merged code with the slide ends in, give: the 8258's function 5 and the 42081's function 2 from partly to works; the other four stay partly with the cause now too little. Gate: the six set files conflicted with the full check's records, so preview's were kept and the check wrote them afresh; type check; 1,040 tests; pivots as kept; ratchet 739 against 737, none worse. In the viewer on port 5174 (scripts/drags/longer-turn.ts): the 8258's motor gear turned 292° each way after the catch is set slides the actuator 1.01 LDU and moves the boom's part 1.25 to 1.32 LDU; each of the 42081's knobs turned 97° each way slides the piston 1.01 LDU and moves the listed parts 3.4 and 6.8 LDU; no stops. Both gains are just over the 1 LDU that counts as moved, which is what the function does in the real set too: a long turn for a small motion. The Mac was restarted during this gate (the desktop app locked up); nothing in the repository was lost, the viewers were started again, and the sessions restarted what they had lost.
229. **The Mac ran out of memory twice on 2026-11-19; one check at a time until the check has a pool for the whole machine.** The desktop app locked up at 18:41 and the Mac panicked at 19:03; Erik restarted it both times. The panic report (userspace watchdog timeout, no check-ins from WindowServer for 120 s) shows 16 check children holding about 82 GB on this 32 GB, 12-thread Mac mini: two checks of the solver owner side by side, 5 children each at 5.5 to 8.1 GB, and the slow check I had just started, 6 children. A report of 09:02 the same day shows one full check alone at 59 GB of node, with the system ending other programs for lack of swap. The fault was shared: each session ran its own checks without knowing of the others, the check gave each child the whole machine's memory as its heap limit, and I started the slow check without telling anyone. Decided: at once, one check on the machine at a time with at most 2 jobs, taken through a lock folder; and the solver owner builds a pool into the check, shared by every working folder, in which each child is entered with the memory it is expected to take and starts only while the sum stays within half the machine's memory (16 GB), the expected figure being the peak the set reached at its last run, stored by the check. A child's heap limit becomes the budget.
230. **The pin search is parked; a flexible part never ties two bodies.** (1) The last pass on the 8880's hatch (decision 226) gave no gain: pushed, the tail went into a floppy motion and the trial kept seven pins; on the only three functions the trial for turns can gain on (the 42036's 5, the 8081's 6, the 8880's 10) nothing changed. claude/pin-search is not merged, and the 8880's function 10 stays no, its cause named: the check never turns the control about the function's hinge. (2) The 8110's compressor (functions 9 and 10): the file is right (book 1, step 28 hangs the pump on its crank); the pump's hose is what locks it, because a path's segments are carried rigid by the nearest part and the hose's end plugs into the pump, so the hose fuses the pump base to a cross block that turns another way. The rule: the parts of a flexible path and its end fittings (a hose, a string, a rubber band, and decorations read the same way) keep one tie only, to the part they plug into or else the nearest part, and never join two bodies. Built by the worker on claude/hose-rides.
231. **The hand-made lock failed twice within a minute; everything heavy goes through the pool.** The worker wrote the process id of a shell that ended at once into the lock, and the solver owner's script took a word that was no number as proof that the holder was gone; for some minutes two sessions ran side by side again (the machine held). So the lock scripted by each session is given up: the pool gets a wrapper that enters any heavy command that is not a check (a reading of all models, the collision files, a probe that loads a large model) and makes it wait for the budget as a check's child does.
232. **At most 10 heavy processes on the machine at a time.** Erik, 2026-11-19: 'let's do a worker limit too - 16 check processes on a 12 thread machine ain't gonna work; how about 10 max'. The pool has a second limit beside the memory budget: at most 10 live entries across every check and wrapped command from every working folder. The figure is his and is kept as a named constant, not derived from the core count.
233. **The hose rule needs five sets checked by name, not a full check.** The worker read every model before and after the rule: 5 of 190 change (the 42053, 42080, 42114, 8063 and 8110), each gaining bodies where a hose, a chain end or, once, a minifigure's cup had joined two parts or had been held still by a second tie to the same part. By decision 96 only those five sets' functions can change. Parked by decision 236 until the viewer sweep's trial is reported.
234. **Where a check child's memory goes: the collision test holds 7.5 of 8.4 GB.** Erik asked what takes so many gigabytes: a 4,000-part model at 8 GB is 2 MB a part. Measured on the 42100's function 7, one child alone, memory every 5 s: with the collision test a peak of 8,436 MB in 732 s; with it off, 926 MB in 487 s. It is taken in the first 10 s, before anything turns, and it falls to 3.7 GB and returns to 8 GB several times in the run, so the shapes are also built more than once. The code agrees: the Collisions constructor shrinks and builds a search tree for every placed part, so each placed part has its own copy of its triangles and its own tree, where a model of 4,000 parts has a few hundred part types.
235. **Shapes are shared per part type.** Erik, 2026-11-19: 'share shapes per part type, go ahead whenever you decide it's appropriate'. Built now, before the long runs, since it makes them faster and safer: one shrunk shape and one tree per part type, shared by every placed copy, each copy keeping only its matrix; the shapes built once per process; the same in the viewer if it holds them per placed part. No result may change. By the solver owner on claude/shared-shapes, with by-name runs whose records must be identical and the 42100's function 7 measured before and after.
236. **Every working function is tried in the viewer; the viewer's time per step is measured and mended; no offer of main before that.** Erik opened the 9397 on 2026-11-19: the steering did nothing under his hand, and with a motor running the page took seconds per frame while the FPS card showed 60. He asked whether all sets marked green need a sanity check. Measured, the same on preview and on main: the viewer's own turnPart on the steering knob's axle turns the front wheels 12.9° for 0.6 radian, as the check has it, but takes 9.4 s, about half a second a step (1,460 parts, 426 bodies, 695 joints); a profile gives jointMotion 51% (the free motions of the whole model, dense, at each step), the choice of drag holds 15%, showing the poses 10%. So the check's works is true of the motion and says nothing of whether a user can do it. Decided: (1) the worker builds a sweep (scripts/drags/sweep.ts) that, for every function stored as works, turns each control in the viewer as the check did and compares the listed parts' motion with the record, then takes the control with the pointer or starts its motor, and records shown or not shown and the time per step, with no threshold picked for too slow; the 57 sets where every checked function works go first, after a trial on five sets. (2) The solver owner, after the shared shapes, measures the time per step on four models and proposes a mend for my decision (first candidate: solve a step only on the part of the mechanism the turned joint can move), and mends the FPS card. (3) Parked until the sweep's first results: the full check of the origin mend, the hose rule's by-name runs, the plunging shafts' merge, and any offer of main. The rule is the old one: the check passes only what the viewer shows; the sweep makes it a measurement.
237. **Erik's sign-offs of 2026-11-19: the 42078's doors and the 8110's crane. 740 functions work.** In his words: 'Sign 42078 doors' and 'Sign 8110 crane'. (1) The 42078: door 2's four erratum entries signed (the 13L beam and two blue pins out, two black pins in) and function 12's list taken from claude/42078-door-list (both doors as controls, each lock bar lifted first by a slide step); its hold in data/holds.json removed. Checked by name with 2 jobs: function 12 from no to works, nothing else changes: 9 work, 4 partly. (2) The 8110: the two entries for the crane's shaft signed (the universal joint made whole, the ball moved into its cup) and the lists of functions 6 to 10 and 16 taken from claude/8110-shaft (the lever settings read from the file and matched to page 43 of printed book 5). Its stored records are as before the sign-off until the slow check has run: function 8 should go from partly to works and function 10 from works to partly. (3) The kept pin files of both sets are made again; their kept collision files are stale and removed, and the worker makes them again. Gate: type check, 1,043 tests, ratchet 740 against 739, none worse; in the viewer on port 5174 on the signed files, the 42078's lock bars lift and its doors swing (yes, 3,336 s) and the 8110's levers set the crane's drive and the turntable turns (yes, 1,270 s). The plan, the README's counts and the decisions page were updated one commit after the sign-offs' own commit (0cc11fee), because my script stopped on a wrong line number.
238. **The shared shapes are in: a check child for the 42100 takes 1.3 GB where it took 8.4. My account of where the memory went was partly wrong.** claude/shared-shapes 831b4ff9 merged (decision 235). The solver owner's stage probe corrects decision 234: the held shapes were small (217 MB of positions, 120 MB of trees). The 8.4 GB was (1) the LDraw loader in the check's partShapes, which read all 7,283 placed parts of the 42100 copy by copy and held 6 to 8 GB at once at the start, of which 126 MB stayed in use; and (2) 1.6 GB in Collisions.watch, which listed every watched pair as a small array of its own (22,057,675 pairs), anew each time the mechanism was worked out, the old list waiting for the garbage collector, which are the dips and returns in my log. The mend: the loader reads each colour-and-part key once (229 keys for 185 part types), without the line records no collision uses, and every placed copy takes its key's list (positions identical to the bit against the old way; the loader's peak 0.75 GB against 8.5, 4 s against 42); Collisions shrinks a list and builds its trees once; a group of pairs keeps its parts on each side and walks the pairs in the old order instead of listing them. The 42100's function 7: peak 1,268 MB against 8,436, 753 s against 732, the same result, turn, back and stop. Gate: type check; 1,053 tests, none fail in a bare checkout; ratchet 740 against 740; pivots as kept; by name through the pool the 8258, 8418, 8032 and 42078 give records identical to preview's; in the viewer on port 5174 four drags with collision stops all yes (42080 fn2 and fn3, 8272 fn2, the 8868's rods). The wrapper's wait line now says what it waits for and only when it does. The viewer still reads its shapes copy by copy; sharing them there follows the step-time work. Also reported and parked: with the plunging shafts the 8880's functions 7 and 8 go from no to works by name, but each way turns only 0.07 radian before the shaft's cross block meets the arm's liftarm, and function 8 has four ways not back, so it is not a clean gain and needs a trace before any merge; the 8466's function 8 is unchanged because its balls in their sockets are read as no coupling, which is the worker's reading to look at. The FPS card's fault: every frame of a second or more is left out as a hidden tab coming back, so when all frames are slow only the fast ones are counted.
239. **The viewer's time per step, measured; my smaller-domain idea dropped; the FPS card mended.** The solver owner's figures, one step of 0.035 radian in node: the 9397's steering 571 ms (1,460 parts, 426 bodies, 695 joints), the 8110's crane about 4 s (4,044 parts), the 42100's function 7 839 ms (7,283 parts), the mid-size 42094's function 1 37 ms. On the 9397 and the 8110 jointMotion takes 79 to 90%: every step orthonormalizes, decomposes and chooses among all the model's free motions (204 on the 9397, 316 on the 8110), most of which are single parts that nothing else touches; on the 42100 half the step is closeLoops. My candidate, to solve a step only on the part the turned joint can move, is dropped: that part is still most of the model (259 of 451 coordinates on the 9397), and a smaller domain would need a guess that can change a motion. The FPS card left out every frame of a second or more as a hidden tab coming back, so with all frames slow it counted only the fast ones and read 60; it now leaves out only a frame during which the page was hidden (claude/step-time e04583ed, merged: on port 5174 the 9397 reads 1 and, with its motor running, 0, where a frame count in the page gives 0.8 and 0.6 in a headless browser).
240. **Mend 1 withdrawn before it was built: a part nothing touches is not given zero motion.** I had accepted, on the solver owner's word, that leaving the untouched coordinates out of a step is exact. It tested the claim and found it false: least turning measures a body's turning in the world, so a free part on a swinging arm is turned back against the arm, and its coordinate is not zero (all 38 on the 42100, all 4 on the 42094, one of 98 on the 9397). Dropping them would make those parts turn with what carries them.
241. **The block form is built in its place; the solver is to move off the page's thread.** Measured for the exact alternatives: the tiers before turning (mounted, steering, pins, housing, frame tip) see none of the untouched coordinates; turning and the joints tier see all; so an elimination would need a Schur complement in the turning tier and substitution in two more, for little gain under a structural test (34 of 200 motions on the 9397). The cheaper exact route is taken: in freeMotionsApart the coordinates whose columns are zero by the null space's own RANK_TOLERANCE come out as unit vectors, an identity block, and the null space, the orthonormalizing, the parked, rolling and suspension split and leastScrub run on the touched block alone, the unit vectors appended unchanged; chooseMotion still gets every motion, so the choice is the same except at an exact tie. Expected, not yet measured: the 9397 from 571 to about 260 ms a step. Proof by name with the near-tie functions among the sample, and it rides with the next full check. After it: the same block form in leastTurning's first tiers, proposed first; a trace of the 42100's loops, which take 13 rounds a step and rebuild the tree basis five times; and, since even then the 8110 stays near a second a step, a design for running the solver in a worker so the page keeps drawing and a drag follows a few steps behind. A usable drag needs about 50 to 100 ms a step.
242. **A fault in the shared shapes, found by the train check: shapes were handed out by their place in a list. Mended; the check now fails where shapes and parts do not match.** After the daily merge of preview into preview-train (999ab12e) the train owner ran its check: 50 of 51, the 4558 Metroliner's run failing with 'the parts' shapes don't match the model's parts'. The loader of decision 235 placed each distinct line once and took the i-th child as the i-th line's shape; a line that yields no child (the 4558 places two subparts of a split tile on their own) shifted every later shape to the next part. The train check has a guard; the Technic check had none. The solver owner scanned all 190 models: on c942ecfd 8 did not match (the 42112, 42113, 42115, 8275 and 8466 by decision 235; the 42120, 42123 and 42125 never had, since they embed a subpart file the loader and the parser read differently, and ran without their collision stop, silently). The 8110, 42078, 8258, 8418 and 8032 match, so the slow check and the proof of decision 238 stand, and no stored record is from the faulty code. claude/shapes-by-name b57dc2b3 merged: each child is matched to its line by file and colour; given the parts' names the reading returns one shape per part; a part the loader made nothing of gets an empty shape and is named; and the check fails a function with an error where shapes and parts do not match one for one. All 190 models match with it. The three kept collision files made from misplaced shapes are removed. My gate for decision 238 compared four sets and ran four drags, none of which has such a line; a scan of every model was the missing step, and the guard now makes it on every run.
243. **Route 5 held: six functions change, and not by a tie in the solver's choice.** By name on 12 sets no result changes, but six functions get one more way not back and three lose a collision stop. The solver owner's trace of three of them: the chosen motions agree between the two runs to 1e-8 to 1e-15, no eigenvalue lies near a tier's cut, and what differs is a collision stop that one run meets and the other does not.
244. **The stops that flip are flush faces, not grazes or crossings.** Measured on the 42075: the crank and the engine part's triangles meet at distance exactly 0 at several steps as the crank comes round, whole faces, and the collision test reports one such meeting in one run and none in the other, on matrices that differ by 2e-11. LOOP_TOLERANCE (1e-11) is the size of that noise, so a rule that takes a shallow overlap as a touch leaves no room, and the library's closest distance is wrong on these meshes, so a rule on the growth of depth cannot be built on it.
245. **Not the shrink either: the collision library mistakes two triangles in one plane.** I guessed that the scale about a part's middle left faces flush. Tested: the 42075's meeting faces did move the full 0.25 LDU. The fault: three-mesh-bvh 0.9.16 takes two triangles as lying in one plane only if each corner is off the other's plane by less than 1e-15, absolute; faces in one plane of a model a few hundred LDU across are off it by their coordinates' rounding (1.9 to 8.6e-15 here), so the test treats them as two crossing planes with parallel normals and may answer that they meet, though they are 11 LDU apart in the plane. Faces of different parts in one plane are common on the stud grid, so false stops can come anywhere, in the check and in the viewer. The shrink does have a weakness of its own (about 35% of triangles move less than half of INSET along their normal), noted for a mend after this.
246. **Our own guard on triangles in one plane; its full check comes before everything else. The pool's estimate corrected.** claude/coplanar 2e0bff21 (not yet merged): where the library says two triangles intersect and every corner of each is off the other's plane by no more than the rounding of its coordinates (16 ulps of the largest, a count of the rounded operations, not a tried value), the answer is false; two faces lying on each other are a touch, not a crossing, since a crossing of solid parts shows in their other faces. Set once in src/collisions.ts for the check, the viewer and the collision worker. By name on the four sets with the six flips, preview's code and route 5's code agree in every field but rates (at most 2.8e-8); against preview's stored records two false stops go (the 42075's function 2 turns its full 1.5 radian; the 42077's function 2 comes back). How many of the 761 stored collision stops (359 functions, 138 sets) are of this kind is counted by the full check itself. Also: the slow check's 8110 and 9397 finished on c942ecfd and their records are taken into preview (the 8110's function 8 works, as signed; functions 9 and 10 partly, the pump locked by its hose); its last set was stopped. It crawled because the pool still entered a function of the 42100 at 11.2 GB, an estimate from before the shared shapes, against a real 1.3 GB, and ran one at a time on an idle machine; a by-name run of mine waited 46 minutes without starting. The estimate for a set with no stored peak is now 1000 MB and 0.75 MB a part, above every peak measured since decision 235.
247. **A nameless child belongs to no line: the 6990's shapes.** After the mend of decision 242 the train check gave 49 of 51: the 4558 worked again, the 6990 monorail failed in the reading. It places a subpart directly, for which the loader makes a child with an empty name in the middle of the list, and the match by name stopped there. The train owner, who held the case, mended it on a branch from preview (claude/shapes-nameless f3c158bd, merged): such a child belongs to no line wherever it stands. With it all 51 train functions work. It also wrote scripts/scan-part-shapes.ts, which reads every model the sets use and compares shapes with parts by name.
248. **The match by name lost its place at a part an erratum removes: in four models most parts had no shape. Mended; a part with no shape must now have a reason.** The scan counted empty shapes: the 42052 659 of 1,245, the 42109 592 of 973, the 42093 569 of 576, the 42078 22 of 2,691 (and the 42120's 252 and the 8094's 2, which are other things). The cause: a part an erratum removes stays in the parts list, marked removed, and is packed as an empty file under another name; the match by name of decision 242 did not find the part's name there, gave it an empty shape and did not move on, so every later part was compared with that same line and got none. The guard passed over removed parts, and the names still matched. It came only with decision 242 (0a240b8b); matching by place had stayed in step. No stored record is affected: preview's records are from c942ecfd or earlier, and no kept collision file was made since. claude/shapes-all 57825241 merged: a removed part is matched to its empty line; every part with no shape carries its reason (removed by an erratum; a path's part; a line the loader made nothing of; a file in neither the model nor the library; a part with no triangles); any other mismatch throws, naming the part; the check's guard fails a function where a part has no shape and no reason, and the scan fails on the same rule. Gate: type check; 1,053 tests, none fail in a bare checkout; ratchet 740 against 740; pivots as kept; and my own run of the scan on the merged code: 190 of 190 match, the parts with no shape being 8 removed by errata (the 42052's one, the 42078's three, the 42093's one, the 42109's three), the 42120's 252 segments of a flexible axle, and two tiles of the 8094 whose files are in neither the model nor the library (a gap of its own: their current library names are to be found). Two shape faults reached preview in one night through my gate, which compared a few sets' records and ran a few drags; from now the scan of every model, counting empty shapes, is part of the gate for any change to how shapes are read.
249. **The guard on triangles in one plane is merged: five false stops go, no result changes. Three sets get their first collision stops, and one of them shows a dead V8.** The full check of claude/coplanar (221c9a1f, the first full check through the pool with shared shapes: 6,094 s with 10 jobs, the slow sets 5,420 s more, the four sets of decision 248 run again by name on the mend): ratchet 740 against 740, 3 better (the 42041's function 4, the 42077's function 2 and the 42110's function 10 now come back each way), 0 worse, no result changes. Collision stops: 760 the same, 5 vanished (the 42041's function 1, the 42075's function 2, the 42096's function 1, two of the 42110's function 10), none moved, 16 appeared. So of 765 stored stops 5 were the library's fault: far fewer than I feared in decision 245, and the stored results stood on true stops. The 16 new stops are all on the 42120, 42123 and 42125, the three sets of the first shapes fault (decision 242) that never had a collision test; the pairs touching as built are the same with and without the guard (896, 1,694 and 3,512), so the guard does not cause them. My gate: type check, 1,056 tests (none fail in a bare checkout), pivots as kept, and my own drags in the viewer on 5174 with the guard in: the 42075's p506, the 42096's p1686 and the 42041's p647 and p645 each turn 1.5 radian both ways with no stop, as the new records say; the 42123's wheel p1069 stops at 0.105 radian each way, p165 against p257 and p268, as its new record says. No scan of shapes was needed: the branch changes no reading of shapes. Open, and the solver owner's next task before route 5: the new stops of the three sets. The 42123's function 4 (the wheels roll freely) is stored as works though the wheel that turns the engine stops after 0.1 radian; its function 2 (rolling turns the V8) is stored as partly, rightly, since the pistons stay still (I first wrote here that it was stored as works: wrong, see decision 250). Either the model has two parts overlapping there (an erratum for Erik) or the test is wrong there; the trace says which. The check counting such a function as works is the weakness decision 236's sweep is for: the worker's trial now reports, for each working function, how far its control went before a stop and the pair that stopped it. The worker's trial had stood still since the evening of 2026-11-19 (its turn ended waiting for a run and nothing woke it); told to commit its script, merge preview and go on.
250. **The new stops traced: none is a fault of a shape or of the test. The viewer cannot open the 42120 at all, which is mended first.** The solver owner turned each control to its first hit and measured the pair (new-stops-trace.md in the notes folder). (1) The 42123's engine: a crank web (p165) runs into a piston (p257, p268), 0.6 LDU apart as built. In the real set the webs push the pistons up and gravity brings them down; Kinebrick hinges each piston to its bank and ties none to the crankshaft, so the piston stays and the web hits it. (2) The 42123's steering stops at ±0.7 radian: the rack (p110) hangs only on its two hinges to the hubs with no slide, moves on an arc and meets the beam in front of it. (3) The 42120's fans stop after the swivel has turned 47.5°, the crossbar against a toggle joint: probably the true end. (4) The 42125's steering stops after the knuckle has turned 8.2°, against a half-beam on a link between the wishbones: open, the booklet decides. (5) The doors of the 42123 and 42125 stop closed against body and frame: right. A correction of the report and of my own words to Erik: the 42123's function 2 is stored as partly (its pistons still, not driven), not as works; the one stored as works is function 4. The new fault, which I confirmed on 5174: the viewer says "Loader made 721 parts and shapes, model has 968 and 2" for the 42120 and shows nothing. src/main.ts matches the loader's children to the parts by place and by count, and the 252 segments of the flexible axles make no child. So every result of the 42120 is the check's only, against the rule that the check passes only what the viewer shows; nobody had opened it. Decided: the solver owner mends it now, before route 5, by matching by name as the check's partShapes does, and says since which commit it has been so. A new gate goes with it: a script that opens every one of the 190 models in the viewer and records an error or the count of parts, run through the pool by whoever changes the viewer's loading and again by me. The scan of decision 248 covers the check's reading only; the viewer reads by its own code, and I had no gate on it.
251. **Pushed pistons and guided racks: not built now; first their number.** A contact drive for pistons that a crank web pushes would be a new kind of joint (the piston's place a function of the crank's angle, held down by gravity). From the stored records only the 42123 has it among 71 piston-engine functions (the other 12 that are partly have ordinary rods), so it waits; the 42123's functions 2 and 4 are marked by the sweep (decision 252). A rack with no slide may be common and costs steering range where it is, so it gets a count first: the worker, after its sweep trial, lists every model whose steering rack has no slide joint and says what guides the rack in the 42123 and why no slide is read. Two look-ups in the booklets go to the worker while its browsers run: the 42125's front axle (does the half-beam p431 stand where the model has it; an 8° lock is too little for a car) and the 42120's fans (how far they swing).
252. **A collision stop on a control that turns without end is "not shown"; small travel is compared with the booklet. No rule in the check for it: the sweep judges, and holds mark.** From preview's records at 87452d1b: of 740 working functions 271 have a collision stop. A stop is right where the real part has an end (a door against its frame, steering at its lock), so the check cannot fail a function for a stop, and how far is far enough has no number that is not picked. But two groups are suspect, listed in works-with-stops-87452d1b.md in the notes folder. List A, 24 functions: their kinds turn without end (wheels that roll freely, tracks, engines, rotors, power take-offs, differentials), yet a collision stops a control, as the 42093's front wheels at 0.07 radian, the 8851's tracks at 0.1 and the 42123's rear wheel at 0.1. A wheel that should roll and is stopped after a few degrees is not shown, whatever its listed parts did before the stop. List B, 33 more: every control stops within 0.2 radian each way (the 42054's bonnet tips 0.03 radian, the 42031's boom turns 0.03); some are true (a suspension's short travel), some are not. Decided: the sweep of decision 236 judges these, control by control. For each stopped control the worker records whether the real part turns without end (with the words of the function's text that say so); if it does, the verdict is not shown. For a control with an end and a travel of 0.2 radian or less both ways, the worker compares with the booklet's picture of the function and gives not shown where the real part plainly goes much further. Each not shown is traced or goes into data/holds.json at partly with its reason, as decision 220 has it; that is how the count of working functions comes down to what the viewer shows. Lists A and B are swept first, before the rest of the 57 green sets. I expect the 740 to lose 20 to 40 by this, and say so to Erik now rather than after.
253. **The viewer opens every model: two had never opened. Merged with its gate script; and the pool gets a queue.** claude/viewer-open-all c7ba90ae. The 42120: the loader makes no object of its own for a line whose file is a primitive or a subpart and draws it into the model; the 252 segments of the 42120's flexible axles are such lines (an unofficial primitive the model carries), so matching by place and count failed. src/loadedLines.ts now matches each line of the packed model to the loader's object of the same file and colour; a line with no object gets an empty group with its reason, and a line with no object and no reason, or an object no line has, is an error. The 42106 Stunt Show Truck & Bike had never opened either: the dev server answered 404 for the & in its file's name; vite.config.ts's rule for # now covers &. The solver owner found no commit where the 42120 opened (the count test is older than the set's listing), and by the code main does not open it. scripts/drags/open-all.ts opens every model the sets use in a viewer and prints an error or the parts drawn; every change to the viewer's loading is gated with it from now.
