So stage B is two parts. **Decided:** (B1) the rows are passed to the null space as their places and values, not dense; it keeps the same bits, is proved as stage A was (kernel tests old against new bit for bit, the same bits end to end on the sample's drags, timed pairs of a full step on the three cases), and is built now, on stage A or not as the solver owner finds best. (B2) the LU is not built now. It gives another basis of the same space, so it moves bits and needs the sample and the sets; its one near miss on rank shows where it can go wrong; and its gain is on the largest models only, where a full step would still take over a second (the 8110's holds about 1.3 s for 2.0 s). Those models need to do fewer full steps, not a quicker one. The count is kept and B2 is looked at again when the full step is the last thing between a large model and a first move a user can bear. The solver owner's order after B1: the cause of a result depending on how often the holds are chosen (decision 286, cause before any mend), then the working set for pushes, since pushing a model along is what a user does most and a push still works every step in full, then the design note for the solver in a worker and for a drag's first move on the set, then the drift of free parts with decision 282's seven cases.
288. **The viewer stops 12 of the 14 functions the stop rule names; they are held, and 700 functions work. The guard against a hang is merged.** (a) The worker mended the sweep so that a control that is itself a part that turns without end is asked a full turn of 1.5 rad each way, whatever the check's record turned; before, the sweep turned a control only as far as the check had, so where the check stopped at a collision the sweep never asked for more and called the function shown. Run on the 14 functions of decision 284 (claude/sweep-steps 3dc0096a, data/viewer/sort-14-full): in 12 the viewer itself stops at the same pair of parts as the check, within 0.03 rad (42024 function 5, 42081 function 8, 42093 function 3, 42106 function 5, 42112 function 2, 42123 function 4, 8844 function 2, 8848 function 7, 8849 functions 4 and 7, 8851 function 6, 9393 function 3). They are held at partly on the viewer's evidence, as decision 279's were, and the check of their 11 sets wrote the records: 12 functions from works to partly, nothing else. This is not the stop rule's proof, which still waits for Erik's word in the worker's session (decision 283); when the rule is merged these holds and eight of decision 279's are no longer needed. The 8840's functions 2 and 5 are not held: the viewer stops that wheel at another pair and angle than the check (parts 201 and 200 at 0.70 and 1.05 rad, against 104 and 113 at 0.07 and 0.03), and the sweep recorded a turn of 0 and still said shown, which the worker traces; a turn of 0 must never count as shown. The 42078's function 12 was refused by the sweep's own fault (it looked for the slide joint as the body's parent link, and the lock bar's slide closes a loop); the worker mends that with the 8840. (b) Counts: 700 work, 254 partly, 91 do not; 52 sets have every checked function working. Of the morning's 740, 40 are now held: 14 collision stops of decision 279, 11 shown by neither, 3 of decision 286 and 12 of this one; two more were held and lifted the same day. With the 8274's hold of 19 November the file holds 41. (c) The guard of decision 280 is merged (claude/nonfinite-guard 3da39520 as ce7cdc46): the null space throws on a row that holds a value that is not finite, or comes to one, and a turn, a joint's motion, the choice of holds and a push step each catch it, undo the step and say so once a drag in the console. The solver owner's proof: the kernel tests bit for bit, the same bits end to end on the sample's 19 sets and 114 functions, and new tests that a NaN ends at once. My gate: the change read, the type check, all 1,076 tests, and the 9397's steering drag in the viewer three times before and three times after: 15 ms a step before, 14 to 15 after, the first two steps 138 and 205 ms before, 133 and 198 after. So keeping the step's state at each call costs nothing that can be measured. The test suite's peak was 8,222 MB against the 7,500 it claims from the pool; it claims 8,500 from now.
289. **Three more holds, two mends to the sweep, and a difference between the check's mechanism and the viewer's in 30 models.** The worker's traces (claude/sweep-steps 5a4079a1, records 208e09be). (a) The 8840's wheel p210 turned 0 in the sweep because the sweep pressed Shift on it wherever the check's record had freed pins, and in the viewer Shift on a part that no friction pin holds asks it to slide on its axle. Mend: Shift is pressed only where a plain turn of 0.01 does not move the control, as a user would. Function 5 was called shown with every wheel at 0 because it lists no part to move. Mend: a function is shown only if each of its controls turned or ran 0.01 or more in some way. With both, the viewer stops p210 by pointer at the check's own pairs and in one long turn at 0.70 and 1.05 rad at another pair; functions 2 and 5 are held, as the 12 of decision 288. (b) The 42078's function 12: with the sweep's slide step mended (the asked slide joint found by its bodies, the part pushed as the viewer's drag pushes), the viewer's collision stop ends the push of the lock bars at once at parts 2618 and 2641, so the doors do not open, where the check makes the same slide and passes. Held; the difference is traced after item 5. The count is 697 when the check has written the two sets' records. (c) The worker's item 5, kept touches per model, found two things. First, the scripts gave a path's parts (chain links, hose segments) their shapes when finding what a loose part touches, where the viewer gives them none, so the check's mechanism has differed from the viewer's in 30 of 190 models since decision 259; the scripts are changed to do as the viewer does. Second, the kept collision files are from 18 November, before decision 246 changed the near test (the 9397's has 3 near pairs fewer than the test finds today), and the files' key did not see that; the key now carries the test's version and the files are made afresh. So the check has been stopping at collisions by near pairs that are not today's, which may be why the check and the viewer stop at other pairs on the 8840 and the 42078. **Decided:** item 5 is split. Branch one, with no result to change: kept touches from the kept files, the key, the fresh files; proved by the digest with kept touches against without, the same to the last bit on 190 models, and the pivots' check; I merge it on that, and my own daily full check of preview is the net for collision stops that move, each looked at. Branch two: the path parts' change, which changes 30 mechanisms and needs the check by name, so it waits for Erik's word in the worker's session with the stop rule (decision 283); the worker sends the list of the 30 first.
290. **Stages A and B1 of the cheaper search are merged: the null space is worked on its rows' places, with the same bits. A full step is a fifth to a quarter quicker in the solver owner's pairs, but the 9397's drag in the viewer gains little.** claude/nullspace-rows acc5a3ee, built on stage A, merged as 4931db18. The solver owner first corrected its own report of decision 287: a profile by line shows that the dense copy and first pass of the rows are only about 14% of the null space (46 of 323 ms on the 42083); the rest was the elimination's bookkeeping, a dense search down each column (130 ms), the list of unused columns made afresh at every pivot (91 ms), a Map lookup for each row and a second pass over the pivot row. So B1 is more than the rows passed by their places: inBasis notes the places it writes beside each row and the null space copies from those alone; each row is one record with no Map or Set lookups; the first unused column is a pointer; taking the pivot row from a row adds its new places in the same pass; stage A's column index lies beneath. The pivots and every sum are the same, in the same order. Proof: 1,080 tests, among them random rows made by inBasis with sums that cancel, ties and repeated rows against the old elimination bit for bit, and in five model cases every null space of a step worked on its own rows against the old elimination there and then; the same bits end to end on the sample's drags, 19 sets and 113 functions. The solver owner's timed pairs of a full step, old and new in one process: the 9397's steering, null space 212 to 132 ms a drag, the two calls 195 and 192 to 149 and 168 ms; the 42083's steering, null space 552 to 270 ms, calls 493 and 507 to 388 and 406 ms; the 8110's function 8, null space 1,030 to 673 ms, holds 1,685 to 1,353 ms. My gate: the change read, the type check, all 1,080 tests, and the 9397's steering drag in the viewer three times before and after: the first two calls 139 and 207 ms before, 139 and 196 after, a later step 15 ms both. So in the viewer the first call did not gain and the second gained 5%, far less than the pairs in node; the solver owner is asked why, with a pair taken in the viewer on the 9397 and the 42083 that counts the choice of holds at pointer-down. One thing is ordered on top: the places beside a row are trusted, and a later change that writes into a row between inBasis and the null space would give a wrong answer without a sign; a setting that makes the null space compare each row's places with the whole row is added, and the tests' model cases and the bits harness run with it. What is left in the null space is the fill, which is B2 and waits (decision 287). The guard's copy of the step's state was timed by the solver owner as well: 0.5 microseconds.
291. **The two engine holds of decision 286 are wrong, by the sweep's measure; the 8844's wheel is a solver fault, and the fault of rolling a model is taken up now, before the working set for pushes.** The solver owner's cause-finding (probes only, nothing built). (a) The 42043's function 10 and the 42075's function 2 do not slip. The sweep's part A turns a control in one call and measures each part from its start pose to its end pose, as an angle between 0 and 180 degrees, so a part that turns nearly a whole turn reads as hardly moved: the 42075's crankshaft at rate 3.89 turns 5.83 rad for 1.5 rad in, and 2 pi less 5.83 rad is the 25.8 degrees the sweep reported; the 42043's gear at rate 4.2 ends 1 degree past a whole turn. Turned in one call or in 25, with the holds chosen once or afresh, the rates are the check's. So my narrowing of decision 267's rule in decision 286 rested on a wrong measure, and I withdraw it: the pointer's short drags were right. The sweep must measure a turn by pieces or by the joint's angle (the worker's), and every hold that rests on the sweep's turn moving a listed part too little is swept again with that: the two engines here, and of decision 279 the 42069's function 5 and the 8258's function 7 (both an engine run by pushing, the same kind), the 42055's function 7, the 42129's function 4, the 8275's function 5 and the 8835's function 2. Holds that fall are lifted in one check run after that sweep, not one by one. (b) The 8844's function 4 is a solver fault, and not the holds, the working set or the kept motion. To keep the frame where it is, the choice of motion lets the wheel turn on its pin while its axle turns back and runs the gear train behind it, so the tyre hardly rolls; 41 bodies end up to 19 LDU out of place after 0.25 rad and two parts meet at 0.2 rad. The turn that came out right, with the holds chosen afresh at each small call, was right by accident: after the first call rounding of 1e-8 in the tyres' turning about the upright is taken for a heading, five bodies of the train count as steering and are kept still, and the helicopter rolls. The check stops as the viewer does. (c) So a result does depend on how often a drag starts (decision 286 d), but through steeringBodies taking rounding for a heading, which is to be mended with the real fault and not alone, since alone it makes the accidental right case wrong too. (d) The real fault is that of decision 282's rolling cases: a model that stands on the ground and is rolled by a wheel should roll, and the solver instead keeps the frame still and runs or swings whatever is free. Twenty held functions are a wheel, track or shaft that a collision stops (42024 function 5, 42038 function 9, 42060 function 7, 42093 function 3, 42106 functions 2 and 5, 42112 function 2, 42113 function 6, 42123 function 4, 8840 functions 2 and 5, 8844 function 4, 8848 functions 2, 3 and 7, 8849 functions 4 and 7, 8851 function 6, 8856 function 8, 9393 function 3), and how many share this cause is not known. **Decided:** the solver owner takes this class now, ahead of the working set for pushes, because rolling a model is what a user does most and a quicker wrong roll helps nobody. First a count with no build: for each of the twenty, whether the stop comes from a motion that keeps the frame still while something free runs or swings, and what the real model does; then a rule proposed to me, with the steeringBodies mend inside it; nothing that moves a result is built before I answer. The working set for pushes follows, then the design note.


## First steps

1. The triage and the stop-reason probe (phase 1), on a branch of their own.
2. Slowest sets first, and results in a folder of their own.
3. The ratchet, and a full check quick enough to run on demand (done 2026-11-11).
4. The status page: its first version, the grid of sets, was published on 2026-11-10 from that day's results. Causes joined it on 2026-11-11.
